## 0. Conventions every module follows

- Public import paths are `tracer_evalschema.<module>`. Tests import them lazily (`importlib.import_module`) so a missing module fails one test with `ModuleNotFoundError`, never collection.
- "Type" below means a pydantic-compatible annotated type used with `TypeAdapter(Type)` or as a field annotation; it validates to the plain Python value named in the signature (`str`, `int`, `datetime`).
- **Typed errors are not wrapped by pydantic.** Exceptions in `tracer_evalschema.errors` that derive from `TracerSchemaError` (and not from `ValueError`) propagate out of `model_validate`, `model_validate_json`, `TypeAdapter.validate_*` and the loader as themselves. Ordinary constraint violations stay `pydantic.ValidationError`. Section 2 says which is which.
- Models: `extra="forbid"` everywhere. Value objects carry no `id`, `schema_version`, `created_at`, `metadata`.
- Data files are read through one place (`tracer_evalschema.data_loader`, free to design) but **observed-language validation must not open any file under the package `data/` directory** (test uses an audit hook in a fresh interpreter).
- Package data: the existing four JSON files, plus `data/bidi_markers.json` (section 8).
- Supported Python 3.11 to 3.14. Tests passed their import/collection on 3.11, 3.13 and 3.14 (venvs synced with `uv sync --locked --all-groups`).

## 1. Package root `tracer_evalschema`

- `__version__: str == "0.0.1"` (exists), `SCHEMA_VERSION: str == "0.0.1"` (new). Both equal `importlib.metadata.version("tracer-evalschema")` and the `version` in `packages/evalschema/pyproject.toml`.

## 2. `tracer_evalschema.errors`

All derive from `TracerSchemaError(Exception)` unless noted.

| Name | Base | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `TracerSchemaError` | `Exception` | |
| `SchemaVersionError` | `TracerSchemaError` | attributes `found`, `expected`; `__init__(self, found=..., expected=...)` (keyword use in tests); `str()` contains both |
| `SchemaVersionTooNew`, `SchemaVersionMismatch`, `SchemaVersionInvalid` | `SchemaVersionError` | siblings: neither is a subclass of another |
| `UnknownDigestScheme` | `TracerSchemaError` | raised by `ContentDigest` validation and by `verify_digest` |
| `DigestKeyUnavailable` | `TracerSchemaError` | keyed digest, no resolver / resolver returned `None` / resolver raised |
| `DigestMismatch` | `TracerSchemaError` | a digest was verified and does not match |
| `NonIJsonValueError` | `TracerSchemaError` | NaN, +-Infinity, integers beyond +-(2**53 - 1), lone surrogates (wrap the `rfc8785` errors) |
| `DuplicateKeyError`, `DepthLimitExceeded` | `TracerSchemaError` | `DepthLimitExceeded` is not a `RecursionError` |
| `BundleError` | `TracerSchemaError` | base of the four below |
| `MixedSchemaVersionError`, `ConflictingVersionError`, `ConflictingRecordError`, `RefDigestMismatchError` | `BundleError` | |
| `UnresolvedReferenceError`, `BundleSizeError` | `BundleError` | (rev 22) the seam's `require_resolved` and the loader's size limit (section 25.2.1) |
| `ConfusablePrefixError` | `TracerSchemaError` and `ValueError` (tests accept either) | |
| `DataFileError` | `ValueError` | a bad vendored or test-supplied data file |

## 3. `tracer_evalschema.ids` (P1-04a)

- `ID_PREFIXES: Mapping[str, str]`: prefix -> entity class name, exactly the 27 rows of ADR 0001 Decision 4. **Read-only** (`types.MappingProxyType` or equivalent: item assignment raises `TypeError`).
- `class TypedId`: not a `str`, not a `uuid.UUID`. `prefix: ClassVar[str]` (also readable on instances), `uuid: uuid.UUID` attribute, `new() -> Self` classmethod (fresh v7), `__str__` -> `"<prefix>_<uuid>"`, hashable, equal only to the same class with the same uuid (never equal to its wire string).
- One subclass per prefix named `<Entity>Id` (`RunId`, `EvaluatorId`, `PersonaId`, `ConfigSnapshotId`, ...), importable from `ids`; `id_type(prefix: str) -> type[TypedId]`.
- Pydantic behaviour of every ID class: accepts only the wire string `^<prefix>_[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-7[0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$` (exact; a trailing `\n` or space is rejected, so use `fullmatch`/non-multiline semantics) or an instance of the same class; rejects other kinds' IDs and instances, uppercase, v1/v4/v6/v8 UUIDs, wrong variant, bare UUID strings, and `uuid.UUID` objects. Serializes to the wire string in JSON mode. JSON Schema: `{"type": "string", "pattern": "<the regex above, with ^ and $>"}`; for `RunId` the pattern is exactly `^run_[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-7[0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$`.
- `class Uuid7Generator(clock_ms: Callable[[], int] | None = None)` with `next() -> uuid.UUID`. RFC 9562 section 5.7 layout (48-bit Unix ms, version 7, variant `10`), section 6.2 monotonic counter. Strictly increasing even with a frozen clock (10,000 values) and with a clock that goes backwards. It may bump the millisecond when the counter space runs out. `uuid7() -> uuid.UUID` uses a shared generator on the real clock.
- `validate_new_prefix(prefix: str, existing: Mapping[str, str] | None = None) -> None`: raises `ConfusablePrefixError` when the prefix is already registered, within Levenshtein distance 1 of an existing prefix, or an anagram of one; raises `ConfusablePrefixError` or `ValueError` for anything that is not lowercase letters only (`"Run"`, `"ru n"`, `"ru_n"`). Default `existing` is `ID_PREFIXES`.
- `class ExternalRef` (value object): `system`, `kind`, `value` (strings; the value is stored verbatim).
- `from_external(id_type: type[TypedId], *, system: str, kind: str, value: str) -> tuple[TypedId, ExternalRef]`: mints a fresh v7 ID of that kind on every call and returns the `ExternalRef` the caller attaches. (ADR 0001 writes `from_external(kind, system, value)`; see ambiguity 3.) (rev 7, item 3) This signature is adopted by ADR 0001 Decision 7. The function is pure: it neither looks up nor stores anything, and Phase 2 importers do the P-02 lookup before calling it.
- (rev 7, item 2) Opaque `*_id` fields: exactly `KeyRef.key_id`, `ToolCallEvent.external_call_id` and `Run.caller_external_id` (ADR 0001 I12). Every other field named `*_id`, `*_ids` or `ref_id` is a typed ID, including `SealedPayload.aad_fields.subject_id`.
- (rev 7, item 4) The `uuid.UUID` is exposed as the attribute `.uuid` (ADR 0001 Decision 1).
- (rev 7, item 1) `validate_new_prefix` accepts only `^[a-z]{2,6}$`. A prefix is confusable with an existing one when the Levenshtein distance is 1 or the two are anagrams (same letters in another order). An exact duplicate is rejected as already registered.

## 4. `tracer_evalschema.primitives` (P1-04b)

- `UtcDatetime`: accepts timezone-aware `datetime` and ISO 8601 strings with an offset or `Z`; rejects naive datetimes and strings without an offset (including date-only), and `:60` leap seconds, in Python and JSON mode. Validates to an aware `datetime` whose `utcoffset()` is zero. Serializes (JSON mode) to `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.ffffffZ`, always six digits. (rev 7, item 7) Also rejected, in both modes: strings with more than six fractional digits (never truncated or rounded), JSON numbers and Python `int`/`float` (no epoch values; pydantic's default accepts them, so this must be enforced), `bool`, and `datetime.date` objects that are not `datetime` instances.
- `Ms`: non-negative integer. Rejects floats (including `1.0`), negatives, `bool`, numeric strings, in Python and JSON mode. JSON Schema `{"type": "integer", "minimum": 0}`. Every model property ending in `_ms` must have this schema (directly or as `anyOf` with null).
- `DecimalString` (rev 7, item 5: **accepts canonical strings only, never canonicalizes**): validates `str` only (rejects Python floats and ints and JSON numbers). Accepts exactly the canonical form `-?(0|[1-9][0-9]*)(\.[0-9]*[1-9])?` in ASCII digits, scale <= 18, not `-0`. Non-canonical spellings of a valid number ("1.50", "2.0", "0.0", "10.500", "-1.250") are **rejected** with a message that names the input and the canonical spelling (for example `decimal "1.50" is not in canonical form; write "1.5"`). The value is returned unchanged. Pydantic and the JSON Schema `pattern` therefore accept exactly the same strings. Normalizing a source lexeme is the importer's job, done with an IngestWarning `decimal_normalized` that keeps the raw lexeme (ADR 0014 Decision 1a). The rest of this paragraph is qa's original text and still holds for the rejected forms: rejects exponent forms, `NaN`/`Infinity` in any case, `-0` (and negative zero from "-0.0"), a leading `+`, "1.", whitespace, non-ASCII digits, underscores, and scale > 18 (19 significant fraction digits). Negative values are valid. Never silently loses precision. JSON Schema: `{"type": "string", "pattern": <canonical form only>}` where the pattern accepts `0`, `-1`, `0.5`, `0.00000015`, `123456789.5` and rejects `-0`, `1.`, `1.50`, `2.0`, `+1`, `1e3`, `NaN`, empty and padded strings.
- `load_supported_currencies(path: Path | None = None) -> tuple[str, ...]`: reads `{"_meta": ..., "currencies": [...]}`; default path is the shipped `supported_currencies.json`. Raises `DataFileError` (or `ValueError`) for an empty list, duplicates, a non-string or non-`[A-Z]{3}` code, or a code absent from the shipped `iso4217_active.json`. Returns codes in file order.
- `SupportedCurrency`: type built from `load_supported_currencies()`; accepts exactly the listed codes; JSON Schema is `{"type": "string", "enum": [...]}` (an `enum`, not pydantic's single-value `const`; see ambiguity 9). (rev 7, item 9) How: `Annotated[str, <membership validator built from the codes>, WithJsonSchema({"type": "string", "enum": list(codes)})]`, with both parts built from the same `codes` tuple (verified with pydantic 2.14.1: `Literal["USD"]` emits `const`, while `WithJsonSchema` emits the same `enum` in validation and serialization mode). The same helper is used for every closed value set (ADR 0003 Decision 6).
- `make_money_model(codes: Sequence[str], name: str = "Money") -> type[BaseModel]`: builds a Money model whose `currency` accepts exactly `codes`, with the same behaviour as `Money`. This is the test seam for "a test-only file with an extra code changes both the validator and the enum".
- `Money`: value object `{amount: DecimalString, currency: SupportedCurrency}`; both required (no default currency), `extra="forbid"`. Equal by value. `Money + Money -> Money` with exact Decimal arithmetic (no float, and not limited by the default 28-digit context: the tests add a 36-digit and an 18-fraction-digit amount) returning the canonical amount (`"0.1" + "0.2" == "0.3"`, a zero sum is `"0"`); `Money + int` raises `TypeError`. `display(places: int = 2) -> str` returns the rounded text and never changes `amount`. (rev 7, item 8) Rounding is `ROUND_HALF_EVEN`, the same mode as stored rounding (P-35) and the weighted-score display (ADR 0013). The output always has exactly `places` fractional digits (`"0.00"`), and `places` must be an `int` >= 0.
- Rejection messages for a non-supported `currency`: for a code in the vendored ISO file, contains the code, the ISO name from the file, "not supported" and "USD" (ADR example: "currency INR (Indian Rupee) is not supported in 0.0.1; only USD"); for any other string contains the string, "not an ISO 4217 code" and "USD". Lowercase `usd`, `""`, `None`, ints, padded strings are rejected.

## 5. `tracer_evalschema.language` (P1-04c)

Types (validate to the canonical-case `str`; JSON mode too):

- `SupportedLanguage`: one of the nine languages of `languages.json`; optional script (only the language's native script) and optional region (only `IN`, `US`, `GB`, read from the vendored IANA subset); nothing else (no extlang, variants, extensions, private use, `und`/`mul`/`zxx`, grandfathered). Canonical case: `HI-in` -> `hi-IN`, `PA-guru-in` -> `pa-Guru-IN`. No other rewriting (`hi-Deva-IN` stays, no script or region is added). Rejection messages: unsupported region names the region, lists `IN`, `US`, `GB` and says "data change"; the `hi-en` pair (region `EN` on a non-English supported language) returns the unsupported-mixed-language message constant (exact identifier: see the import in `tests/test_language.py`, the test whose name ends `_with_codemix_message`) and takes precedence over the region message; a script outside the native set names the script (`Latn`).
- `ObservedLanguageTag`: syntax-only RFC 5646 `langtag` plus private-use-only `x-...` tags; primary subtag 2 or 3 letters, optionally followed by up to three 3-letter extlang subtags (so `hindi`, `hind`, `hindus` are rejected, `zh-yue` and `zh-cmn-Hans-CN` are accepted); all 26 grandfathered tags rejected, matched case-insensitively and exactly (`zh-min-nan` rejected, `zh-min-nan-TW` accepted). Same case canonicalization. `hi_IN`, whitespace, newline, empty, non-ASCII, `hi--IN`, `-hi`, `hi-IN-` rejected. Never reads a data file.
- `primary_language(tag: str) -> str`: lowercase first subtag.
- `LanguageLabelWarning` (value object): `code: str`, `raw: str`. Codes: `language_label_normalized`, `language_label_unparseable`.
- `normalize_language_label(raw: str) -> tuple[str | None, list[LanguageLabelWarning]]`: pure, total, fresh list each call. `_` -> `-`, then validate as an observed tag. Clean tag: `(tag, [])`. Underscores replaced: `(tag, [normalized])`. Not a tag: `(None, [unparseable])`. Every warning's `raw` is the input label. (rev 7, item 10) A case-only difference (`HI-in` -> `hi-IN`) gives no warning. The helper never trims white space, so `" hi-IN "` is unparseable here. (E4, decided by the user, section 17a: trimming surrounding white space is an importer step, done before the helper is called, with a `language_label_normalized` warning and the untrimmed label kept as raw; the types and this helper never trim.) `""` and whitespace-only labels are unparseable. A label that is not a tag after `_` replacement gets only the `unparseable` warning. `None` (no label) gives no warning. The importer turns each `LanguageLabelWarning` into an `IngestWarning` (P1-10c) with the same `code` and `raw`.
- `ObservedLanguage` (value object): `tag: str | None`, `raw: str | None`, `warnings: list[LanguageLabelWarning]`; `observe_language_label(raw: str | None) -> ObservedLanguage` keeps `raw` code point for code point (whitespace, case, underscores, non-ASCII, empty) and never raises. This is the shared logic behind `Transcript.detected_language(_raw)` and `Turn.language(_raw)`; those models arrive in P1-10b.
- The unsupported-mixed-language message constant (see above).

## 6. `tracer_evalschema.text` (P1-04d step 2)

- `VerbatimText`: `str`, strict (no coercion), stored code point for code point (no NFC/NFD, no strip), rejects lone surrogates in Python mode (JSON mode already rejects the escape).
- `HumanLabel`: `str`; rejects U+202A-202E, U+2066-2069, U+200E, U+200F, U+061C, U+200B, U+FEFF, U+2060, C0 (U+0000-001F, so tab and newline too), C1 (U+0080-009F), lone surrogates, and every other Default_Ignorable_Code_Point of Unicode 16 (the test lists U+00AD, U+034F, U+115F, U+1160, U+17B4, U+17B5, U+180E, U+2061-2064, U+206A-206F, U+3164, U+FFA0, U+1BCA0, U+1BCA3, U+1D173, U+1D17A, U+E0001, U+E0020). Accepts ZWJ and ZWNJ anywhere, ordinary letters, spaces, punctuation. Variation selectors are untested (ambiguity 15). (rev 7, item 15) Also rejected: every Default_Ignorable_Code_Point of Unicode 16.0.0 apart from ZWJ and ZWNJ, **including the variation selectors and tag characters** (pending user confirmation, section 16 E2); DEL U+007F (all of general category Cc); U+2028 and U+2029; the empty string; and labels with leading or trailing white space (`str.isspace()` at either end; never trimmed).
- `MachineKey`: `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:/@+-]{0,255}$`, strict, `$` must not accept a trailing newline; JSON Schema `pattern` equals this string exactly.
- `MetricKey`: lowercase snake case, starts with a letter (`wer`, `p95_latency`, `tool_call_success`; rejects upper case, `-`, `.`, `/`, space, non-ASCII, trailing newline).
- `contains_bidi_controls(text: str) -> bool`: true iff the text contains any of U+202A-202E, U+2066-2069 (and nothing else: not LRM/RLM/ALM).
- `BidiFlaggedText` (value object): `{text: VerbatimText, contains_bidi_controls: bool}`. The flag is computed when omitted and **verified** when supplied: a supplied flag that differs from the computed one is rejected in both directions. It is serialized. This is the reusable "computed and verified on load" pattern that `Turn` and `Segment` will use (ADR 0017 Decision 2).
- `MARKER_TABLE: Mapping[str, str]`: read-only; keys are exactly the nine one-character strings U+202A-202E and U+2066-2069; values are distinct, non-empty, printable ASCII, contain no space and no bidi control (the ADR example is `[RLO]`; the tests do not fix the spelling). (rev 7, item 14) The spelling is fixed as the Unicode abbreviation in brackets: U+202A `[LRE]`, U+202B `[RLE]`, U+202C `[PDF]`, U+202D `[LRO]`, U+202E `[RLO]`, U+2066 `[LRI]`, U+2067 `[RLI]`, U+2068 `[FSI]`, U+2069 `[PDI]`.
- `display_safe(text: str) -> str`: `U+2068 + text with each of the nine controls replaced by its marker + U+2069`. LRM, RLM, ALM, ZWJ, ZWNJ unchanged. Pure. `display_safe("") == U+2068 U+2069`.
- `marker_table_json() -> str`: JSON text `{"wrapper": {"open": "U+2068", "close": "U+2069"}, "markers": {"U+202A": "<marker>", ...}}` for the nine entries. The same document is shipped as `data/bidi_markers.json` (parsed JSON equal). (rev 7) The file is a generated artifact, not vendored data. It has no `_meta`, it is never edited by hand, and `test_marker_table_json_matches_function` is its drift check.

## 7. `tracer_evalschema.digest` (P1-05)

- `DIGEST_SCHEMES: Mapping[str, Any]`: read-only; keys exactly `"sha256-jcs/v1"` and `"hmac-sha256-jcs/v1"`.
- `KeyRef` (value object): `key_id` (required), optional `key_version`, `provider_hint`; `extra="forbid"` (a `key`, `secret`, `password`, ... field is rejected). Lives here for now; P1-26 may re-export it. (rev 19) P1-26 does not re-export it: `KeyRef` keeps its one home here. `SealedPayload` is the first model that embeds `KeyRef` outside `ContentDigest` (`dek_wrap.kek_ref`, section 24). No value-level credential rule on `key_id` in 0.0.1 (S-N83, known gap).
- (rev 19) `jcs_bytes(document) -> bytes`: RFC 8785 bytes of a JSON-mode value exactly as given (no digest-domain rule); `NonIJsonValueError` outside I-JSON. `canonical_bytes` calls it, and `sealed.aad_bytes` uses it, so `rfc8785` stays imported in this module only.
- `ContentDigest` (value object): fields exactly `scheme`, `value`, `key_ref` (no `salt_ref`). `value` is 64 lowercase hex characters. An unregistered scheme raises `errors.UnknownDigestScheme` (unwrapped), including case or whitespace variants. `sha256-jcs/v1` forbids `key_ref` (`ValidationError`); `hmac-sha256-jcs/v1` requires a `KeyRef` (`ValidationError`).
- `digest_domain(obj: BaseModel | Mapping) -> dict`: the dict that is hashed. A model is dumped with `model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True)`. Removes the **top-level** `content_digest` and `schema_version`, then removes every key whose value is `None` at any dict depth. Lists and empty lists/dicts are kept. (rev 7, item 18) This is the generic rule, and it is defined on JSON alone so that a verifier in another language needs no model. Nulls are removed at every object depth, including inside user `metadata`; `null` elements of lists are kept. Entity-specific exclusions are declared by the entity and listed in ADR 0006 Decision 5: the P-17 profile digest, and the `schema_version` of entities embedded in a ConfigSnapshot (P1-12). `pydantic`'s `exclude_none` is not a substitute, because it keeps nulls inside dict values (verified with pydantic 2.14.1).
- `canonical_bytes(obj) -> bytes`: RFC 8785 (JCS) of `digest_domain(obj)` (use `rfc8785`; keys sort by UTF-16 code unit). Raises `NonIJsonValueError` for non-I-JSON values.
- `compute_digest(obj, *, scheme="sha256-jcs/v1", key: bytes | None = None, key_ref: KeyRef | None = None) -> ContentDigest`: plain takes no key (raises `ValueError`, `TypeError` or `TracerSchemaError` if given one); keyed needs both `key` and `key_ref` (same exception families if missing). Keyed = `hmac.new(key, canonical_bytes, sha256).hexdigest()`.
- `verify_digest(obj, digest: ContentDigest, *, key_resolver: Callable[[KeyRef], bytes | None] | None = None) -> None`: returns `None` on a match, raises `DigestMismatch` on a mismatch, `UnknownDigestScheme` for an unregistered scheme (also for a model built with `model_construct`), `DigestKeyUnavailable` for a keyed digest when there is no resolver, the resolver returns `None`, or the resolver raises; the key question is decided before the content is hashed. `NonIJsonValueError` propagates. This is the only function in the package that hashes or compares digests: `hashlib`, `hmac`, `rfc8785` and `compare_digest` appear only in `digest.py` (a source scan enforces it).
- Golden values (hex) are in `tests/test_digest.py`; they were computed from hand-written canonical strings, not from the package.

## 8. `tracer_evalschema.base` (P1-05)

- `check_schema_version(value: object) -> None`: raises `SchemaVersionInvalid(found=value)` unless `value` is a `str` matching strict `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` (no leading zeros, no pre-release, no whitespace; `"1"`, `"v0.0.1"`, `"0.0"`, `"0.0.01"`, `1`, `None` are invalid); then compares as numbers (so `"0.0.10"` is newer than `"0.0.2"`): newer -> `SchemaVersionTooNew`, older -> `SchemaVersionMismatch`; `expected` is `SCHEMA_VERSION`. Also exposes `SCHEMA_VERSION`.
- `MetadataFlag` (value object): exactly `path: str`, `reason: str`.
- `EntityBase(BaseModel)`: class variable `id_prefix: ClassVar[str]` set by each subclass (a registered prefix). Fields: `id` (typed ID of that prefix, wire string in JSON), `schema_version: str` (required, no default; checked with `check_schema_version` before field validation, errors unwrapped), `created_at: UtcDatetime` (required), `metadata: dict[str, Any]` (optional, default `{}`), `metadata_flags: list[MetadataFlag]` (optional on input, always serialized). `extra="forbid"`. JSON Schema: `required` contains `id`, `schema_version`, `created_at`; the `id` property carries the subclass's prefix pattern (`^evl_...`).
  - `metadata_flags` are computed from `metadata` at validation (JSON-pointer-like paths such as `/metadata/note`, `/metadata/nested/token`; the test only requires the key name in the path). A value is flagged when it starts with a known credential prefix (the tests use `sk-...` and `AKIA...` shapes); a non-secret value is not flagged. The value itself is preserved byte for byte. If `metadata_flags` is supplied it must equal the computed list (missing, extra, or renamed entries are a `ValidationError`); if omitted it is computed. (rev 7, item 20) The exact rule is in ADR 0007 Decision 5a: RFC 6901 JSON Pointer paths from the document root; string leaves only; reasons `known_credential_prefix` and `high_entropy_token`; one flag per path; sorted by path in UTF-16 code-unit order; a supplied list that differs in content or order is rejected.
- `VersionedEntity(EntityBase)`: adds `version: int` (strict, `>= 1`, JSON Schema `minimum: 1`) and `content_digest: ContentDigest` (required). After validation it calls `digest.verify_digest(self, self.content_digest)`; a mismatch raises `errors.DigestMismatch` unwrapped. A keyed `content_digest` raises `DigestKeyUnavailable` (no resolver at this layer). The digest covers the JSON-mode dump, so a document must contain every field the model serializes (the tests build full documents including `metadata: {}` and `metadata_flags: []`).
- `VersionedRef(BaseModel)`: value object `{id, version: int >= 1, content_digest: ContentDigest | None}`; `id` is a typed ID of any registered prefix; `version` is required.
- Test doubles (`Widget` with prefix `evl`, `VersionedWidget` with prefix `per`) are defined in `tests/_support.py`, outside the package; the registry must ignore classes defined outside `tracer_evalschema`.

## 9. `tracer_evalschema.bundle` (P1-05)

`check_bundle_consistency(docs: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]], refs: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]] = ()) -> None` on JSON-mode documents (so a mixed-version bundle can be tested without constructing an invalid entity):

- distinct `schema_version` values -> `MixedSchemaVersionError`;
- two docs with one `(id, version)` and different `content_digest` -> `ConflictingVersionError` (identical duplicates and other versions of the same id are fine);
- two docs without `version`/`content_digest` (records of fact) with one `id` and different content -> `ConflictingRecordError` (identical duplicates fine);
- a ref `{id, version, content_digest?}`: version required (else `BundleError`, `ValidationError`, `ValueError` or `KeyError`); when the bundle contains that `(id, version)` and both digests use the same scheme and differ -> `RefDigestMismatchError`; no digest on the ref, no matching entity, or a different version present -> no error.
- (rev 22) The signature gains the keyword-only `require_resolved=False`; with `True`, a ref whose `(id, version)` no document holds raises `UnresolvedReferenceError`. The module also holds `RunBundle`, `ExportBundle` and the export rules: section 25.

## 10. `tracer_evalschema.loader` (P1-05)

- `loads_strict(data: str | bytes, *, max_depth: int = <your default, 10_000 must exceed it>) -> Any`: standard JSON value; duplicate object keys (also after unescaping, nested, inside lists) -> `DuplicateKeyError` naming the key; nesting deeper than `max_depth` (arrays and objects, also unterminated input) -> `DepthLimitExceeded`, never `RecursionError`; syntax errors stay plain `ValueError`s (not the two typed errors). `max_depth=5` accepts `[[[1]]]` and rejects six nested arrays; `max_depth=1` accepts `1`.
- `load_model(model: type[BaseModel], data: str | bytes, *, max_depth: int = ...) -> BaseModel`: strict parse then `model_validate` (so a version error surfaces as its typed error).
- (rev 22) `load_bundle(model, data, *, max_bytes=DEFAULT_MAX_BUNDLE_BYTES, max_depth=DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH)` for `RunBundle` and `ExportBundle`, with a size limit checked before parsing (`BundleSizeError`; default 256 MiB): section 25.2.5.

## 11. `tracer_evalschema.registry` (walks over all models; gate-bound)

- `model_classes() -> list[type[BaseModel]]`: every pydantic model defined inside the `tracer_evalschema` package (after importing every model module). (rev 7) That means module-level classes reached by walking each model module's attributes, not every subclass in memory. Models built at runtime by `make_money_model()` (default name `Money`) are excluded, so the name-keyed `value_object_classes()` cannot have two classes named `Money`.
- `entity_classes() -> dict[str, type[EntityBase]]`: prefix -> class, only real entities (each has a registered `id_prefix`).
- `value_object_classes() -> dict[str, type[BaseModel]]`: class name -> class for every non-entity model.
- Three tests are **red by design until every entity and value object exists** (the gate, not P1-05): `test_every_entity_has_registered_prefix` (all 27 prefixes have exactly one `EntityBase` subclass named as in ADR 0001), `test_value_objects_have_no_entity_fields` (the 42 value-object names listed in the test exist and carry no entity fields), `test_versioned_entity_set_matches_adr` (exactly nine versioned entities). The three walks `test_every_id_reference_field_has_registered_prefix`, `test_ms_field_names_are_integers_schema` and the `model_classes()` loops pass vacuously on an empty registry and become meaningful as models arrive.

## 12. Data files

- `supported_currencies.json` must gain `_meta.sources` (non-empty list), `_meta.retrieved_at` (`YYYY-MM-DD`) and `_meta.verification` (>= 20 characters) like the other three files (P1-03b, `test_vendored_data_has_verification_note`); the key `currencies` stays `["USD"]`.
- `bidi_markers.json`: section 6.
- `iso4217_active.json` `_meta.fields.is_x_code` still describes the rule "no X-codes" removed in revision 4; text only, not asserted.

## 13. Test support and fixtures (QA)

- `tests/_support.py` (lazy `api()`, `adapter()`, test doubles `widget_classes()`, document builders `entity_doc`, `versioned_doc`, `redigest`), `tests/bidi_conformance.py` (the conformance harness and `uba_visual`, the single seam for display tests).
- `tests/fixtures/unicode/`: `BidiTest.txt`, `BidiCharacterTest.txt` (Unicode 16.0.0, unmodified), `UNICODE-LICENSE-V3.txt`, `provenance.json`.
- (P1-04e) `tests/support/uax9/`: the clean-room, test-only UAX #9 implementation behind the `uba_visual` seam (not shipped; see `docs/phase1/uax9-provenance.md`).

## 14. Deferred out of batch 1

- `test_wer_not_applicable_for_observed_romanized`, `test_romanized_observed_scored_when_not_script_sensitive` (listed under P1-04c in SCOPE) need `MetricDefinition` (P1-08) and `MetricResult` (P1-11); they go with those batches.
- `test_turn_language_raw_verbatim`, `test_f3_*`, `test_adapter_malformed_language_label_recorded_as_warning` need `Turn`, `Transcript` and the adapters; the label logic they will call is `observe_language_label`.
- Everything in SCOPE section 5 for P1-06 and later, the harness tests (golden fixtures, frozen fixtures, schema export), T-DRIFT, and adversarial A-xx cases not named above.

## 15. Ambiguities and contradictions found (qa's list, kept as written; every item is resolved in section 15a)

1. **ADR 0001 Decision 4 and P-03 name three grandfathered one-edit pairs (run/rub, exc/exp, dts/dti); the frozen table has five** (also agt/art at one edit, tsc/cst as an anagram). `test_frozen_registry_confusable_pairs_are_the_known_five` pins the real set. The ADR text, not the table, should be corrected (the table is frozen).
2. **ADR 0001 I12 says every `*_id`/`*_ids` field is a typed ID**, but `KeyRef.key_id`, `ToolCallEvent.external_call_id` and `Run.caller_external_id` are opaque identifiers. The walk allowlists exactly those three; SealedPayload `aad_fields.subject_id` is not yet decided (typed, presumably).
3. **`from_external(kind, system, value)`** (ADR 0001): `kind` is the `ExternalRef.kind` (what the external value is, e.g. `call_id`), not the entity kind; the minted ID's kind needs its own argument and "records an ExternalRef on the owning entity" has no owner yet. Chosen: `from_external(id_type, *, system, kind, value) -> (id, ExternalRef)`. The Phase 2 unique-index tuple "(entity kind, system, kind, value)" is consistent with this.
4. The ADR says the typed ID "exposes the `uuid.UUID`" without a name: chosen attribute `.uuid`.
5. **`DecimalString` "1.50"**: the tests accept and canonicalize it (`test_decimal_canonical["1.50"->"1.5"]`), but its JSON Schema pattern can only describe the canonical form, so pydantic accepts a string the schema rejects. `test_invalid_rejected_by_both` and the generated schema either need a documented known gap or the type should reject non-canonical input (then `test_decimal_canonical` changes). Decision needed from staff-reviewer.
6. SCOPE P1-03b says "content exactly `["USD"]` with `_meta`", which cannot be one JSON array; the shipped file is an object with `currencies`. Chosen: the object form (ADR 0003 says "`currencies` key"). The shipped `_meta` lacks sources, retrieval date and verification note, so `test_vendored_data_has_verification_note` is red for the right reason.
7. ADR 0002: not stated whether input with more than six fractional digits is truncated, rounded or rejected, whether epoch numbers are rejected, or whether `UtcDatetime` accepts `date` objects. Untested.
8. ADR 0014 Decision 6: display rounding mode unspecified. Tests avoid ties.
9. pydantic emits `{"const": "USD"}` for a one-member `Literal`; ADR 0003/0014 and gate item 15 require an `enum`. Force `enum`.
10. ADR 0005: no statement whether a case-only change (`HI-in`) raises `language_label_normalized`, what a blank or whitespace-padded label does (`" hi-IN "` is not a tag; trimming is not allowed by the "verbatim raw" rule but nothing says the normalized field may not trim), or the type of the importer's warnings (`IngestWarning` is a P1-10c model). Chosen: no warning for case-only changes, no trimming, a small `LanguageLabelWarning`.
11. ADR 0005 Decision 5: the `hi-en` message must win over the region message (`EN` is not a vendored region); same for any non-English supported language with region `EN`. `en-EN` is unspecified.
12. ADR 0005 P-12 "grandfathered, read literally": chosen case-insensitive exact match. `zh-min-nan-TW` and `art-lojban-x-a` stay valid.
13. P-12 "optionally followed by extlang subtags": chosen up to three 3-letter extlangs.
14. ADR 0017: the exported marker table's location, shape and marker spelling are not specified (only "for example `[RLO]`"). Chosen as in section 6.
15. ADR 0017 P-50: variation selectors (U+FE00-FE0F, U+E0100-E01EF, Mongolian U+180B-180D, U+180F) are Default_Ignorable but "accepted if emoji labels are wanted"; tag characters (U+E0020-E007F) are also DI and used in emoji flags. Untested, except that U+E0001 and U+E0020 are rejected. DEL (U+007F), U+2028/2029, empty labels and edge whitespace are unspecified.
16. ADR 0017 I4 needs a model to test "forgery"; Turn does not exist yet. `BidiFlaggedText` is proposed as the reusable verified-flag value object.
17. **ADR 0017 conformance** (historical finding; python-bidi was removed 2026-10-22 on the owner's approval because the clean-room reference passes 100% of both files): `python-bidi` 0.6.12 implements **Unicode 16.0.0** (crate `unicode-bidi` 0.3.18, its `UNICODE_VERSION` is `(16, 0, 0)`), not the 18.0.0 used for the language data; the 16.0.0 conformance files are vendored (the 18.0.0 files have identical data lines). The wheel's `LICENSE-THIRD-PARTY.yml` lists crate 0.3.15 while the compiled module embeds 0.3.18. The pure-Python `bidi.algorithm` module is UAX #9 v5 without isolates and is not usable.
18. ADR 0006 Decision 5: digest domain removal of `schema_version`/`content_digest` is written for "the entity"; for a ConfigSnapshot that embeds versioned entities it is not said whether nested `schema_version`/`content_digest` are also removed (only the TestProfileRef digest is, P-17). Chosen: top level only, nulls at every depth, null list elements kept. Also whether `verify_digest` returns a bool or raises ("never reports success"): chosen raise-on-mismatch, `None` on success.
19. ADR 0008: how typed errors surface through pydantic is not said (chosen: unwrapped); whether `schema_version` is required on input (chosen: required); pre-release versions (chosen: invalid).
20. ADR 0007 I10 "forged or missing flag list is rejected" vs "computed at validation": chosen omitted -> computed, supplied-but-different -> rejected. The "high-entropy token" heuristic is unspecified, so the tests use only known credential prefixes; the path syntax is unspecified.
21. ADR 0009 and SCOPE P1-05 assign the four bundle tests to P1-05, but `RunBundle` and `ExportBundle` are P1-14. `bundle.check_bundle_consistency` on JSON documents is the proposed seam; P1-14 will call it. Refs with a keyed digest cannot be compared without a key and are skipped.
22. SCOPE lists the entity-wide walks (`test_every_entity_has_registered_prefix` and friends) under P1-04a although they cannot pass before P1-27; they are in the gate-bound group of section 11.
23. ADR 0003 mentions "revision 6" while the ADR index says revision 5 (cosmetic).
24. **CI** (historical; the bidi-ref group was removed 2026-10-22 on the owner's approval, the CI jobs keep `--all-groups`): `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `uv sync --locked` / `uv run --locked pytest` without the `bidi-ref` group, so the required test jobs lack the UBA reference ADR 0017 and SCOPE P1-02 say they sync (`test_ci_required_test_jobs_sync_bidi_ref_group` is red for that reason). The 3.15 job probably cannot sync it (no wheel, `no-build-package`); it is non-blocking.
25. **T-DRIFT (ADR 0005 Decision 10)**: the allowlist must also cover `packages/evalschema/tests/test_language.py` and `test_data_files.py` (negative parameters), `src/.../language.py` (message constant), and `docs/phase1/evidence/**` (pytest ids such as `[bn]` appear in the saved red logs) and this file `docs/phase1/api-surface.md` (it names the rejected tags in sections 5 and 15).

## 15a. Resolutions (revision 7; decider: schema-architect)

Every item below is **Resolved** and was decided by the architect. The "Tests to change" column lists qa-owned tests in `packages/evalschema/tests/` whose expectations differ from the resolution. "none" means the test as written already pins the chosen behaviour. Four items have a user-visible part, and that part is escalated in section 16. Until the user answers, the resolution below stands.

| # | Item | Status / decider | Choice | Reason | Tests to change |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Prefix near pairs | Resolved / architect | ADR 0001 Decision 4 and P-03 now name the real five frozen pairs: one edit apart `run`/`rub`, `exc`/`exp`, `dts`/`dti`, `agt`/`art`; anagrams `tsc`/`cst`. Rule for every prefix added after the freeze: 2-6 lowercase ASCII letters, not registered, Levenshtein distance >= 2 from every registered prefix, and not an anagram of one. | The table is frozen, so the text has to follow it. Typed IDs already reject a swapped prefix in pydantic and JSON Schema, so the five pairs cause log-readability problems only. | none |
| 2 | Opaque `*_id` fields | Resolved / architect | Opaque allowlist is exactly `key_id`, `external_call_id`, `caller_external_id` (ADR 0001 I12). Principle: a `*_id` field may be opaque only if its value is assigned by a system other than tracer (key manager, provider, transport) and is stored verbatim. Each such field is named in I12, and adding one is an ADR amendment. `SealedPayload.aad_fields.subject_id` is a typed ID (a union of the sealable kinds, fixed in P1-26). | Without a principle the allowlist grows by convenience. `subject_id` names one of our entities, so the I12 rule applies to it. | none |
| 3 | `from_external` signature | Resolved / architect | `from_external(id_type, *, system, kind, value) -> (TypedId, ExternalRef)`. It is pure; the caller attaches the ref; the P-02 lookup happens before the call (Phase 2). | ADR 0001's `kind` was overloaded: it named both the minted entity kind and `ExternalRef.kind`. | none |
| 4 | Name of the UUID attribute | Resolved / architect | `.uuid` | Matches the stdlib type name. Already used by the tests. | none |
| 5 | `DecimalString` non-canonical input | Resolved / architect (user-visible part escalated: E1) | Only canonical strings are accepted. Non-canonical spellings are rejected with a message that gives the canonical spelling. Importers normalize source lexemes with an IngestWarning `decimal_normalized` that keeps the raw (ADR 0014 Decision 1a). Arithmetic results (`Money + Money`) are produced in canonical form. | Pydantic and the JSON Schema pattern now accept the same set, so `test_invalid_rejected_by_both` holds with no `KNOWN_SCHEMA_GAPS` entry. A stored value is never a rewrite of the given text. | `test_primitives.py::test_decimal_canonical`: all non-canonical parameters (`1.50`, `2.0`, `0.0`, `10.500`, `-1.250`) must now be rejected, with the canonical spelling in the message; `3` stays accepted unchanged. `test_primitives.py::test_money_may_be_negative_but_stays_canonical`: `amount="2.50"` must be rejected. |
| 6 | P1-03b file shape | Resolved / architect | An object `{"_meta": {...}, "currencies": [...]}` (already in revision 6). dev has since filled `_meta.sources`, `retrieved_at` and `verification`. `test_vendored_data_has_verification_note` passes now (run by schema-architect on 2026-10-22). | One JSON array cannot carry `_meta`. | none |
| 7 | `UtcDatetime` input edge cases | Resolved / architect (importer side escalated: E3) | Rejected in both modes: more than 6 fractional digits (never truncated or rounded), JSON numbers and Python `int`/`float` (no epoch values), `bool`, and `date` objects that are not `datetime`. | Never lose precision silently (P-04). An epoch number has no visible unit (ADR 0002 rejected alternative). A date is not an instant, and choosing midnight would be an assumption. | none (untested today; new test proposed in SCOPE section 5, revision 7: `test_utc_rejects_excess_precision_numbers_and_dates`) |
| 8 | `Money.display` rounding mode | Resolved / architect | `ROUND_HALF_EVEN`, exactly `places` digits. | One rounding mode across stored rounding (P-35), the weighted-score display (ADR 0013) and money display. | none |
| 9 | `const` vs `enum` | Resolved / architect | Every closed value set, including single-member sets and discriminator tags, emits `{"type": "string", "enum": [...]}`. One helper does this: `Annotated[str, membership validator, WithJsonSchema(...)]`, built from one tuple. No `const` appears in any generated schema (ADR 0003 Decision 6). | Gate item 15 and ADR 0003 require `enum`. The validator and the schema come from one source. Behaviour checked on pydantic 2.14.1. | none (new contract test proposed: `test_no_const_in_generated_schemas`) |
| 10 | Language label warnings | Resolved / architect (trimming escalated: E4, decided by the user: importer-level trimming, section 17a) | No warning for a case-only change. The types and the pure helper never trim: given a padded label the helper reports it unparseable (the importer trims first, E4, section 17a). `""` and whitespace-only labels are unparseable. `None` gives no warning. The pure helper returns `LanguageLabelWarning{code, raw}`, which the importer converts 1:1 into an `IngestWarning`. | BCP-47 tags are case-insensitive, so a case change loses nothing. ADR 0005 Decision 3 lists the importer normalizations exhaustively. Keeping the helper free of the P1-10c model avoids a dependency cycle. | none |
| 11 | Region `EN` | Resolved / architect | The code-mix message applies iff the primary subtag is one of the eight configured non-English languages and the region subtag is `EN` (any case). It takes precedence over the region message. `en-EN` gets the ordinary region message. | The user decision names that one alias family. Reading `en-EN` or any other pair as code-mix would be extrapolation. | none (proposed extra parameter: `en-EN` in `test_supported_region_outside_vendored_set_rejected`) |
| 12 | Grandfathered match | Resolved / architect | Whole-tag, case-insensitive match against the 26 literal tags of the RFC 5646 production. Longer tags that only start with one (`zh-min-nan-TW`) are ordinary langtags. | RFC 5646 section 2.1.1: tags are case-insensitive. The production lists complete tags. | none |
| 13 | Extlang count | Resolved / architect | Up to three 3-letter extlangs after a 2-3 letter primary subtag. | This is the RFC 5646 ABNF itself: `extlang = 3ALPHA *2("-" 3ALPHA)`. | none |
| 14 | Marker table | Resolved / architect | `data/bidi_markers.json` with the section 6 shape, generated (not vendored, no `_meta`). Spelling `[LRE] [RLE] [PDF] [LRO] [RLO] [LRI] [RLI] [FSI] [PDI]` (ADR 0017 Decision 3a). | These are the Unicode abbreviations, so a reviewer can look them up. Fixed so that renderers in other languages match. | none |
| 15 | `HumanLabel` edge cases | Resolved / architect (variation selectors and tag characters escalated: E2) | Rejected: all Default_Ignorable_Code_Point of Unicode 16.0.0 except ZWJ/ZWNJ (so variation selectors U+FE00-FE0F, U+E0100-E01EF, U+180B-180D, U+180F and tag characters U+E0020-E007F); all of category Cc (C0, DEL, C1); U+2028, U+2029; the empty string; leading or trailing white space. | A label is a short single-line identifier. Invisible or edge characters make look-alike labels. Trimming would rewrite the value. "If emoji labels are wanted" was never stated by the user, so nothing is assumed. | none (new tests proposed: `test_human_label_rejects_variation_selectors_and_tag_characters`, `test_human_label_rejects_del_separators_empty_and_edge_whitespace`) |
| 16 | Verified flag pattern | Resolved / architect | `BidiFlaggedText{text, contains_bidi_controls}` is adopted as the reusable value object. Turn and Segment reuse its validator. Added to ADR 0001 Decision 8 and ADR 0017 Decision 2. | A single implementation of "computed when omitted, verified when supplied". | none |
| 17 | UBA reference conformance | Resolved / architect | python-bidi 0.6.12 fails 1,407 BidiTest lines. schema-architect reproduced the L1 defect by hand: `get_display('a\t', base_dir='R') == '\ta'`, but `get_display('a‪\t', base_dir='R')` is returned unchanged. Under ADR 0017 Decision 7 the fallback **P1-04e is triggered**. The clean-room implementation targets Unicode 16.0.0 (the vendored, provenance-checked conformance files). The `display_safe` tests remain provisional under python-bidi until P1-04e passes, and then python-bidi and its group leave the lock. The wheel's third-party license list (crate 0.3.15 vs embedded 0.3.18) goes to security-licensing. | The user's own fallback rule, which has no allowlist of failures. | none now. At P1-04e: `bidi_conformance.py::uba_visual` (the seam), `test_bidi_conformance.py` reference tests, and in `test_repo_contract.py` `test_ci_required_test_jobs_sync_bidi_ref_group`, `test_license_exceptions_exact` and the bidi-ref group assertions. |
| 18 | Digest domain depth | Resolved / architect | Generic rule, defined on JSON alone: remove the top-level `content_digest` and `schema_version`, then remove null-valued keys at every object depth (including `metadata`), and keep null list elements. Entity-specific exclusions are declared by the entity: the P-17 profile digest, and (new) the `schema_version` of each entity embedded in a ConfigSnapshot, at the paths the snapshot schema names (P1-12). Nested `content_digest` values are kept. `verify_digest` returns `None` or raises. | A TypeScript or Go verifier has only JSON, so the domain cannot depend on Python models. Removing `schema_version` keys by name at every depth would also remove user metadata keys. Embedded versions must leave the snapshot domain for the ADR 0006 stability argument to hold. Consequence (documented): inside `metadata`, `{"a": null}` and `{}` have the same digest. | none (new P1-12 test proposed: `test_snapshot_digest_ignores_embedded_schema_version`) |
| 19 | Typed errors through pydantic | Resolved / architect | **Not wrapped.** Pydantic v2 wraps only `ValueError`, `AssertionError` and `PydanticCustomError` raised in validators, so every `TracerSchemaError` subclass that is not a `ValueError` propagates as itself out of `model_validate`, `model_validate_json`, `TypeAdapter.validate_*` and the loader. `ConfusablePrefixError` and `DataFileError` are `ValueError`s and are never raised inside model validation. `schema_version` is required; pre-release versions are invalid. | Callers can catch the typed class. Consequence: a typed error aborts validation at the first occurrence and carries no pydantic `loc`, so typed errors name the offending value in their message. | none |
| 20 | `metadata_flags` | Resolved / architect | Omitted means computed. Supplied but different (content or order) means rejected. Heuristic and path syntax are in ADR 0007 Decision 5a: RFC 6901 pointers from the document root; string leaves only; `known_credential_prefix` (a fixed pattern list) before `high_entropy_token` (a run of >= 32 characters from `[A-Za-z0-9+/=_-]` with an upper-case letter, a lower-case letter, a digit and >= 16 distinct characters); one flag per path; sorted by UTF-16 code units. The heuristic is part of the schema version. | The flags are verified on load, so the rule must be exact and computable in any language with integer arithmetic only. Lowercase hex digests and our IDs are not flagged. | none (the test values give exactly the two expected flags; new golden test proposed: `test_metadata_flag_rule_golden`) |
| 21 | Bundle checks seam | Resolved / architect | `bundle.check_bundle_consistency(docs, refs)` on JSON documents, called by P1-14. A ref digest is compared when the schemes are equal and either both are plain or both are keyed with equal `key_ref`. Otherwise it is not compared here; P1-14 verifies it through `verify_digest` with a resolver, and an unverifiable ref is never reported as matching. | Bundle models arrive in P1-14, but the rules are P1-05's. | none |
| 22 | Gate-bound walks under P1-04a | Resolved / architect | Kept as qa grouped them. SCOPE section 5 marks `test_every_entity_has_registered_prefix`, `test_value_objects_have_no_entity_fields` and `test_versioned_entity_set_matches_adr` as gate-bound (green at P1-27 or the gate, not at P1-04a/P1-05). | They need all 27 entities. | `test_ids.py::test_value_objects_have_no_entity_fields` must change before the gate for another reason: section 18, N1 |
| 23 | Revision label in ADR 0003 | Resolved / architect | Checked: in the current files ADR 0003 and the index both say revision 6, so no inconsistency remained. Both now say revision 7. | Cosmetic. | none |
| 24 | CI does not sync bidi-ref | Resolved / architect | Fixed by dev since qa's run: the required test jobs run `uv sync --locked --all-groups` and `uv run --locked --all-groups pytest`. `test_ci_required_test_jobs_sync_bidi_ref_group` passes now (run by schema-architect on 2026-10-22). The 3.15 job stays non-blocking. After P1-04e the group goes away (item 17). | ADR 0017 Decision 7. | none now (see item 17 for P1-04e) |
| 25 | T-DRIFT allowlist | Resolved / architect | ADR 0005 Decision 10 adds by path: `packages/evalschema/tests/test_language.py`, `packages/evalschema/tests/test_data_files.py`, `packages/evalschema/src/tracer_evalschema/language.py` (the `CODEMIX_UNSUPPORTED_MESSAGE` constant), `docs/phase1/api-surface.md`, and the directory `docs/phase1/evidence/` (saved, never hand-edited run logs and their summaries). Principle: a file qualifies only if it must spell a matched token to reject it, to test the rejection, to state the rule, or to record a test run verbatim. Discussing the policy in prose does not qualify (reword instead). Every entry is a repository path, and a directory qualifies only if it holds generated evidence. | Without these entries T-DRIFT fails on files that must contain the tokens. A principle stops the list from growing by convenience. | none in batch 1 (the T-DRIFT tests are not yet written; `test_tdrift_allowlist_matches_adr` will compare against the amended list) |

## 16. Escalated to the user (user-visible policy; recommendation given; the architect's resolution applies until the user answers)

- **E1. Decimal amounts must be written in canonical form (item 5).** Example: a pricing catalog line `price: "0.150"` is rejected with "decimal "0.150" is not in canonical form; write "0.15"". Revision 6 text said it would be silently stored as `"0.15"`. Values arriving from a provider through an importer are still accepted: the importer rewrites them and records a warning holding the original. Recommendation: accept, because stored text is then exactly what was written, and the validator and the JSON Schema agree.
- **E2. Labels (names, accents, display names) cannot contain emoji variation selectors or flag tag characters (item 15).** Example: the persona name "Asha" followed by a space, the star U+2B50 and the invisible U+FE0F is rejected, while the same name without U+FE0F is accepted. Labels with a leading or trailing space, empty labels, DEL and line separators are also rejected. Recommendation: accept. P-50 left this open "if emoji labels are wanted", and no such need has been stated.
- **E3. Timestamps finer than a microsecond from providers (item 7, importer side; new).** Example: an OpenTelemetry span carries `startTimeUnixNano = 1761127200123456789`. `UtcDatetime` holds microseconds, so the type cannot store the last three digits. Today's rule (ADR 0002 Decision 6) makes the importer leave the field empty with a warning, which would drop every span time in the F1 mapping. Recommendation: allow importers to truncate toward the earlier instant to microseconds (and to whole milliseconds for `*_ms` offsets and durations), with an IngestWarning `time_precision_reduced` that keeps the raw value. Decide before P1-29.
- **E4. Language labels with surrounding spaces (item 10). Decided by the user (see section 17a): trim at importer level with a warning, raw kept; the types and the pure helpers never trim.** Example: a provider sends `" hi-IN "`. Today the normalized language is empty (raw kept, warning `language_label_unparseable`). For currency labels the user approved trimming (P-52). Recommendation: apply the same trimming to language labels (warning `language_label_normalized`, raw kept). If accepted, `test_language.py::test_detected_language_raw_verbatim` is unaffected, but `observe_language_label(" hi-IN ").tag` becomes `"hi-IN"`, and qa adds that case. Decide before P1-31.

## 17. P-58, decided by the user (revision 7)

The per-turn share is stored in a separate field, not in `MetricResult.value`. This concerns the data record (the pydantic/JSON model that the Phase 2 tables will mirror). Applied in ADR 0010 Decision 3a, ADR 0004 Decision 4, ADR 0013 Decision 8a and SCOPE section 5. Details fixed by the architect while applying it, for user confirmation:
- `MetricResult.per_turn: PerTurnSummary?` with fields `share: DecimalString?`, `counts: PerTurnCounts`, `counted_turns: int`, `total_turns: int` (>= 1). The summary is present for a scored per-turn-share result, and also for the P-56 "no counted turn" outcomes (`error`/`judge_undetermined`, `not_applicable`). It is absent when no per-turn outcome exists (for example, missing inputs).
- `share` is a canonical `DecimalString`: the exact quotient `passed / (passed + failed)`, rounded half-even at scale 18 when it does not terminate (the weighted-score rule). It is present iff `counted_turns > 0`. On load it is recomputed from `counts` and compared, and a mismatch is rejected; `counted_turns` and `total_turns` are checked the same way.
- `value` is `None` for every per-turn-share result. `passed` is the definition's pass rule (a `range`, bounds within [0,1]) applied to the share in `Decimal` arithmetic. A float bound is converted through its shortest round-trip decimal form. Without a pass rule, `passed` is `None` (P-08).
- `per_turn_share{min_share}` is replaced by `per_turn_share{turn_rule}`: the threshold on the share is the ordinary `pass_rule` (`range{min}`), and `turn_rule` (a pass rule fitting `value_type`, required) decides whether one turn passes. Otherwise two fields would hold the same threshold.
- For the verdict the metric is one check (outcome from `passed`). In the weighted score it contributes `w * passed` (1 or 0), never `w * share`.
- No existing qa test in `packages/evalschema/tests/` changes: no batch 1 test touches MetricResult. The conditional test `test_per_turn_share_value_is_share_of_counted_turns` is withdrawn and replaced (SCOPE section 5).

## 17a. E4, decided by the user

Provider language labels with surrounding white space are trimmed **by the importer** (P1-31 and later importers), like the currency labels of P-52: the importer removes white space at either end (`str.isspace()`), calls `normalize_language_label()` with the trimmed label, keeps the label as received (untrimmed) as the raw value, and records `language_label_normalized` with that raw value (one warning per label, also when `_` was replaced too). The pure types (`SupportedLanguage`, `ObservedLanguageTag`) and the pure helpers (`normalize_language_label`, `observe_language_label`) never trim: `observe_language_label(" hi-IN ").tag` stays `None` with `language_label_unparseable`, so the E4 sentence "observe_language_label(" hi-IN ").tag becomes "hi-IN"" in section 16 does not apply. ADR 0005 Decision 3 and SCOPE (P1-31) state the same. No batch 1 test changes; the trimming tests belong to the importer task (P1-31).

## 18. New findings (revision 7)

- **N1 (contradiction).** ADR 0001 I14 and `test_ids.py::test_value_objects_have_no_entity_fields` forbid an `id` field on any value object, but the user-accepted specs give `TestProfileRef{id, version, content_digest?}`, `PricingCatalogRef{id, version, digest}` and `VersionedRef{id, ...}` an `id`: the referenced entity's ID, not their own. `SealedPayload.aad_fields` has `schema_version`: the plaintext's version (ADR 0015 Decision 4). Resolution (architect): I14 is restated as "no identity fields of their own". The exact exceptions are those three reference `id` fields (typed IDs of the referenced kind) and `aad_fields.schema_version`. The test must change before the gate to allow exactly these four fields.
- **N2 (applied).** P-58 versus `per_turn_share{min_share}`: two places for one threshold. Resolved as described in section 17, pending user confirmation.
- **N3 (open detail, P1-26).** For non-versioned sealed subjects (Artifact, Transcript, ToolCallEvent), `aad_fields.subject_version` is undefined. The architect decides at P1-26 (recommendation: present iff the subject kind is versioned).
- **N4 (data text, dev).** The `_meta.sources` note in `supported_currencies.json` cites ADR 0014 "Decision 5" for the closed, data-driven list; the decision is Decision 2. The `iso4217_active.json` `_meta.fields.is_x_code` text is still stale (section 12). Both are data files, so schema-architect did not edit them.
- **N5 (stale evidence).** The red evidence in `docs/phase1/evidence/red/` lists `test_ci_required_test_jobs_sync_bidi_ref_group` and `test_vendored_data_has_verification_note` as red; both pass now. The logs record the red run and are not edited.
- **Unverified:** whether the Default_Ignorable set differs between Unicode 16.0.0 and 18.0.0; qa's statement that the 18.0.0 bidi conformance data lines equal 16.0.0; whether pydantic's discriminated-union JSON Schema stays valid when tag fields use `WithJsonSchema` (checked at P1-08); the vendor key formats behind the credential-prefix list in ADR 0007 Decision 5a (they define our heuristic, whatever their accuracy).

## 19. Batch 2 (tasks P1-06..P1-09, plus Rubric): API assumed by the tests

Owner: qa. Audience: schema-architect (implements exactly this), staff-reviewer. Tests: `test_project_agent.py`, `test_persona_profile.py`, `test_metric_definition.py`, `test_rubric.py`, `test_evaluator.py`, `test_batch2_entities.py`; helpers `_support_b2.py`. Status: written and red; nothing in `src/` was written by qa. A throwaway oracle implementation (outside the repo) passes all of them except the SCHEMA_POLICY.md test (P1-20a) and every one of 73 injected mutants is caught. If a name or field below is wrong for a good reason, escalate to staff-reviewer; do not change a test.

General rules the tests rely on (all as in sections 0-14): `extra="forbid"` everywhere; closed sets emit `{"type": "string", "enum": [...]}`, never `const` (also union tags); value objects carry no entity fields; typed errors propagate unwrapped.

**Security rule added by qa (rev 8: Accepted, ADR 0007 Decision 8, with the extension in 19.4 row S1).** The text of a `ValidationError` raised for a credential (a pasted key in `config_ref`, an extra `api_key`, a URL with userinfo or a credential parameter) must not contain the credential: set `hide_input_in_errors=True` on `ProviderConfigRef`, `Project`, `AgentTarget` and the endpoint variants, and never put the offending value in a custom error message (the `FullMatch`/`ClosedSet` default messages echo the value, so those two helpers must not be used for these fields). Test: `test_rejected_credentials_are_not_echoed_in_error_text`.

### 19.1 Modules, classes and pinned fields

Documents are JSON-mode dicts. `E` = `EntityBase` fields (`id`, `schema_version`, `created_at`, `metadata`, `metadata_flags`); `V` = `E` plus `version`, `content_digest` (`VersionedEntity`). Every field not listed as optional is required. Fields marked (P) are pinned by qa because SCOPE/ADRs do not name them; see 19.3.

| Module | Class | Kind | Fields |
|---|---|---|---|
| `provider` | `ProviderConfigRef` | value object | `provider: MachineKey`, `model: MachineKey`, `config_ref` (`<scheme>:<name>`, see below) |
| `project` | `Project` (`prj`, not versioned) | `E` | `name: HumanLabel`, `provider_configs: list[ProviderConfigRef]` (default `[]`) |
| `agent` | `AgentTarget` (`agt`) | `V` | `name: HumanLabel` (P), `endpoint: Endpoint`, `languages: list[SupportedLanguage]` (default `[]`), `declared_capabilities: list[InputKind]` (default `[]`, P), `declared_stack: list[DeclaredStackEntry]` (default `[]`) |
| `agent` | `WebSocketEndpoint`, `WebRtcEndpoint`, `SipEndpoint` | value objects | `transport` tag `ws` / `webrtc` / `sip`; `url` (ws, webrtc) or `uri` (sip) |
| `agent` | `DeclaredStackEntry` | value object | `stage` (closed set containing at least `stt`, `llm`, `tts`), `provider: MachineKey`, `model: MachineKey` |
| `persona` | `Persona` (`per`) | `V` | `language: SupportedLanguage`, `accent: HumanLabel | None` (optional); every other field the architect adds (`speaking_pace`, ...) must be optional |
| `profile` | `TestProfile` (`tpf`) | `V` | data fields `name`, `dob`, `account_number` (P), each required text and each `x-pii: true` in both schema modes; further data fields must be optional and `x-pii` too |
| `profile` | `TestProfileRef` | value object | `id: TestProfileId`, `version >= 1`, `content_digest: ContentDigest | None` (plain or keyed) |
| `profile` | `profile_placeholder_fields() -> frozenset[str]` | function | exactly the TestProfile data fields |
| `profile` | `find_literal_profile_values(model, profile) -> list[LiteralProfileValue]` | function (Proposed seam under P1-12's `lint_profile_values_in_free_text`) | walks the JSON-mode dump of any model; a string leaf containing a non-empty profile data value yields `LiteralProfileValue{path, field}` (path = RFC 6901 pointer from the document root, `field` = profile field name); sorted by `(path, field)`; the value is never in the hit, its repr or its JSON |
| `metrics` | `InputKind` | closed set | the eleven members of ADR 0003 |
| `metrics` | `RangeRule{kind, min?, max?, inclusive}`, `EqualsTrueRule{kind}`, `OneOfRule{kind, options}`; `PassRule` | value objects, union by `kind` | `min`/`max` strict finite numbers (no bool, no text), at least one, `min <= max`; `inclusive` strict bool; `options` non-empty distinct strings; JSON Schema of `range` requires `min` or `max` |
| `metrics` | `rule_passes(rule, value) -> bool` | function | exact `Decimal` comparison, a float bound read as `Decimal(repr(x))`; no coercion: `equals_true` on a non-bool, `one_of` on a non-str, `range` on a bool or non-number returns `False` or raises `TypeError`/`ValueError` |
| `metrics` | `PerTurnCounts{passed, failed, not_applicable, undetermined}` | value object | strict ints >= 0, all required |
| `metrics` | `per_turn_share(counts) -> str | None`, `per_turn_status(counts) -> "scored" | "not_applicable" | "error"` | functions (Proposed) | share = `passed/(passed+failed)` as a canonical DecimalString, half-even at scale 18, `None` when nothing was counted; status per ADR 0010 Decision 3 / P-56; all-zero counts raise `ValueError` |
| `metrics` | `MetricDefinition` (`met`) | `V` | `key: MetricKey` (see 19.3), `kind`, `value_type`, `scale` (rating: `{min, max}` ints with min < max; enum: `{options}` non-empty distinct; boolean and numeric: none), `unit` (closed non-currency set containing `ms`, `ratio`, `count`, `percent`, `wer`; numeric only), `requires: list[InputKind]` (non-empty, unique; schema `minItems`, `uniqueItems`), `languages: list[SupportedLanguage]`, `script_sensitive: bool` (required, no default), `aggregation` (`{kind: per_conversation}` or `{kind: per_turn_share, turn_rule: PassRule}`), `pass_rule: PassRule | None`, `implementation_ref: MachineKey | None` (P), `prompt_template`, `prompt_variables: list[str]` (P), `judge_model_ref: ProviderConfigRef | None` (P) |
| `metrics` | `MetricBinding` (`mbd`) | `V` | `evaluator_id: EvaluatorId`, `metric: {id: MetricDefinitionId, version}` (a `VersionedRef`), `params: dict[str, JSON]`, `enabled: bool` |
| `metrics` | `check_bindings_unique(bindings)` | function (Proposed) | `ValueError` naming the metric ID when two bindings (listed twice, other version, disabled or not) of one evaluator name one definition |
| `rubric` | `Rubric` (`rub`) | `V` | `items: list[{binding: {id: MetricBindingId, version}, required: bool, weight: DecimalString | None}]` (P) |
| `rubric` | `validate_rubric(rubric, *, bindings, definitions) -> None` | function (Proposed) | resolves item -> binding -> definition; `ValueError` naming the binding (or definition) ID |
| `evaluator` | `Evaluator` (`evl`) | `V` | `name: HumanLabel` (P), `language: SupportedLanguage`, `instructions` (placeholder-checked text), `expected_outcome: VerbatimText` (required, non-empty), `persona: {id: PersonaId, version}`, `test_profile: TestProfileRef | None`, `metric_bindings: list[{id: MetricBindingId, version}]` (unique ids), `scripted_turns: list[ScriptedTurn]` (default `[]`), `expected_tool_calls: list[ExpectedToolCall]` (default `[]`), `expected_tool_calls_exhaustive: bool | None` (strict, default `None`; batch 17, F5-2), `max_duration_ms` (required) |
| `evaluator` | `ScriptedTurn` | value object | `index >= 0` (contiguous 0, 1, 2, ... in list order), `text` (placeholder-checked), `language: SupportedLanguage` |
| `evaluator` | `ExpectedToolCall` | value object | `name: MachineKey`, `match` (closed enum, at least two lowercase snake_case members; the tests read the members from the schema), `order_index >= 0` |

**`config_ref`:** a string matching `^<scheme>:<name>$` with `scheme = [a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*` and `name` the `MachineKey` charset, total length <= 256, no `=`, and no match of the known-credential patterns (`credentials.credential_reason(...) == "known_credential_prefix"`); the JSON Schema carries the shape as a `pattern`.

**URL rules (P-20):** ws takes `ws`/`wss`, webrtc `http`/`https`, sip `sip`/`sips` and a host; userinfo is rejected for ws and webrtc, only a password in the userinfo for sip (`sip:1001@pbx` is an address); query parameter names are compared after percent-decoding and case-folded (`token`, `key`, `api_key`, `x-amz-signature`, `x-amz-credential`, `sig`, `signature`), whole names only; an `@` in the path is not userinfo.

**`max_duration_ms`:** a strict integer, `> 0` and `<=` a maximum the schema states as `maximum` (the tests only require it to exceed 60 000). Because `test_ms_field_names_are_integers_schema` (batch 1) demands `minimum == 0` for every `*_ms` property, emit both `minimum: 0` and `exclusiveMinimum: 0`, or relax that walk through staff-reviewer.

(rev 8) Resolution 6 keeps both keywords: the emitted schema is exactly `{"type": "integer", "minimum": 0, "exclusiveMinimum": 0, "maximum": 14400000}` (1 ms to 4 hours), and the batch 1 walk is unchanged.

**Module and class locations are free as long as the names above are importable from the module shown** (`registry.model_classes()` walks the package).

**(rev 8) Changes to the table above** (section 19.4.1 has the full field list; where the two differ, 19.4.1 governs):
- `{id: <Kind>Id, version}` references are the typed value objects `PersonaRef`, `MetricDefinitionRef` and `MetricBindingRef` (subclasses of `VersionedRef` that narrow `id`), each `{id, version >= 1, content_digest?}`.
- `MetricDefinition.judge_model_ref` is a `ModelRef{provider: MachineKey, model: MachineKey}`. It is no longer a `ProviderConfigRef`, so it carries no `config_ref`. Required iff `kind=llm_judge`, forbidden otherwise.
- `MetricDefinition` gains `parameters: list[MetricParameter]` (default `[]`), which declares what `MetricBinding.params` may set.
- `MetricDefinition.unit` is exactly `ms`, `count`, `ratio`, `percent`, `wer`, `cer`.
- `DeclaredStackEntry.stage` is exactly `stt`, `llm`, `tts`, `realtime`.
- `ExpectedToolCall` gains `arguments: dict[str, JSON] | None`, and `match` is exactly `name_only`, `arguments_exact`, `arguments_subset`. `order_index` is a rank.
- `RubricItem{binding: MetricBindingRef, required: bool, weight: DecimalString | None}`, with the weight strictly positive.

eight entities) `test_batch2_entity_is_registered_with_its_prefix`, `..._roundtrip_and_schema_agree`, `..._rejects_unknown_fields`, `..._checks_schema_version_and_id_kind`, `test_batch2_versioned_entity_digest_is_verified`, `test_project_is_not_versioned`, `test_batch2_models_emit_enum_not_const`, `test_batch2_models_are_all_found_by_the_registry_walk`.

Not written in this batch (their prerequisites are later tasks): `test_artifact_uri_rejects_presigned_signature` (Artifact, P1-10a), `test_policy_documents_free_text_lint_limitation` and `test_snapshot_free_text_profile_value_lint_warns` (P1-12), `test_wer_not_applicable_for_observed_romanized` and `test_romanized_observed_scored_when_not_script_sensitive` (need MetricResult, P1-11), all MetricResult/verdict tests, and any `MetricRef` test (see 19.3 item 1).

`test_all_closed_enums_exact` holds the literal ADR 0003 Decision 1 table: it requires `MetricDefinition` and `AgentTarget` to exist and checks every other row as soon as its owner class exists, so it becomes complete without edits. `test_policy_documents_low_entropy_digest_limitation` stays red until P1-20a writes `SCHEMA_POLICY.md` (looked for at `packages/evalschema/SCHEMA_POLICY.md`, `SCHEMA_POLICY.md`, `docs/SCHEMA_POLICY.md`; the path is itself unspecified).

### 19.3 Ambiguities and contradictions (listed, not guessed; the pinned reading is in 19.1; qa's text kept as written, every item resolved in 19.4)

1. **`MetricRef 'key@semver'` (brief) vs ADR 0009.** The plan says `metric_key@version`, the brief says `key@semver`, ADR 0009 Decision 5 versions every definition with an integer and rejects semver strings for entity versions; ADR 0013 leaves `VerdictCheck.metric_ref` undefined. No `MetricRef` test was written. Decision needed: the type of `metric_ref` (`{id, version}` reference, or text `key@N`).
2. **Rubric weights: ">= 0" (brief) vs "positive" (ADR 0013 Decision 7).** Zero is not tested either way (`Σw` could be 0). Negative weights are rejected by the tests.
3. **`MetricDefinition.key`:** ADR 0010 Decision 1 says `MachineKey`; ADR 0017 and SCOPE define `MetricKey` for our own keys. Pinned `MetricKey` (lowercase snake case).
4. **Fields not in any ADR but required by the brief:** `implementation_ref`, `prompt_variables` (the `{{name}}` placeholders of `prompt_template`), `enabled` and `params` on MetricBinding, `AgentTarget.declared_capabilities`. ADR 0010 Decision 1 lists `prompt_template?` and `judge_model_ref?` as optional; the tests make them required for `llm_judge`. The plan's `capabilities{tool_calls, spans, agent_transcript}` struct was replaced by a closed `InputKind` list so that declared and detected (on Run) use one vocabulary. `judge_model_ref` is pinned as a `ProviderConfigRef` (it then carries a `config_ref`); a `{provider, model}` pair would be an alternative. What `params` overrides is undefined (MetricDefinition has no parameter list).
5. **Shapes the ADRs name but do not fix:** `scale` (flat `{min,max}` / `{options}`), `aggregation` and pass rules as objects with a `kind` tag, endpoint variant class names, the `unit` members beyond the five examples, `ExpectedToolCall.match` members, `DeclaredStackEntry.stage` members, `RubricItem` and `Evaluator` field names, `Evaluator.max_duration` -> `max_duration_ms` (ADR 0002 convention) with unspecified bounds, `ScriptedTurn.index` (ADR and test name say "index", the brief says `seq`), `ExpectedToolCall.order_index` semantics (unique? contiguous?), `TestProfile` data fields (only `name` and `dob` appear in the ADR).
6. **`max_duration_ms` positivity vs batch 1:** a positive minimum makes the schema `minimum` 1 (or only `exclusiveMinimum`), which `test_ms_field_names_are_integers_schema` rejects (`minimum == 0`). Resolve by emitting `minimum: 0` with `exclusiveMinimum: 0`, or by relaxing the batch 1 walk (staff-reviewer).
7. **Expected outcome as a required check:** ADR 0013 Decision 2 and SCOPE say the expected outcome "is an ordinary required check (equals_true)", the plan has `Evaluator.expected_outcome` text. Nothing ties the text to a particular binding/definition. Tested: the text is required; the check is expressible (`test_required_boolean_check_with_equals_true_ok`).
8. **Disabled bindings:** `enabled=false` is not defined for Rubric/verdict purposes (can a required rubric item point at a disabled binding?). Not tested; uniqueness counts disabled bindings.
9. **SIP userinfo:** ADR 0007 Decision 6 says every endpoint URL with userinfo is rejected; `sip:user@host` is the address form, so only a password is rejected for sip. Credential parameter names: case-insensitive and percent-decoded is pinned (the ADR lists spellings only).
10. **Placeholder grammar:** `{{profile.<field>}}`; whitespace inside the braces, `{{profile.}}`, case differences and nested paths (`profile.name.first`) are not specified and not tested. The lint's matching rule (substring, exact, case-sensitive) and its treatment of short values (a 1-2 character value matches almost anything) are unspecified; tests use values of 10+ characters. Normalization (NFC/NFD) is not applied (untested).
11. **`per_turn_share` pass rule kinds:** ADR says "a range within [0,1]"; the generic rule "kind must fit value_type" does not apply to it (a boolean per-turn metric has a `range` pass rule). Tested that way.
12. **`languages[]` empty:** meaning ("all languages"?) is unspecified; not tested. `languages` entries are `SupportedLanguage` tags, compared by primary language (ADR 0004 Decision 6).
13. **Declared-stack `stage` set:** P-05 defines the set for `UsageRecord`/`StageSpan`; whether `judge`, `sim_caller_*` belong in an agent's declared stack is undefined (only `stt`, `llm`, `tts` are asserted).
14. **T-DRIFT:** the SCOPE-mandated test name `test_persona_has_no_codemix_field` contains the banned token, so `tests/test_persona_profile.py` must be added to the ADR 0005 Decision 10 allowlist (the same applies to the later `test_dataset_item_no_codemix`). All other excluded tags and the mixed alias used by the tests are assembled from parts in `_support_b2.py`, so no other new file needs an entry.
15. **`DecimalString` weights:** `"5.0"` is rejected as non-canonical (E1); a rubric author must write `"5"`.

### 19.4 Resolutions (revision 8; decider: schema-architect)

Every item below is **Resolved**, and the architect decided it. "Tests to change" lists the qa-owned tests in `packages/evalschema/tests/` whose expectations differ from the resolution. "none" means the test as written already pins the chosen behaviour. Test names marked "proposed" are new tests for qa to write. Items with a user-visible part are escalated in 19.5, and each resolution stands until the user answers. The field-level detail of items 4 and 5 is in 19.4.1.

| # | Item | Status / decider | Choice | Reason | Tests to change |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `MetricRef` and metric versions | Resolved / architect | A MetricDefinition's version is its `VersionedEntity.version`, an integer >= 1. No versioned entity has a semver version. A metric reference is the value object `MetricDefinitionRef{id: MetricDefinitionId, version: int >= 1, content_digest: ContentDigest?}`. It is written as a JSON object and parsed as one, and a string is rejected. `MetricBinding.metric` and `VerdictCheck.metric_ref` both have this type. At P1-22 a check's `metric_ref` must equal the `metric` of the binding named by its `binding_id`. The text `key@version` (for example `p95_latency@3`) is a display form only: `render_metric_ref(definition) -> str` produces it, and it is never stored and never parsed. Keys do not identify definitions, because two definitions may share a key. The definitions bound to one evaluator must have distinct keys, which is checked wherever bindings meet their definitions (`validate_rubric`, and the P1-12 snapshot). | User decision Q4 (ADR 0009 Decision 1 and its rejected alternative "semver strings for entity versions") fixes integer versions, so "key@semver" in the brief would contradict a user decision. A string has to be parsed, cannot carry the per-kind `met_` pattern, and its key part is not unique, so it cannot identify a definition. A typed object gets the same JSON Schema pattern as every other reference. | none (no MetricRef test exists). Proposed: `test_verdict_check_metric_ref_is_typed_and_equals_binding_metric` (P1-22), `test_metric_ref_text_form_is_display_only` (P1-08), `test_bound_definition_keys_unique_per_evaluator` (P1-08/P1-12) |
| 2 | Rubric weights: zero | Resolved / architect | A weight is a canonical `DecimalString` **strictly greater than 0**. Zero and negative weights are rejected by the model, with a `ValidationError` located at `weight`. An item without a weight is not a weighted check. It is in neither sum of `weighted_score = Σ(w·passed)/Σ(w)`, in neither sum of `weight_coverage`, and never in `weighted_excluded[]`. Weights are relative, so there is no upper bound and no required total. | ADR 0013 Decision 7 says positive, and it is the user-accepted text (P-33). A zero weight would mean "contributes nothing", which an omitted weight already says, so zero would be a second spelling of one meaning. A rubric whose weights are all 0 would give Σw = 0, leaving both the score and its coverage undefined. | none (zero is untested). Proposed: add `"0"` to the rejected values of `test_rubric_weight_values` |
| 3 | `MetricDefinition.key` type | Resolved / architect | `MetricKey` (lowercase snake case starting with a letter). ADR 0010 Decision 1 now says `MetricKey`. | ADR 0017 Decision 5 and SCOPE reserve `MetricKey` for our own keys, and `MachineKey` for names of outside things. | none |
| 4 | Fields no ADR named | Resolved / architect | Full list in 19.4.1. `implementation_ref` (scorer name `<namespace>:<name>`) is required iff `kind` is `deterministic`, `audio` or `latency`, and forbidden for `llm_judge`. `prompt_template`, `prompt_variables` and `judge_model_ref` are required iff `kind=llm_judge`, and forbidden otherwise. **`judge_model_ref` is a `ModelRef{provider, model}`**, not a `ProviderConfigRef`. The key is reached at run time through the Project's `provider_configs`, which are unique per `(provider, model)`. **`MetricBinding.params`** sets only parameters that the definition declares in the new `MetricDefinition.parameters: list[MetricParameter]`. It never changes a definition field (pass rule, requires, scale, unit, aggregation, languages, `script_sensitive`, kind, prompt). `enabled` is a required boolean, and its effect is given in item 8. `AgentTarget.declared_capabilities: list[InputKind]` is unique and declaration only: no NA logic reads it (ADR 0004 Decision 2). | A judge without its prompt and model cannot be reproduced. A scorer name on a judge would name two computations for one value. A `config_ref` (`env:...`) is one deployment's variable name: inside a portable, digested definition it would tie every snapshot to that deployment, and renaming a variable would force a new definition version that changes no result. If a binding could override the pass rule, the threshold would live in two places, which is the reason P-58 replaced `min_share`. An undeclared parameter name would be silently ignored by a scorer, so names are declared and checked. | `_support_b2.py::judge_definition` (`judge_model_ref` without `config_ref`). `test_metric_definition.py::test_open_value_fields_accept_unseen_provider` (pass `{provider, model}`, and build it on a judge definition, because `judge_model_ref` on the latency definition is now rejected).
