| 5 | Shapes the ADRs named but did not fix | Resolved / architect | 19.4.1 fixes all of them. Scale is flat (`RatingScale{min, max}`, `EnumScale{options}`). Aggregation and pass rules are `kind`-tagged objects. Endpoint classes are `WebSocketEndpoint`, `WebRtcEndpoint` and `SipEndpoint`. Units are exactly `ms`, `count`, `ratio`, `percent`, `wer`, `cer`. `ExpectedToolCall.match` is exactly `name_only`, `arguments_exact`, `arguments_subset`, with a new `arguments` field. `order_index` is a rank. `DeclaredStackEntry.stage` is item 13. `RubricItem{binding, required, weight}` and the Evaluator fields are as pinned, plus the rules in 19.4.1. **`ScriptedTurn.index` (not `seq`)**: it is a list position, contiguous from 0, like `Exchange.index` and `ChannelMap.channels[].index`. `Turn.seq` is a sequence number that only has to be unique. A scripted turn is not a Turn (agent turns interleave), so a shared name would suggest that the two align. The TestProfile data fields are `name`, `dob` and `account_number`. | Each choice and its reason is in 19.4.1. `match` cannot mean anything without the expected arguments. Tool-call correctness covers "name, args, order" (plan), so without `arguments` it would be a blocking gap under gate item 13. | `test_evaluator.py::test_expected_tool_call_match_enum`: the members `arguments_exact` and `arguments_subset` require `arguments`, so the per-member loop and the evaluator embedding that uses `members[-1]` must supply them (`members[0]` is `name_only`). Proposed: `test_expected_tool_call_arguments_rules`, `test_expected_tool_call_order_ranks_contiguous`, `test_scale_and_unit_domains`, `test_range_bounds_within_value_domain` |
| 6 | `max_duration_ms` versus the batch 1 `*_ms` walk | Resolved / architect | Emit **both** keywords. The exact property schema is `{"type": "integer", "minimum": 0, "exclusiveMinimum": 0, "maximum": 14400000}`. The pydantic type is a strict int with 1 <= value <= 14,400,000 (4 hours). New principle (ADR 0002 Decision 2): every `*_ms` property carries the `Ms` base schema `{type: integer, minimum: 0}` unchanged and may only add tightening keywords (`exclusiveMinimum`, `maximum`). The batch 1 walk `test_ms_field_names_are_integers_schema` and ADR 0002 I10 stay as they are. | The walk checks that every `*_ms` field is an `Ms`. Relaxing it would weaken a qa-owned test, which the owner's rule forbids, for a gain that is only cosmetic (one keyword fewer). Checked with jsonschema 4.26.0 (Draft 2020-12): 0 and 14,400,001 are rejected, 1 and 14,400,000 accepted. Maximum: an evaluator drives a simulated call on the user's keys. 4 h is the longest call the suite already models (A-05), and a larger value is more likely a unit slip than an intent. | none |
| 7 | Expected outcome tied to the ordinary required check | Resolved / architect | The text reaches its check through the **reserved prompt variable `expected_outcome`**: the runner fills `{{expected_outcome}}` with `Evaluator.expected_outcome` verbatim (profile placeholders resolved, E7). An **expected-outcome check** is an enabled binding of the evaluator whose definition is `llm_judge`, `value_type=boolean`, `aggregation=per_conversation`, `pass_rule=equals_true`, and lists `expected_outcome` in `prompt_variables`. Rule (P1-12, ConfigSnapshot): the evaluator has at least one expected-outcome check, and every one of them is a `required` item of the snapshot's rubric. A definition may use the variable in another shape (for example a rating of how well the outcome was met). It is then an ordinary metric, not the expected-outcome check. | ADR 0013 Decision 2 says the expected outcome is an ordinary required `equals_true` check. The prompt variable is the data path that makes a judge evaluate the text, so the link is stated once, in the versioned definition. An extra `Evaluator.expected_outcome_binding` field would be a second statement that could disagree. `expected_outcome` stays required and non-empty because every evaluator has such a check. | none (the batch 2 judge definition already meets the conditions). Proposed: `test_snapshot_expected_outcome_is_a_required_equals_true_check` (P1-12) |
| 8 | `enabled=false` | Resolved / architect | **Rubric validation rejects an item whose binding has `enabled=false`**, whether the item is required or not and weighted or not, and the error names the binding ID. A disabled binding may stay in `Evaluator.metric_bindings`. The runner computes nothing for it and writes no MetricResult, so it never appears in `RunVerdict.checks` or `weighted_excluded`. Uniqueness counts disabled bindings (unchanged, `check_bindings_unique`). There is no `disabled_by_binding` reason. | `not_applicable` describes the inputs of one run (ADR 0004). Switching a metric off is a choice made before any run, not a run outcome. As an NA reason it would add a member to the user-decided `na_reason` set. It would also make every run of that evaluator inconclusive, and with a weight would keep `weight_coverage` below 1 forever (the P-55 argument), for a reason fixed when the rubric was written. Rejecting it surfaces the contradiction at authoring time. | none (untested). Proposed: `test_rubric_rejects_disabled_binding` (P1-22) |
| 9 | SIP userinfo; credential parameter matching | Resolved / architect | As qa pinned, stated exactly. **ws / webrtc**: an absolute RFC 3986 URI, ASCII only (no white space or control characters). The scheme is lowercase: `ws`/`wss` for ws, `http`/`https` for webrtc. A host is required. Any userinfo (an `@` inside the authority, which ends at the first `/`, `?` or `#`) is rejected. An `@` in the path is not userinfo. A fragment is rejected (RFC 6455 section 3 forbids it for ws, and a signalling endpoint never uses one). **sip**: `sip:`/`sips:` (lowercase) with a host. The user part (`sip:1001@pbx`) is the address and is allowed. A password (a `:` in the userinfo before `@`, also when percent-encoded as `%3A`) is rejected. **Parameter names** are checked in the query (ws, webrtc), and in SIP `;uri-parameters` and `?headers`. The text is split at `&` and `;`. The name is the part before the first `=`. It is percent-decoded as UTF-8 (a malformed escape or invalid UTF-8 rejects the URL), case-folded (`str.casefold()`), and compared as a whole name with the P-20 set `token`, `key`, `api_key`, `x-amz-signature`, `x-amz-credential`, `sig`, `signature`. | ADR 0007 Decision 6 wrote "userinfo (user:pass@)" for every URL, but a SIP user part is an address, not a secret. Case and percent-encoding must not let a name through, and decoding before folding is the order a server applies. Whole names keep `tokenizer` and `monkey` usable. A wider parameter list is P-60 (19.5). | none |
| 10 | Placeholder grammar and lint matching | Resolved / architect | **Grammar** (ADR 0006 Decision 9). A placeholder is exactly `{{profile.` + FIELD + `}}`, where FIELD matches `[a-z][a-z0-9_]*`, with no white space inside, matched left to right without overlap. FIELD must be in `profile_placeholder_fields()`, otherwise the text is rejected and the message names FIELD. **Malformed, rejected** (the message shows the well-formed spelling): any `{{`, optional white space, `profile` in any letter case, optional white space, `.` that does not start a well-formed placeholder. This covers `{{ profile.name }}`, `{{Profile.name}}`, `{{profile.}}`, `{{profile.Name}}`, `{{profile.name}` and nested paths such as `{{profile.name.first}}` (no TestProfile field is structured). **Ordinary text**: everything else, including `{{customer}}`, `{{ profile }}`, `{{}}`, `{profile.name}`, `profile.name`, and look-alike or full-width braces (the grammar is ASCII, and no normalization is applied). **Lint matching** (`find_literal_profile_values`, and the P1-12 lint built on it). Every string leaf of the JSON-mode dump is compared with every non-empty profile data value, after folding both: NFC, then `str.casefold()`, then NFC, then every decimal digit (general category Nd) mapped to its ASCII digit, then every run of white space collapsed to one U+0020. Folding is used only for the comparison and stored text is never changed. A hit needs token bounds: where the value's first (last) code point is a letter, mark or digit (categories L, M, N), the code point before (after) it in the text must be absent or not in L, M or N. There is no length threshold. One hit is reported per (path, field), sorted by path and then field in UTF-16 code-unit order, and the value never appears. | Text that looks like a profile placeholder but is malformed would reach the simulated caller as literal braces: rejecting it follows "reject rather than silently drop". Folding catches a value retyped in another case, in native digits (for example Devanagari digits for an account number) or with other spacing. Token bounds stop a short value from matching inside words, IDs and digests without skipping any field. The remaining limitations (another format of the same date, part of a name) are documented in `SCHEMA_POLICY.md`. | none (qa's values give the same hits under these rules). Proposed: `test_malformed_profile_placeholder_rejected`, `test_profile_lint_folding_and_token_bounds` |
| 11 | `per_turn_share` pass rule | Resolved / architect | For `aggregation.kind = per_turn_share`: (a) `turn_rule` is required and must fit `value_type` by the ADR 0010 Decision 2 table (boolean -> `equals_true`, numeric and rating -> `range`, enum -> `one_of`), plus the domain rule of 19.4.1. (b) `pass_rule` is `None` or a `RangeRule`, and `equals_true` and `one_of` are rejected **whatever `value_type` is**. (c) Every present bound b satisfies 0 <= b <= 1, compared in `Decimal` (`Decimal(repr(b))` for a float). (d) The range must admit at least one value in [0,1], so `{min: 1, inclusive: false}`, `{max: 0, inclusive: false}` and `{min: x, max: x, inclusive: false}` are rejected. (e) The value-type fit rule applies to `turn_rule`, never to this `pass_rule`. For every other aggregation, the fit rule applies to `pass_rule` as before. | The share is a number in [0,1] whatever the per-turn type, so the only rule with a direction on it is a range. The per-turn type is judged by `turn_rule`. A range that nothing can pass would fail every run for a configuration error. | none |
| 12 | Empty `languages[]` | Resolved / architect | **`MetricDefinition.languages`**: non-empty (`minItems: 1`), with no two entries sharing a primary language. Empty is rejected. **`AgentTarget.languages`**: may be empty. Empty means "no language declared", never "all languages". Exact duplicates (after case canonicalization) are rejected. No logic reads it. | ADR 0004 Decision 6 compares by primary language. Read literally, an empty metric list would make every result `unsupported_language`, and reading it as "all languages" would be an unstated meaning: both are guesses, so it is rejected. `hi` next to `hi-IN` would suggest a region distinction that the matching never makes. Whether a metric that does not depend on language (latency) should skip the check is a separate question, P-59 (19.5). | none. Proposed: `test_metric_languages_nonempty_distinct_primary`, `test_agent_target_languages_empty_means_undeclared_and_unique` |
| 13 | `DeclaredStackEntry.stage` | Resolved / architect | Exactly `stt`, `llm`, `tts`, `realtime`, a subset of the usage-stage set (P-05). | Joins with `UsageRecord.stage` and `StageSpan.stage` need the same members. `judge`, `sim_caller_llm` and `sim_caller_tts` are evaluation-side stages and never the agent's. `other` needs a raw label (`stage_raw`), which the entry does not have (qa's test forbids it), and a user-declared stack has no unmapped source label to keep. | none. Proposed: a `DeclaredStackEntry.stage` row in `test_all_closed_enums_exact` |
| 14 | T-DRIFT allowlist | Resolved / architect | ADR 0005 Decision 10 adds `packages/evalschema/tests/test_persona_profile.py`. The principle gains one case: a file also qualifies if it defines a test whose binding name (SCOPE section 5) contains a matched token. The P1-13 module that will hold `test_dataset_item_no_codemix` is added by path when it is written (one entry per file, never a glob). `_support_b2.py` needs no entry, because it assembles the tokens from parts. | The binding test name `test_persona_has_no_codemix_field` cannot be reworded, so its module must be listed. Listing modules by path keeps the list from growing by convenience. | none (the T-DRIFT tests are not written yet; `test_tdrift_allowlist_matches_adr` will compare with the amended list) |
| 15 | Canonical weights (`"5.0"`) | Resolved / architect | Confirmed. Weights are `DecimalString`, so `"5.0"` is rejected with the canonical spelling `"5"` in the message (E1). | One canonical spelling for stored decimals, the same as everywhere else. | none |
| S1 | qa's no-echo rule for credentials | Resolved / architect (Accepted into ADR 0007 Decision 8) | (1) Every model whose validation can reach a credential-checked field sets `hide_input_in_errors=True`. The setting of the outermost model being validated governs: verified on pydantic 2.14.1, an embedding model without it shows the rejected value even when the embedded model hides it. The set is therefore closed under embedding: today `ProviderConfigRef`, `Project`, `AgentTarget`, `WebSocketEndpoint`, `WebRtcEndpoint`, `SipEndpoint`; later `Artifact` and every bundle or export model that embeds one of them. (2) The same applies to models that hold personal data: `TestProfile` now, and the other PII-bearing models at their tasks. (3) Custom messages for these fields name the rule (and a parameter name where useful), never the value. The value-echoing `FullMatch`/`ClosedSet` messages are not used for them. (4) `ValidationError.errors()` always contains the input (verified), so code that logs validation errors calls `errors(include_input=False)`. (5) A walk test enforces (1). | The check that refuses a key must not write that key to a log. PII has the same exposure. A per-class list without the embedding rule would leak through `Project`. | none (`test_rejected_credentials_are_not_echoed_in_error_text` pins it). Proposed: `test_hide_input_set_closed_under_embedding`, `test_test_profile_errors_do_not_echo_values` |

#### 19.4.1 Fields and shapes fixed in revision 8

"Required" means no default. Every model has `extra="forbid"`. Every closed set emits `enum`. Rules that need several fields are checked by a model validator and, where JSON Schema can express them, emitted as `allOf` of `if`/`then` (see N12 in 19.6 for the rules that remain code-only).

- **References.** `VersionedRef{id: TypedId of any kind, version: int >= 1, content_digest: ContentDigest?}` stays the generic form (the bundle seam). The typed subclasses narrow `id`: `PersonaRef` (`PersonaId`), `MetricDefinitionRef` (`MetricDefinitionId`) and `MetricBindingRef` (`MetricBindingId`). `TestProfileRef` and `PricingCatalogRef` are unchanged. The digest on a reference is optional and is verified against the resolved entity when present (ADR 0009 I7). This resolution was escalated as E5 and was **decided by the owner in revision 9 (accepted)**.
- **`ProviderConfigRef{provider: MachineKey, model: MachineKey, config_ref}`** as pinned. `Project.provider_configs` is unique per `(provider, model)`, because the run resolves a `ModelRef` to the one entry with the same pair.
- **`ModelRef{provider: MachineKey, model: MachineKey}`** (value object): names a model without a key. Used by `MetricDefinition.judge_model_ref`, and recommended for `JudgeInfo.model_ref` (P1-11).
- **Endpoint** is the union type alias `Endpoint = WebSocketEndpoint | WebRtcEndpoint | SipEndpoint`, discriminated by `transport` (`ws`, `webrtc`, `sip`). Fields: `{transport, url}`, `{transport, url}`, `{transport, uri}`. URL rules are in item 9.
- **`AgentTarget`** (`agt`, versioned): `name: HumanLabel`, `endpoint: Endpoint`, `languages: list[SupportedLanguage]` (default `[]`, item 12), `declared_capabilities: list[InputKind]` (default `[]`, unique), `declared_stack: list[DeclaredStackEntry]` (default `[]`, in order). The plan's `description`, `declared_tier` and `adapter` are not fields in 0.0.1: an adapter is a provider-specific concern, and a declared tier would be read as availability, which ADR 0004 Decision 2 forbids.
- **`DeclaredStackEntry{stage, provider: MachineKey, model: MachineKey}`**, `stage` in `stt|llm|tts|realtime` (item 13).
- **`Persona`** (`per`, versioned): `language: SupportedLanguage` (required), `accent: HumanLabel?`. Nothing else in 0.0.1 (N11).
- **`TestProfile`** (`tpf`, versioned): `name`, `dob`, `account_number`, each a required non-empty `VerbatimText` with `x-pii: true`. Further data fields are optional and `x-pii`. `hide_input_in_errors` (S1).
- **`MetricParameter{name: MetricKey, value_type: boolean|integer|number|string|json, default?}`** (value object). `default`, when present, must match `value_type`: `integer` is a JSON integer, `number` is a finite JSON number, `boolean` and `integer` never accept each other, and `json` is any I-JSON value.
- **`MetricDefinition`** (`met`, versioned), with each field's condition:
  - `key: MetricKey`.
  - `kind`: `deterministic|llm_judge|audio|latency`.
  - `value_type`: `boolean|rating|enum|numeric`.
  - `scale`: `RatingScale{min: int, max: int}` (strict ints, min < max) iff rating; `EnumScale{options: list[MetricKey]}` (non-empty, distinct) iff enum; absent otherwise.
  - `unit`: required iff numeric, absent otherwise. Members and value domains: `ms` (>= 0, milliseconds), `count` (integers >= 0), `ratio` ([0,1]), `percent` ([0,100]), `wer` (word error rate, >= 0, may exceed 1), `cer` (character error rate, >= 0). No member is a currency.
  - `requires: list[InputKind]` (non-empty, unique).
  - `languages: list[SupportedLanguage]` (item 12). Revision 9 (P-59): or the string `"language_independent"`, which requires `script_sensitive=false` (section 20).
  - `script_sensitive: bool` (required).
  - `aggregation`: `PerConversationAggregation{kind: per_conversation}` or `PerTurnShareAggregation{kind: per_turn_share, turn_rule: PassRule}`.
  - `pass_rule: PassRule?`. It must fit `value_type` (item 11 for per-turn share). Range bounds lie within the value domain (for rating, within the scale; for numeric, within the unit's domain; for the share, [0,1]), and the set of passing values must be non-empty, counting integers only for rating and `count`. `one_of` options are a subset of the scale's options.
  - `parameters: list[MetricParameter]` (default `[]`, names unique).
  - `implementation_ref: MachineKey` of the form `<namespace>:<name>` (namespace `[a-z][a-z0-9_]*`, for example `builtin:wer`): required iff kind is deterministic, audio or latency; forbidden for llm_judge. It names the scorer, and the runtime's scorer registry resolves it (Phase 3).
  - `prompt_template: VerbatimText` (non-empty), `prompt_variables: list[MetricKey]` (unique, equal as a set to the `{{name}}` variables used in the template; any other `{{` in a template is rejected), `judge_model_ref: ModelRef`: all three required iff llm_judge and forbidden otherwise. `expected_outcome` is the reserved variable of item 7.
- **`MetricBinding`** (`mbd`, versioned): `evaluator_id: EvaluatorId`, `metric: MetricDefinitionRef`, `params: dict[MetricKey, I-JSON]` (required, may be `{}`, stored unchanged), `enabled: bool` (required). When a binding meets its definition (`check_binding_params(binding, definition)`, called by `validate_rubric` and the P1-12 snapshot): every key is a declared parameter, every value matches its declared `value_type`, and every declared parameter without a default is present. A `ValueError` names the binding ID and the key. The effective parameters are the defaults overlaid by `params`. A different pass threshold therefore needs another definition or version (E6, decided by the owner in revision 9).
- **`RubricItem{binding: MetricBindingRef, required: bool, weight: DecimalString?}`** (value object). The weight is > 0 (item 2) and needs a pass rule (P-55). **`Rubric`** (`rub`, versioned): `items: list[RubricItem]`, non-empty, with at least one required item and no two items with the same binding `id` at any version. `validate_rubric` also rejects: a binding or definition that is not supplied or has another version; bindings of more than one evaluator; two items resolving to the same definition `id`, or to definitions with the same `key` (item 1); a disabled binding (item 8); a required item or a weighted item whose definition has no pass rule; and parameter errors (above). Each error names the binding (or definition) ID.
- **`ScriptedTurn{index: int >= 0, text: VerbatimText, language: SupportedLanguage}`**: strict index, contiguous 0, 1, 2, ... in list order; text non-empty and placeholder-checked.
- **`ExpectedToolCall{name: MachineKey, match, arguments: dict[str, I-JSON]?, order_index: int >= 0}`**:
  - `match = name_only` forbids `arguments`. `arguments_exact` requires `arguments` (`{}` means "called with no arguments"). `arguments_subset` requires a non-empty `arguments`.
  - Comparison is with the observed `ToolCallEvent.arguments`, as JSON values: numbers by value, objects as unordered maps. Exact means equal objects. Subset means every expected top-level key is present with an equal value.
  - String leaves of `arguments` may hold profile placeholders (E7, decided by the owner in revision 9).
  - `order_index` is a **rank**: calls of rank r must all be observed (by `ToolCallEvent.seq`) before any call of rank r + 1, calls of equal rank in any order. The ranks used by an evaluator form 0..R without gaps, so "unordered" is "all 0".
  - Each expected call is matched to a different observed call.
  - Truncated observed arguments (`arguments_complete=false`) can neither pass nor fail an argument comparison (scorer rule, Phase 3).
- **`Evaluator`** (`evl`, versioned):
  - `name: HumanLabel`, `language: SupportedLanguage`.
  - `instructions: VerbatimText` (non-empty, placeholder-checked), `expected_outcome: VerbatimText` (non-empty; placeholder-checked, E7 decided by the owner in revision 9).
  - `persona: PersonaRef`, `test_profile: TestProfileRef?`.
  - `metric_bindings: list[MetricBindingRef]` (non-empty, because item 7 needs at least one check; unique by binding `id` at any version).
  - `scripted_turns: list[ScriptedTurn]` (default `[]`), `expected_tool_calls: list[ExpectedToolCall]` (default `[]`).
  - `expected_tool_calls_exhaustive: Annotated[bool, Strict()] | None = None` (batch 17, F5-2, owner decision; ADR 0010 Decision 8): `None` not stated; `True` a call outside `expected_tool_calls` is a failure (with an empty list: must call nothing); `False` explicitly not exhaustive. Strict boolean or `null`, optional, default `null`, part of the content digest. Tests: `test_evaluator_exhaustive.py` (43).
  - `max_duration_ms` (item 6).
  - Rule: any profile placeholder anywhere in the evaluator requires `test_profile` (N8).

### 19.5 Escalated to the user (revision 8; recommendation given). Revision 9: all five items below were decided by the owner and accepted; see section 20

- **E5. References between definitions may omit the digest (19.4.1 "References"; contradicts the sentence of ADR 0009 Decision 4 accepted in revision 3).** Example: an evaluator says "persona `per_...`, version 2". ADR 0009 Decision 4 says that a reference must also carry the persona's digest unless a shared export requires dropping it. The revision 7 `VersionedRef` and every batch 2 test leave it out. Choice: optional, and verified when present. Recommendation: accept. A run's ConfigSnapshot embeds the referenced records with their own verified digests, and a bundle cannot hold two contents under one `(id, version)`, so inside a run the extra digest adds no check. `TestProfileRef` and `PricingCatalogRef` keep their own rules (ADR 0006). If declined: every reference needs a digest, and the batch 2 documents and `_support_b2.ref()` change. **Decided (rev 9, owner): accepted.**
- **E6. A binding cannot change a pass threshold (item 4).** Example: two evaluators want p95 latency limits of 800 ms and 1500 ms. That takes two metric definitions (or two versions used by different evaluators), not one definition with two binding params. Recommendation: accept, because the threshold then lives in one place, the versioned definition that the verdict reads. **Decided (rev 9, owner): accepted.**
- **E7. Profile placeholders also in the expected outcome and in expected tool-call arguments (widens P-16, which named instructions and scripted turns).** Example: expected call `lookup_account{"account_number": "{{profile.account_number}}"}` and expected outcome "The agent greets {{profile.name}}". Without this, the account number must be written literally into the evaluator, which is copied into every snapshot (the lint would warn). Recommendation: accept. Limitation: only string values can hold a placeholder, so a numeric argument cannot. **Decided (rev 9, owner): accepted.**
- **P-59 (new [Proposed], decide before P1-11). Language applicability for language-independent metrics and for input without a language tag.** Example: an F8 transcript has no language tag. Read literally, ADR 0004 Decision 6 would make every metric `unsupported_language` on it, including p95 latency, which is a false reason. Proposal: (a) `MetricDefinition.languages` may be the single value `"language_independent"` instead of a list, and such a metric skips the language check (latency, interruption counts, talk ratio). (b) A language-dependent metric whose input has no observed language tag is `not_applicable` with a new `na_reason` `language_not_observed`, placed before `unsupported_language` in the P-10 order. Reason: never assume a language, and never report a reason that is untrue. If decided otherwise: item 12 stands as written, and P1-11 must define what an untagged input means for Decision 6. **Decided (rev 9, owner): accepted** (ADR 0004 Decision 6).
- **P-60 (new [Proposed], decide before P1-06 is closed). Wider credential detection in endpoint URLs.** Example: `wss://agent.example.com/ws?access_token=...` is accepted today, because `access_token` is not in the P-20 list. Proposal: add the ADR 0007 Decision 3 credential names (`apikey`, `access_token`, `auth_token`, `refresh_token`, `client_secret`, `password`, `passwd`, `secret`, `bearer`, `authorization`, `credential`, `credentials`, `private_key`) and the presign names `x-amz-security-token`, `x-goog-signature`, `x-goog-credential` to the parameter set, and reject a URL whose percent-decoded text matches a known-credential pattern (ADR 0007 Decision 5a) anywhere, including the path and a SIP user part. Recommendation: accept. If decided otherwise: item 9 stands with the P-20 list. **Decided (rev 9, owner): accepted** (ADR 0007 Decision 6b).

### 19.6 New findings (revision 8)

- **N6 (contradiction, test must change before the gate).** Typed references need their own value-object classes, so `PersonaRef.id`, `MetricDefinitionRef.id` and `MetricBindingRef.id` share the entity field name `id`. ADR 0001 I14 and `test_ids.py::test_value_objects_have_no_entity_fields` allow exactly four such fields. Resolution: I14 is restated as a rule: a value object named `<Entity>Ref`, where `<Entity>` is a registered entity class name, may have `id` typed as `<Entity>Id`; `VersionedRef.id` (any kind) and `aad_fields.schema_version` stay. The test changes from the fixed `REFERENCE_ID_FIELDS` table to that rule (it then also covers `TestProfileRef` and `PricingCatalogRef`). A generic `VersionedRef[PersonaId]` was rejected: pydantic gives it the class name `VersionedRef[PersonaId]`, and it is not a module-level class, so the registry walk would not see it.
- **N7 (ADR text).** ADR 0001 Decision 8 listed `Endpoint` as a value object. It is the union alias of three classes, and Decision 8 now lists the classes and the other new value objects (`PersonaRef`, `MetricDefinitionRef`, `MetricBindingRef`, `ModelRef`, `MetricParameter`, `RatingScale`, `EnumScale`, `PerConversationAggregation`, `PerTurnShareAggregation`, `RubricItem`, `WebSocketEndpoint`, `WebRtcEndpoint`, `SipEndpoint`, `LiteralProfileValue`).
- **N8 (new rule, tests must change).** A profile placeholder that has no profile to fill it would reach the simulated caller as literal text. Resolution: an Evaluator with any profile placeholder must have `test_profile`. Tests to change: `test_persona_profile.py::test_every_profile_field_is_usable_as_a_placeholder` (give the evaluator a `test_profile`), `test_persona_profile.py::test_literal_profile_value_in_free_text_is_flagged_with_path_not_value` (both evaluators carry `{{profile.name}}`, so they need a `test_profile`). Proposed: `test_profile_placeholder_requires_test_profile`.
- **N9 (completeness).** `ExpectedToolCall` had no expected arguments (resolved by item 5), so argument correctness was not expressible.
- **N10 (revision 9: proposed as P-61, ADR 0010; decide before P1-11).** `implementation_ref` names a scorer but not its build, so results from a changed scorer under one definition version cannot be told apart. Recommendation for P1-11: record the scorer and its version on each MetricResult, as `JudgeInfo` does for judges.
- **N11 (open, staff-reviewer).** Plan fields not adopted in 0.0.1: Persona `speaking_pace`, `interruption_style`, `emotion`, `background_noise`, `voice_ref`; AgentTarget `description`, `declared_tier`, `adapter`; Project's default rubric. Adding optional fields later is a minor change. The persona fields need closed sets reviewed with indic-linguistics before the simulator (Phase 4). Whether MVP-1 needs any of them before the Phase 1 gate is for staff-reviewer.
- **N12 (pre-existing tension, staff-reviewer).** Some rules cannot be written in JSON Schema: template variables equal to `prompt_variables`, `one_of` options a subset of the scale, contiguous indexes and ranks, uniqueness by `id` or by primary language, placeholder field names, URL parameter names, `config_ref` credential patterns. `test_invalid_rejected_by_both` needs a `KNOWN_SCHEMA_GAPS` entry for each, but gate item 2 allows only non-blocking mapping gaps there (HANDOFF already lists `HumanLabel` and `UtcDatetime`). Recommendation: a separately named list of code-only rules, each with owner and reason, allowed by gate item 2.
- **N13 (limitation).** The lint folds text with the running Python's Unicode database (believed to be Unicode 14.0 on 3.11 and 16.0 on 3.14, not verified here), so hits can differ for characters assigned after 14.0. It is a warning, not a validation rule, so no document's validity changes.
- **Unverified:**
  - The exact pydantic constructs that emit `minimum` together with `exclusiveMinimum`, emit the `if`/`then` rules, and keep discriminator mapping correct with `WithJsonSchema` tags (P1-08/P1-09; only the JSON Schema semantics were checked).
  - SIP parsing of telephone-subscriber user parts (RFC 3966) beyond the cases tested.
  - Whether F5 scenario YAML expected tool calls carry arguments in a form `arguments_exact`/`arguments_subset` can express (P1-34).

### 19.7 What the revision 8 tests pin (qa, 2026-10-23)

Owner: qa. The owner accepted E5, E6, E7, P-59 and P-60 as proposed, so the tests treat them as binding. Evidence: `docs/phase1/evidence/red/batch2-rev8/`. A throwaway oracle (outside the repo) passes every new and changed test, and all 259 injected mutants are caught.

**Changed existing tests (7).** The six that 19.4 and 19.6 name: `_support_b2.py::judge_definition`, `test_open_value_fields_accept_unseen_provider`, `test_expected_tool_call_match_enum`, `test_every_profile_field_is_usable_as_a_placeholder`, `test_literal_profile_value_in_free_text_is_flagged_with_path_not_value`, and `test_ids.py::test_value_objects_have_no_entity_fields` (now the `<Entity>Ref` rule; `VALUE_OBJECT_NAMES` gains the revision 8 value objects). One more that the architect did not list: `test_numeric_units_exclude_currency` uses the default pass rule `max 800`, which lies outside the domain of `ratio` and `percent`, so its positive control now passes `pass_rule=None` (the assertion is unchanged, and `cer` is added). Also edited: `test_rubric_weight_values` gets the rejected value `"0"` (19.4 row 2), `test_all_closed_enums_exact` gets the rows ADR 0003 I11 asks for (`unit`, `MetricParameter.value_type`, `DeclaredStackEntry.stage`, `ExpectedToolCall.match`) and `na_reason` gains `language_not_observed` (P-59 (b); ADR 0003 still lists four members and needs the amendment).

**Locations proposed** (19.1 fixes none): `tracer_evalschema.metrics.render_metric_ref(definition) -> str` and `tracer_evalschema.metrics.check_binding_params(binding, definition) -> None`. The value objects of 19.4.1 are found by class name through `registry.model_classes()`, so their module is free.

**Readings the tests pin where the text leaves room** (confirm or escalate):
- P-59 (a) is the scalar string `"language_independent"`, not a one-element list; a list containing it is rejected.
- `MetricParameter.default` of `null` means "no default" (`model_dump` writes `null` for an absent default, and sealed documents round-trip it), so a `json` parameter cannot default to JSON null.
- Judge-only fields are tested with non-null values on the other kinds; an explicit `null` is not tested. A judge must carry the `prompt_variables` key (it may be `[]`).
- Parameter names: a query segment without `=` is a name (`?token` is rejected); `;` and `&` both split; a malformed escape or invalid UTF-8 in a name rejects the URL; `ſ` (U+017F) must fold to `s`. An IPv6 literal host (`ws://[::1]:8765/ws`) is accepted. High-entropy tokens (Decision 5a, second reason) are not part of the P-60 URL check and are not tested either way.
- Under E7 the placeholders in `ExpectedToolCall.arguments` are checked in every string leaf at any depth (19.4.1 says "leaves", ADR 0006 9a says "values"); dictionary keys are never placeholder text.
- Ranks of `order_index` are tested as a set (0..R without gaps, several calls per rank); the order of the list is not tested.
- The kind, scale, unit and `match`/`arguments` conditions are tested to appear in the JSON Schema (`allOf` of `if`/`then`, 19.4.1), because N12 does not list them as code-only.
- For a `per_turn_share` `turn_rule` the tests pin the bounds within the value domain; the non-empty passing set is pinned for `pass_rule` only.
- A lint hit next to a zero-width joiner or non-joiner counts as bounded (those are category Cf, not L, M or N). Not tested; indic-linguistics should confirm that this is intended for Indic words that contain them.
- Not tested: whether `validate_rubric` verifies the `content_digest` of a reference (E5: the bundle seam does, shown in `test_reference_digest_is_optional_and_verified_when_present`); the equality of `VerdictCheck.metric_ref` with the binding's `metric` (P1-22); the rule that an evaluator has an expected-outcome check (P1-12); `language_independent` skipping the language check and `language_not_observed` (P1-11).
- N15 (accepted by the owner): `test_url_credential_parameter_names_exact` pins `credentials.URL_CREDENTIAL_PARAMETER_NAMES` as exactly 25 case-folded names (the 23 of ADR 0007 Decision 6b plus `x-api-key` and `x-goog-api-key`) and `test_url_credential_parameter_names_hyphen_underscore_equivalent` pins `-` equal to `_` (also when percent-encoded) with look-alikes such as `access.token`, `accesstoken` and `x-api-keys` accepted. The ADR text in revision 9 still says 23 names and "`access-token` passes"; it needs the amendment.
- Revision 9 test names in 20.4 that are not written yet (not requested in this batch): `test_url_credential_pattern_anywhere_rejected`, `test_url_high_entropy_segment_accepted`, `test_metric_languages_list_or_language_independent`, `test_language_independent_metric_not_script_sensitive`, `test_artifact_uri_...` and the P1-11/P1-36 ones. My P-60 tests already cover the pattern rule in the path, query and SIP user part, as written and percent-decoded; they do not pin the decoded-once/as-written pair or the high-entropy acceptance.


**Revision 9 group A additions (qa, 2026-10-23).** Evidence: `docs/phase1/evidence/red/batch2-rev8/` (`*-rev9a.*.log`, `mutants-group-a-rev9.txt`, SUMMARY section "Revision 9 group A additions"). This supersedes the bullet above that says high-entropy tokens are "not tested either way" and the one that lists the four names as not written. Written: `test_url_credential_pattern_anywhere_rejected`, `test_url_high_entropy_segment_accepted` (P1-06), `test_metric_languages_list_or_language_independent`, `test_language_independent_metric_not_script_sensitive` (P1-08), plus one proposed name, `test_url_pattern_fitting_slug_is_rejected_known_false_positive` (the positive/negative pair that documents the 6b false positive).
- Readings pinned that the text leaves room for (confirm or escalate): (a) the error message of a pattern rejection contains `pattern` or `credential` and one word for the part: `path`, `query`, `header`, `parameter`, `user` or `authority` (SIP user part), `host` or `author` (host labels); the exact wording is free. (b) A malformed percent escape or invalid UTF-8 anywhere in the URL, including the path and a SIP user part, rejects it (6b: "over its whole length"), not only in parameter names. (c) "Decoded once" means a double-encoded pattern (`%2573k-...`) is accepted. (d) The patterns are case-sensitive (`SK-...`, `akia...` pass); the AWS tail class is upper case and digits only. (e) Every pattern is searched separately in each part, which equals a search of the whole text because a separator character is never part of a pattern; the tests do not depend on that. (f) The percent-encoded PEM header (`-----BEGIN%20RSA%20PRIVATE%20KEY-----`) is rejected; the written form with spaces is already rejected by the white-space rule. (g) `["language_independent"]`, `[]`, a sentinel in another case, with white space or as any non-string is rejected; the schema is an `anyOf` of exactly two branches (string `enum` of the sentinel, array with `minItems: 1`). (h) The sentinel with `script_sensitive=True` is rejected at `languages`, at `script_sensitive` or at the model, with a message that holds `script_sensitive` or `language_independent`, for every kind and in the JSON entry point. Whether the rule is also emitted in the JSON Schema is not pinned (N12 keeps it code-only).
- Not covered here: `Artifact.uri` (P1-10a `test_artifact_uri_rejects_wider_credential_names_and_patterns`); percent-encoded host labels; revision 10 text (the architect is amending ADR 0003 and 0007 to 5 `na_reason` members and 25 URL names; these tests use the 25 names already and do not touch `na_reason`).

## 20. Revision 9 (2026-10-23; decider of E5-E7, P-59, P-60: owner; notes: schema-architect)

### 20.1 Owner decisions and how they were applied
| Item | Decision | Where | Contract change |
|---|---|---|---|
| E5 | Accepted | ADR 0009 Decision 4 (rewritten, with rationale) | None beyond 19.4.1. `PersonaRef`, `MetricDefinitionRef` and `MetricBindingRef` have `content_digest?`, verified when present. `TestProfileRef` and `PricingCatalogRef` are unchanged. |
| E6 | Accepted | ADR 0010 Decision 4a | None beyond 19.4.1. At P1-11, `passed` is computed from the definition's `pass_rule` alone and never from binding params. |
| E7 | Accepted | ADR 0006 Decision 9a, ADR 0010 Decision 8, ADR 0013 Decision 2 | Placeholder rules apply to `Evaluator.expected_outcome` and to every string value, at any depth, of `ExpectedToolCall.arguments`. Object keys and non-string values are not checked. Any placeholder in these places also requires `test_profile`. |
| P-59 | Accepted | ADR 0004 Decisions 4, 6, 7; ADR 0003; ADR 0005 Decision 6; ADR 0010 Decision 1a | See 20.2. |
| P-60 | Accepted | ADR 0007 Decision 6b | See 20.3. |

### 20.2 P-59 contract
- `MetricDefinition.languages: Literal["language_independent"] | list[SupportedLanguage]`. It is required, with no default. The JSON Schema is `anyOf` of `{"type": "string", "enum": ["language_independent"]}` and the revision 8 array schema (`minItems: 1`). The distinct-primary-language rule applies to the list form.
- Model validator: `languages == "language_independent"` with `script_sensitive=True` is rejected at `languages` (or at the model level), and the message names the rule.
- `na_reason` closed set: `missing_input`, `agent_channel_unknown`, `language_not_observed`, `unsupported_language`, `unsupported_script`. Precedence is in that order.
- The P1-11 language steps (ADR 0004 Decisions 6c-6e) are pure functions over the definition, the read turns and their transcripts. Suggested seam: `language_na_reason(definition, turns, transcripts) -> NaReason | None`. P1-11 may name it differently; tests should call it through `missing_inputs()`-style helpers or through MetricResult validation, whichever P1-11 pins.

### 20.3 P-60 contract
- `tracer_evalschema.credentials.URL_CREDENTIAL_PARAMETER_NAMES: frozenset[str]` holds exactly these 23 names (revision 10: 25 names, see 21.3): `token`, `key`, `api_key`, `sig`, `signature`, `x-amz-signature`, `x-amz-credential`, `apikey`, `access_token`, `auth_token`, `refresh_token`, `client_secret`, `password`, `passwd`, `secret`, `bearer`, `authorization`, `credential`, `credentials`, `private_key`, `x-amz-security-token`, `x-goog-signature`, `x-goog-credential`.
- Name matching is unchanged from 19.4 item 9. (Revision 10: `-` and `_` are equal, see 21.3.)
- Pattern rule: the ADR 0007 Decision 5a `known_credential_prefix` patterns are searched, with the 5a left boundary, in the URL as written and in the URL percent-decoded once as UTF-8. Any match rejects the URL. `high_entropy_token` is not used.
- Scope: `WebSocketEndpoint.url`, `WebRtcEndpoint.url`, `SipEndpoint.uri` (P1-06) and `Artifact.uri` (P1-10a).
- Errors name the rule and the URL part, never the value (ADR 0007 Decision 8). Both rules are code-only (19.6 N12).
- Every URL that the batch 2 tests accept is still accepted. Every URL that they reject is still rejected.

### 20.4 New test names for qa (each also listed in SCOPE section 5 under its task)
Binding since revision 9, names unchanged from revision 8: `test_language_independent_metric_skips_language_check` and `test_untagged_input_is_language_not_observed` (P1-11), `test_endpoint_rejects_wider_credential_names_and_prefixes` (P1-06).

New (revision 9):
- P1-06: `test_url_credential_parameter_names_exact`, `test_url_credential_pattern_anywhere_rejected`, `test_url_high_entropy_segment_accepted`.
- P1-08: `test_metric_languages_list_or_language_independent`, `test_language_independent_metric_not_script_sensitive`.
- P1-09 (and P1-12): `test_profile_placeholders_in_expected_outcome_and_tool_call_arguments`, `test_definition_refs_digest_optional_and_verified_when_present`.
- P1-10a: `test_artifact_uri_rejects_wider_credential_names_and_patterns`.
- P1-11: `test_und_and_unparseable_labels_are_language_not_observed`, `test_turn_language_takes_precedence_over_transcript_language`, `test_configured_language_never_used_as_observed`, `test_per_conversation_any_untagged_turn_is_language_not_observed`, `test_per_turn_share_untagged_turns_not_counted`, `test_passed_uses_definition_pass_rule_not_binding_params`.
- P1-36 (F8): `test_f8_untagged_transcript_language_reasons`.
- Conditional on P-61 (P1-11): `test_scorer_info_present_iff_non_judge_scored_or_error`, `test_scorer_info_matches_definition_implementation_ref`, `test_scorer_version_stored_verbatim`.

Meaning extended, name kept: `test_na_reason_precedence` (five reasons), `test_all_closed_enums_exact` (its table's `MetricResult` `na_reason` row must gain `language_not_observed`; qa may add a `MetricDefinition` `languages` row for the sentinel), `test_metric_languages_nonempty_distinct_primary` (the list form only). No test is renamed or removed.

qa-owned test that must change: `test_metric_definition.py::test_all_closed_enums_exact`, the `na_reason` row of `_closed_enum_table()`. It is checked only once `MetricResult` exists (P1-11), so it does not affect group A.

### 20.5 New findings and open items (revision 9)
- **P-61 ([Proposed], ADR 0010; decide before P1-11).** Each result records its scorer and version: `MetricResult.scorer: ScorerInfo{implementation_ref: MachineKey, version: MachineKey}`. It is present iff the definition's kind is deterministic, audio or latency and the status is scored or error. It is absent for not_applicable and for llm_judge. Its `implementation_ref` equals the definition's, and `version` is verbatim and compared only for equality. The proposal is user-visible, so it is not applied. Recommendation: accept. **Decided (rev 10, owner): accepted as proposed** (21.1).
- **N14 (open, owner).** Under P-59, an import with no language tag (F8) gives `language_not_observed` for every language-dependent metric until a producer records a tag, even when the user knows the call's language. One option is to let a user assert a language for an import, as an observation with its own provenance (for example a new `Transcript.source` value or a per-record field). That would be a new design decision. It is not proposed here, because the owner's P-59 text says "no observed language tag". Recommendation: ask the owner before P1-13 / P1-36. **Decided (rev 10, owner): accepted**, as a declaration that is never an observation (21.2).
- **N15 (open, owner).** P-60 compares whole names, so `access-token`, `api-key` and `x-api-key` pass the name check. Only the pattern rule can still catch such a value. Options: compare names with `-` and `_` treated as equal, or add the hyphenated forms to the set. Recommendation: treat `-` and `_` as equal when comparing, which catches `access-token` and `api-key`. Separately add `x-api-key` and `x-goog-api-key`, which no folding catches. Ask the owner before P1-06 is closed. **Decided (rev 10, owner): accepted** (21.3).
- **N16 (note).** `JudgeInfo.model_ref` names the model a judge was asked for. It does not record the model version that the provider reports in its response (provider aliases move). Whether to record a provider-reported model string, kept verbatim, belongs to P1-11 together with P-61. It is not proposed yet. **Revision 10: proposed as P-62** (21.1).
- **Unverified:** the presign parameter names of the cloud providers (ADR 0007 Decision 6b) were taken from our knowledge of their documentation and were not re-checked against current provider documentation in this revision. The claim that every known presign form carries `Signature` or `sig` is unverified for providers not named there.

## 21. Revision 10 (2026-10-23; decider of P-61, N14, N15: owner; notes: schema-architect)

### 21.1 P-61 contract (accepted) and P-62 (proposed, from N16)
- **`ScorerInfo{implementation_ref: MachineKey, version: MachineKey}`** (value object, both required). **`MetricResult.scorer: ScorerInfo | None`.**
  - Required iff the definition's `kind` is `deterministic`, `audio` or `latency` and `status` is `scored` or `error`. Forbidden otherwise (every `not_applicable` result and every `llm_judge` result).
  - Presence depends on the definition's kind, so it is checked where the result meets its definition. P1-11 names that check; the batch 2 style is a pure function such as `check_result_against_definition(result, definition) -> None` raising `ValueError` that names the result ID. The same check verifies `scorer.implementation_ref == definition.implementation_ref`. The bundle check (P1-14) applies it to every result in a bundle.
  - `version` is kept verbatim: no normalization, no parsing, no ordering; equality only. `"1.4.2"` and `"v1.4.2"` are different versions.
  - A scorer the registry cannot resolve stops the run before it starts (Phase 3). No non-judge `error` result lacks `scorer` (ADR 0010 Decision 5a).
- **P-62 ([Proposed], decide before P1-11; not applied).** `JudgeInfo.reported_model: VerbatimText | None` and `JudgeInfo.per_turn[].reported_model: VerbatimText | None`. Each is present iff the provider's response to that call carried a model string. The value is non-empty and verbatim, never filled from `model_ref`, and compared only for equality (ADR 0010 Decision 6 and "Proposed items awaiting owner review (revision 10)"). Conditional tests: `test_judge_reported_model_verbatim_and_optional`, `test_judge_per_turn_reported_model_verbatim_and_optional`.

### 21.2 N14 contract (accepted): declared languages
- **`LanguageDeclaration`** (value object): `language: SupportedLanguage` with no script subtag, `source: "user_declared"` (closed set of one, emitted as `{"type": "string", "enum": ["user_declared"]}`), `declared_by: MachineKey` with no `@`, `declared_at: UtcDatetime`. All four are required.
- **`Transcript.declared_language: LanguageDeclaration | None`** (default `None`). A model validator rejects it unless `source` is one of `agent_export`, `agent_telemetry`, `observer`, `human_reference` and `detected_language` and `detected_language_raw` are both `None`. The message names the rule.
- **Effective language of a read turn** (pure function; P1-11 names it, extending the 20.2 seam). The function returns `(language, language_source)` or "none":
  1. `Turn.language` not `und`: that tag, `observed`.
  2. `Turn.language` is `und`, or `Turn.language_raw` is present while `Turn.language` is `None`: none.
  3. `Transcript.detected_language` not `und`: that tag, `observed`.
  4. `Transcript.detected_language` is `und`, or `detected_language_raw` is present while the tag is `None`: none.
  5. `Transcript.declared_language` present and the definition has `script_sensitive=False`: the declared tag, `declared`.
  6. Otherwise none.
  Steps 1-4 are the P-59 rules of ADR 0004 Decision 6c, unchanged. Configured languages are never read.
- **Language steps** (ADR 0004 Decision 6e, amended): (1) any read turn with no effective language gives `language_not_observed`; (2) any effective primary language not covered gives `unsupported_language`; (3) for `script_sensitive=True`, the script rule on observed tags gives `unsupported_script`; (4) otherwise scored. The precedence of the five reasons is unchanged.
- **`MetricResult.language_source: Literal["observed", "declared"] | None`** (closed set) and **`MetricResult.declared_language_transcript_ids: list[TranscriptId]`** (default `[]`, unique, sorted by string form).
  - On the result alone: `declared_language_transcript_ids` is non-empty iff `language_source == "declared"`, and `language_source` is `None` when `na_reason` is `missing_input` or `agent_channel_unknown`.
  - Against the definition (the P1-11 check): `language_source` must be `None` for `languages == "language_independent"`. It must not be `"declared"` when `script_sensitive=True`. For the list form it is required iff the language steps found an effective language for at least one read turn (ADR 0004 Decision 6g).
  - In a bundle (P1-14): every listed transcript exists and has `declared_language`.
- `DatasetItem.language` (P1-13) and every other configured language are never copied into `declared_language`.
- Decision 6h of ADR 0004 is the F8 worked example. The P1-36 test runs it on the public synthetic twin, with a declaration supplied by the test.

### 21.3 N15 contract (accepted): URL credential parameter names
- `tracer_evalschema.credentials.URL_CREDENTIAL_PARAMETER_NAMES: frozenset[str]` holds exactly these 25 names: `token`, `key`, `api_key`, `sig`, `signature`, `x-amz-signature`, `x-amz-credential`, `apikey`, `access_token`, `auth_token`, `refresh_token`, `client_secret`, `password`, `passwd`, `secret`, `bearer`, `authorization`, `credential`, `credentials`, `private_key`, `x-amz-security-token`, `x-goog-signature`, `x-goog-credential`, `x-api-key`, `x-goog-api-key`.
- Comparison: percent-decode once as UTF-8 (malformed rejects), then `str.casefold()`, then replace every U+002D with U+005F, one for one. The set entries go through the same replacement, and the strings must be equal as wholes. No other character is equated. The pattern rule of 20.3 is unchanged.
- No two entries are equal after the replacement.
- Errors name the parameter as found in the URL, never its value.

### 21.4 New test names for qa (each also listed in SCOPE section 5 under its task)
Binding since revision 10, names unchanged from revision 9 (P-61, P1-11): `test_scorer_info_present_iff_non_judge_scored_or_error`, `test_scorer_info_matches_definition_implementation_ref`, `test_scorer_version_stored_verbatim`.

New (revision 10):
- P1-06 (N15): `test_url_credential_parameter_names_hyphen_underscore_equivalent`. Meaning extended, name kept: `test_url_credential_parameter_names_exact` (25 names).
- P1-10b (N14): `test_transcript_declared_language_shape_and_provenance`, `test_declared_language_only_on_untagged_imported_transcript`.
- P1-11 (N14): `test_declared_language_lifts_language_not_observed`, `test_declared_language_never_treated_as_observed`, `test_declared_language_not_used_for_und_or_unparseable_labels`, `test_observed_turn_tag_takes_precedence_over_declared_language`, `test_declared_language_still_gives_unsupported_language`, `test_declaration_does_not_suppress_unsupported_script`, `test_script_sensitive_metric_ignores_declared_language`, `test_result_language_source_presence_and_values`, `test_declared_language_transcript_ids_iff_declared`.
- P1-13 (N14): `test_dataset_item_language_is_not_a_declaration`.
- P1-14 (N14): `test_bundle_declared_language_transcript_ids_resolve`.
- P1-36 (N14, F8): `test_f8_declared_language_reasons`.
- Conditional on P-62 (P1-11): `test_judge_reported_model_verbatim_and_optional`, `test_judge_per_turn_reported_model_verbatim_and_optional`.

Meaning extended, name kept: `test_all_closed_enums_exact`. Once P1-10b and P1-11 exist, its table gains rows for `LanguageDeclaration.source` (`user_declared`) and `MetricResult.language_source` (`observed`, `declared`). `test_ids.py::test_value_objects_have_no_entity_fields`: `VALUE_OBJECT_NAMES` gains `ScorerInfo` and `LanguageDeclaration` once they exist. No test is renamed or removed.

### 21.5 New findings and open items (revision 10)
- **P-62 ([Proposed], ADR 0010; decide before P1-11).** See 21.1. Recommendation: accept. **Decided by the owner (recorded in revision 11): accepted as proposed;** the two conditional tests are binding under P1-11.
- **N17 (note, not proposed).** With a declaration, a script-sensitive metric on declared-only turns gives `language_not_observed`. That is true, because the language was not observed, but it is less specific than it could be: the language is declared and only the script is unknown. A new reason `script_not_observed` would say this exactly. It would add an `na_reason` and change the five-reason precedence that the owner accepted, so it is not adopted. Reports can still tell the cases apart, because the transcript carries `declared_language`. Revisit when Phase 3 records script evidence (P-11).
- **N18 (open, Phase 2).** In 0.0.1 a declaration is supplied at import only. Adding or correcting a declaration on a stored transcript is undefined. With idempotent re-import (P-02) a second import of the same external call finds the existing transcript, so the declaration cannot be changed by re-importing either. Phase 2 must choose between two options: a new transcript record linked to the old one, or a versioned declaration. Either way, results that cite the old declaration must keep their meaning.
- **N19 (open, security-licensing at P1-14).** `LanguageDeclaration.declared_by` names an actor. `@` is rejected, but a `MachineKey` can still hold a phone number or a user handle. Whether shared and benchmark exports drop or key it, like `Run.caller_external_id`, is decided at P1-14.
- **Details for owner confirmation** (ADR 0004 and ADR 0007 "Decided items (revision 10)", ADR 0010 Decision 5a): listed there, not repeated here.
- **Unverified:** the claim that servers commonly treat `-` and `_` as interchangeable in parameter names, which is the reason given for N15 (true of some frameworks' header handling; not surveyed). The cloud presign names (20.5) are still not re-checked. The qa N15 tests were read, not run.

## 22. Batch 3 (tasks P1-10a, P1-10b, P1-10c, P1-11, P1-22, P1-12, P1-13 and the privacy foundation P1-27): API assumed by the tests

Owner: qa (2026-10-23); revision 11 (schema-architect, 2026-10-23) records the owner's decisions on N20 to N27 and P-62, resolves what the ADRs settle (22.3), lists the test changes (22.9) and the questions for the owner (22.10); revision 12 (schema-architect, 2026-10-23) records the owner's decisions on O1 to O7 (22.10), extends 22.9 and lists new owner items (22.11); revision 13 (schema-architect, 2026-10-23) records the owner's decisions on O3a, O8, O9 and N41 (all option A) and the owner's confirmation of the O1 and O5 details (22.11), turns 22.9 into one final, unconditional list of test changes, and records new finding N42 and the details fixed while applying the decisions (22.12). Audience: schema-architect (implements exactly this), staff-reviewer. Tests: `test_privacy.py`, `test_artifact.py`, `test_transcript.py`, `test_events.py`, `test_results.py`, `test_results_language.py`, `test_verdict.py`, `test_snapshot.py`, `test_run.py`, `test_dataset.py`, `test_batch3_entities.py`; helpers `_support_b3.py`. Status: written and red; nothing in `src/` was written by qa. A throwaway oracle implementation (outside the repo, built on the real batch 1 and 2 code) passes every new test except `test_policy_documents_free_text_lint_limitation` (P1-20a), on Python 3.11 to 3.14, and 470 injected mutants were run against it (section 22.8). If a name or field below is wrong for a good reason, escalate to staff-reviewer; do not change a test.

The owner's decisions P-61, P-62, N14 and N15, E5-E7, P-59 and P-60 are treated as binding, with the revision 10 contract of sections 20 and 21 unchanged. Where the ADRs name a shape but not a field, a type or a module, section 22.1 pins one reading and section 22.3 lists it as an ambiguity. Readings the tests pin are marked (P). Conventions of section 19 apply (`extra="forbid"`, closed sets emit `enum`, typed errors unwrapped, rules across fields reported at the field they are about, value objects carry no entity fields).

### 22.1 Modules, classes and pinned fields

Entities are `EntityBase` (`id`, `schema_version`, `created_at`, `metadata`, `metadata_flags`) unless a row says otherwise. Every field not marked optional is required. Value objects have no entity fields. Helper return types marked `NamedTuple` are plain `typing.NamedTuple` classes, not pydantic models (so they are not value objects of the registry).

**`tracer_evalschema.privacy` (P1-27)**

| Name | Kind | Pinned contract |
|---|---|---|
| `ConsentRecord` | value object | `status` (obtained, not_obtained, unknown), `reference?: VerbatimText`, `recorded_at?: UtcDatetime` |
| `RetentionPolicy` | value object | `retain_until?: UtcDatetime`, `policy_ref?: MachineKey` (P, the tests give `policy:retain-12-months`), `delete_on_expiry: bool` (required, strict) |
| `PrivacyMarker` | value object | `human_subject: bool` (required, strict), `human_subject_reason?` (marked_before_role_evidence, real_person_likeness, manual), `consent?`, `retention?`. Consent and retention are both present iff `human_subject` is true (P-46). A reason with `human_subject=false` is rejected (P). Errors are located at `consent`, `retention` and `human_subject_reason` |
| `derive_human_subject(run, turns) -> bool` | function (P; rev 12: `turns` required, O1) | `run` is a `Run` (reads `caller_origin` and `channel_maps[].channels[].role`); `turns` are the `Turn` records of the run's transcripts (reads `role`). True for `caller_origin=human`; true if there is no role evidence at all; true if any role is `unknown`; otherwise false. A `mixed` channel is evidence of caller and agent. Role evidence is the set of roles found, never the presence of a container: no turns supplied and no channel role give "no role evidence", so true, never false (N24, decided by the owner in revision 11). Turns are an argument because a `Run` does not hold them. (rev 12, O1 decided by the owner, option A) `turns` has no default: omitting it raises `TypeError`, and `turns=None` raises `TypeError` too (detail confirmed by the owner in revision 13: only a real sequence is accepted); an explicit `()` or `[]` is "no turns" |
| `check_human_subject_marker(marker, run, turns) -> None` | function (P; rev 12: `turns` required, O1) | `ValueError`: stored false where derived true ("looser"), or stored true where derived false without a reason. A reason on a true marker is always accepted. The marker is never changed. Same `turns` parameter as `derive_human_subject` (N24; required since rev 12, O1) |

**`tracer_evalschema.artifact` (P1-10a)**

| Name | Kind | Pinned contract |
|---|---|---|
| `AudioFormat` | value object | `container: MachineKey`, `codec: MachineKey`, `sample_rate_hz`, `channels` (required), `bits_per_sample?` (optional, revision 11, N27): strict integers > 0 when present. A lossy codec has no bit depth, so a producer omits it rather than inventing one. No rule ties it to a codec, because `codec` is an open value. An explicit `null` is the same as absent |
| `Artifact` (`art`) | entity | `uri` (see below), `sha256` (64 lowercase hex), `size` (strict int >= 0), `audio_format?`, `turn_ref?: TurnId`, `privacy: PrivacyMarker`. An artifact is audio iff `audio_format` is present. `hide_input_in_errors=True` (ADR 0007 Decision 8 (a); the set stays closed under embedding) |
| `ChannelAssignment` | value object (P, name) | `index` (int >= 0), `role` (caller, agent, unknown, mixed) |
| `ChannelMap` | value object | `artifact_id: ArtifactId`, `channels: list[ChannelAssignment]` (non-empty, revision 11, N36 part), `determination` (declared, inferred), `method: MachineKey` (required for both, P), `confidence?` (strict float in [0,1], finite). Declared: no confidence at all. Inferred: confidence required. At most one `agent` channel. Channel indexes are unique (P). Errors at `confidence` and `channels` |
| `agent_audio_available(channel_maps) -> bool` | function (P) | true iff some map has exactly one `agent` channel. No map, `unknown` only, `mixed` only: false |
| `check_channel_map(channel_map, artifact) -> None` | function (P) | `ValueError`: the map names another artifact, the artifact has no `audio_format`, or a channel `index` is >= `audio_format.channels` |

`Artifact.uri` (P-20, P-60, N15, ADR 0007 Decisions 6, 6a, 6b): an absolute RFC 3986 URI with a lowercase scheme and a host, ASCII only, no white space, no userinfo. (rev 12, O5 decided by the owner, option A; ADR 0007 Decision 6c) The scheme set is closed: `http`, `https`, `s3`, `gs`, `file`. `http`, `https`, `s3`, `gs` need `<scheme>://` and a non-empty host. `file:` only as `file:///` followed by an absolute path: no host (`localhost` in any case and every other host rejected), not the `file:/path` form, no query, no `.` or `..` segment, not an empty path (these details confirmed by the owner in revision 13); `file:///C:/rec/1.wav` is an absolute path and accepted (confirmed in revision 13). A `#` is rejected for every scheme, including an empty fragment. Relative references and `//host/...` are rejected. Messages name the rule and the part ("scheme not allowed", "fragment not allowed", "file URI must be file:/// with an absolute path"). (rev 13, O8 decided by the owner, option A; ADR 0007 Decision 6d) Loopback hosts in `http`/`https` URIs are accepted on the record and in `internal` exports; P1-14 rejects them in `shared_export` and `benchmark` exports. A loopback host is, after ASCII case-folding and removal of one trailing dot, `localhost` or a name ending in `.localhost`, an IPv4 host that the WHATWG URL Standard IPv4 parser maps into `127.0.0.0/8` (so `127.0.0.1`, `127.1`, `0x7f.0.0.1` and `2130706433` all count), or an IPv6 literal equal to `::1` or to an IPv4-mapped `::ffff:127.0.0.0/104` address in any spelling (ADR 0007 Decision 6d; the exact definition is a detail fixed while applying O8, 22.12). P1-14 rejects `file:` URIs in `shared_export` and `benchmark` exports. Every one of the 25 names of `credentials.URL_CREDENTIAL_PARAMETER_NAMES` is rejected in the query in any case, with `-` and `_` interchangeable and percent-encoded; a Decision 5a credential pattern anywhere (host, path, query), as written or percent-decoded once, is rejected; a high-entropy segment without a named pattern is accepted. Messages name the rule and the part, never the value. The rules are code-only (N12).

Run rule (P): `"agent_audio" in Run.available_inputs` requires `agent_audio_available(Run.channel_maps)`; the error is located at `available_inputs`. The converse is not required.

**`tracer_evalschema.transcript` (P1-10b)**

| Name | Kind | Pinned contract |
|---|---|---|
| `WallTime` | value object | `at: UtcDatetime`, `meaning` (start, end, logged, unknown) |
| `TimelineOrigin` | value object | `kind` (artifact, telemetry, observer), `ref?: ExternalRef or ArtifactId`, `started_at?: UtcDatetime`. `kind=artifact` needs `ref` to be an `art_` ID (an `ExternalRef` is rejected); telemetry and observer take an `ExternalRef` or nothing and reject an `art_` ID (P). When `ref` is an `ExternalRef` it serializes as the object, when an ID as the string (declare the union so that the serializer picks the right member; a one-function-serializer for IDs applied to the whole union serialized the object as text in the oracle). Revision 11 (N39, resolved): one field `ref`, as ADR 0002 Decision 3 names it; the member is chosen by an explicit discriminator on the input's JSON type (string: the ID, object: the `ExternalRef`), not by union order, and the JSON Schema is a `oneOf` of the ID pattern and the `ExternalRef` object |
| `Segment` | value object | `granularity` (word, phrase, utterance), `start_ms?`, `end_ms?`, `text: VerbatimText`, `confidence?` (strict float in [0,1]), `contains_bidi_controls` (computed when omitted, verified when supplied, serialized). `end_ms >= start_ms` |
| `Turn` (`turn`) | entity | `transcript_id: TranscriptId` (P, N21, decided by the owner in revision 11: turns are separate records and the transcript does not embed them), `seq` (strict int >= 0, required), `role` (caller, agent, unknown), `start_ms?`, `end_ms?`, `wall_time?`, `timing_source` (audio_alignment, telemetry, observer, none; required), `source` (the eight of ADR 0003; required), `text`, `segments?` (a list, may be empty), `language?: ObservedLanguageTag`, `language_raw?: VerbatimText`, `interrupted?` (bool or None, default None), `exchange_index?` (int >= 0), `contains_bidi_controls`. `timing_source=none` iff neither offset is present (a `wall_time` is not an offset); `end_ms >= start_ms`; each segment bound lies inside the same-named turn bound when both are present. No `started_at`, no confidence. Errors at `timing_source`, `end_ms`, `segments`, `contains_bidi_controls` |
| `Exchange` (`exc`) | entity | `index` (int >= 0), `started_at?`, `duration_ms?`, `was_interrupted?` (bool or None, default None), `source` (audio_alignment, telemetry, observer, inferred_from_turns) |
| `LanguageDeclaration` | value object | `language: SupportedLanguage` without a script subtag, `source` (exactly `user_declared`), `declared_by: MachineKey` without `@`, `declared_at: UtcDatetime`; all four required |
| `Transcript` (`tsc`) | entity | `source` (our_stt, agent_export, agent_telemetry, observer, script, human_reference), `provider?`, `model?` (MachineKey), `detected_language?: ObservedLanguageTag`, `detected_language_raw?: VerbatimText`, `language_confidence?` (strict float in [0,1]), `declared_language?: LanguageDeclaration`, `timeline_origin?`, `privacy: PrivacyMarker`. No `turns` field. `declared_language` only on `agent_export`, `agent_telemetry`, `observer`, `human_reference` and only when `detected_language` and `detected_language_raw` are both None; error at `declared_language`. `provider_call_ref?: ExternalRef` (revision 11, N28 resolved: a provider's request identifier is a foreign identifier, ADR 0012 Decision 6; not yet tested, see 22.9) |
| `check_transcript(transcript, turns, exchanges=None) -> None` | function (P) | `ValueError`: a turn of another transcript (names the turn's transcript), a repeated `seq` (names it), offsets (any turn or segment bound) without `timeline_origin`, a `timeline_origin` without offsets (both messages contain `timeline_origin`), and, only when `exchanges` is given, a repeated exchange index (names it) or a turn whose `exchange_index` is not in the list (names it). `exchanges=None` skips the exchange checks |

No-echo (revision 11, N31 resolved for these models): `Transcript`, `Turn`, `Segment`, `ToolCallEvent` and `IngestWarning` set `hide_input_in_errors=True`. They hold source text or raw payloads that are personal data on a human call (ADR 0007 Decision 8 (b); ADR 0016 Decision 7 names text and tool-argument payloads). Messages keep naming the field and the rule. (rev 12, O4 decided by the owner, option A) `ConfigSnapshot` and `Evaluator` hide their input too, and every model embedding them (P1-14 bundles) hides; the batch 2 `Evaluator` code gains `hide_input_in_errors=True`. (rev 13, N41 decided by the owner, option A) `ScriptedTurn`, `ExpectedToolCall`, `Persona`, `MetricDefinition`, `MetricBinding` and `Rubric` hide their input too, also when validated on their own; the set stays closed under embedding (today only `Evaluator` and, at P1-12, `ConfigSnapshot` embed them, and both hide). The batch 2 code of these six gains `hide_input_in_errors=True`. `hide_input_in_errors` removes the `input_value` part only; messages that quote a rejected value are finding N42 (22.12).

**`tracer_evalschema.events` (P1-10c)**

| Name | Kind | Pinned contract |
|---|---|---|
| `ToolCallEvent` (`tcl`) | entity | `name: MachineKey`, `arguments_raw?: VerbatimText`, `arguments?` (I-JSON object), `arguments_complete` (required strict bool, no default), `result_raw?: VerbatimText`, `result_complete?` (strict bool or None), `status` (completed, cancelled, unknown), `result_error?` (bool or None, default None), `seq` (strict int >= 0), `offset_ms?`, `timeline_origin?`, `source` (rev 12, O2 decided by the owner, option A: the closed set agent_export, agent_telemetry, observer; required; emitted as `enum`), `external_call_id?: VerbatimText` (opaque, stored verbatim: a v4 UUID, text with spaces and `run_...` text are kept). Rules: `arguments` present implies `arguments_complete`; `arguments_complete=true` with an `arguments_raw` that is not a whole JSON object is rejected (this is how a truncated payload "never yields arguments_complete", P, N28); `result_complete` needs `result_raw`; `timeline_origin` iff `offset_ms`. Errors at `arguments`, `arguments_complete`, `result_complete`, `timeline_origin`. No privacy marker |
| `StageSpan` (`spn`) | entity | `stage` (stt, llm, tts, judge, sim_caller_llm, sim_caller_tts, realtime, other), `stage_raw?: MachineKey` iff `stage=other` (error at `stage_raw`), `provider?`, `model?`, `start?: UtcDatetime`, `duration_ms?`, `ttfb_ms?`, `exchange_index?`, `span_ref: ExternalRef` (required, P; N28 resolved in revision 11: ADR 0012 Decision 2 lists it without `?`, and Decision 6 makes trace and span IDs `ExternalRef`s) |
| `EvidenceRef` | value object | `kind` (turn, exchange, tool_call, span, artifact_range), `ref_id` typed as the union `TurnId, ExchangeId, ToolCallEventId, StageSpanId, ArtifactId` (not the base `TypedId`: the existing walk `test_every_id_reference_field_has_registered_prefix` reads `.prefix` of every leaf), `start_ms?`, `end_ms?` (`end_ms >= start_ms`). The ID kind must match `kind` (error at `ref_id`) |
| `IngestWarning` | value object | `code: MachineKey`, `path?: VerbatimText`, `raw?: VerbatimText`, `message: VerbatimText` (non-empty) |
| `check_tool_call_seqs(events) -> None` | function (P) | `ValueError` naming a repeated `seq` among the events of one run (the caller passes the events of one RunBundle, N22) |
| `check_evidence_resolves(refs, records) -> None` | function (P) | `ValueError` naming the `ref_id` that is not the `id` of any supplied record |

**`tracer_evalschema.results` (P1-11)**

| Name | Kind | Pinned contract |
|---|---|---|
| `ResultError` | value object (P, name) | `code: MachineKey`, `message: VerbatimText` (non-empty) |
| `ScorerInfo` | value object | `implementation_ref: MachineKey`, `version: MachineKey`, both required; the version is stored verbatim (no normalization) |
| `JudgeTurnVerdict` | value object (P, name) | `turn_seq` (int >= 0), `verdict` (yes, no, undetermined), `p_yes?` (strict float in [0,1]), `reported_model?: VerbatimText` (non-empty; P-62, decided by the owner: accepted) |
| `JudgeInfo` | value object | `model_ref: ModelRef`, `prompt_sha256` (64 lowercase hex), `params` (I-JSON object, required), `per_turn: list[JudgeTurnVerdict]` (default `[]`), `confidence?` (strict float in [0,1]), `reported_model?: VerbatimText` (non-empty, never filled from `model_ref`; P-62, decided by the owner: accepted; a plain `VerbatimText` without a stored flag, N32) |
| `PerTurnSummary` | value object | `share?: DecimalString`, `counts: PerTurnCounts`, `counted_turns`, `total_turns` (>= 1). Checked on load: `counted_turns = passed + failed`, `total_turns` = sum of the four counts, `share` equals `metrics.per_turn_share(counts)`; a missing share while turns were counted is rejected |
| `MetricResult` (`res`) | entity | `metric: MetricDefinitionRef` (P, N33), `status` (scored, not_applicable, error), `value` (bool, int, finite float or text, or None; integers within +-(2**53 - 1)), `passed?: bool`, `na_reason?` (the five), `missing_kinds: list[InputKind]` (default `[]`), `error?: ResultError`, `per_turn?: PerTurnSummary`, `scorer?: ScorerInfo`, `judge?: JudgeInfo`, `language_source?` (observed, declared), `declared_language_transcript_ids: list[TranscriptId]` (default `[]`), `evidence_refs: list[EvidenceRef]` (default `[]`). No `run_id`, no `binding_id` (N22, N33). Rules on the result alone (errors at the named field): scored has no `na_reason`, `error` or `missing_kinds`, and exactly one of `value` and `per_turn` (a scored `per_turn` has a counted turn); only a scored result has `value` or `passed`; not_applicable needs `na_reason`, has no `error`, `scorer` or `judge`, `missing_kinds` non-empty iff `na_reason=missing_input`, and `per_turn` only for the every-turn-not-applicable case (counted 0, undetermined 0); error needs `error`, has no `na_reason` or `missing_kinds`, and `per_turn` only with counted 0, undetermined > 0 and `error.code=judge_undetermined`; `declared_language_transcript_ids` non-empty iff `language_source=declared`, unique and sorted by string form; `language_source` is None when `na_reason` is `missing_input` or `agent_channel_unknown` |
| `NA_REASON_PRECEDENCE` | tuple | the five reasons in the order of ADR 0004 Decision 4 |
| `strongest_na_reason(reasons) -> str` | function | the first of the precedence present in `reasons`; `ValueError` for none or an unknown reason |
| `missing_inputs(definition, run) -> list[str]` | function | `definition.requires` minus `run.available_inputs`, in the order of `requires`. Reads nothing else (not the tier, not `detected_capabilities`); pure; parameters named `definition`, `run` |
| `EffectiveLanguage` | NamedTuple | `(language, source)` with source observed or declared |
| `effective_language(turn, transcript, definition) -> EffectiveLanguage or None` | function | the six steps of section 21.2, `turn` may be None (no turn read: steps 3 to 6). `und` compares case-insensitively. Parameters named `turn`, `transcript`, `definition` |
| `LanguageCheck` | NamedTuple | `(na_reason, language_source, declared_language_transcript_ids)` |
| `check_language(definition, turns, transcripts) -> LanguageCheck` | function (P) | The steps of ADR 0004 Decision 6e on all given turns together (for a `per_turn_share` metric the caller passes one turn). `language_independent`: `(None, None, [])`. Otherwise each turn is read with the transcript named by `Turn.transcript_id` (`ValueError` if it is not supplied); with no turn the transcripts supply the language (steps 3 to 6). Reason: any turn without an effective language gives `language_not_observed`; else any effective primary language not in `languages` (compared by primary language) gives `unsupported_language`; else, for a script-sensitive definition, an observed tag with a script subtag outside the language's allowed scripts of `languages.json` gives `unsupported_script`. `language_source`: `declared` if any turn used a declaration, else `observed` if any turn has an effective language, else None; this is also reported with `language_not_observed` when some other turn had a language. The IDs are the distinct transcripts whose declaration was used, sorted by string form. With no turn and no transcript a language-dependent definition gives `("language_not_observed", None, [])` (ADR 0004 Decision 6d: no tag, so no observed language; N29, resolved in revision 11). Parameters named `definition`, `turns`, `transcripts` |
| `Applicability` | NamedTuple | `(na_reason, missing_kinds, language_source, declared_language_transcript_ids)` |
| `applicability(definition, run, *, turns=(), transcripts=()) -> Applicability` | function (P) | `missing_inputs` first: if non-empty, `missing_input` with the kinds, except that when the only missing kind is `agent_audio`, `audio` is available and `agent_audio_available(run.channel_maps)` is false the reason is `agent_channel_unknown` with no kinds (N23, decided by the owner in revision 11; every other combination gives `missing_input` listing all missing kinds, worked examples in ADR 0004 Decision 5a). Then `check_language`. `language_source` and the IDs are empty when a missing-input reason is reported |
| `check_result_against_definition(result, definition) -> None` | function | `ValueError` naming the result ID. Rules: same `{id, version}`; a scored value fits `value_type` (boolean: bool; rating: int within the scale; enum: one of the options; numeric: int or float, never a bool); a `per_turn_share` definition scores through `per_turn` and never `value`, any other aggregation has no `per_turn`; `passed` equals the pass rule applied to the value (to the `Decimal` share for `per_turn_share`) and is None without a pass rule; `scorer` is required for scored and errored results of `deterministic`, `audio` and `latency` definitions and forbidden otherwise, with `implementation_ref` equal to the definition's (case-sensitive, message names both); `judge` is required for a scored `llm_judge` result, optional for an errored one, and forbidden for every other result (P, N37); `language_source` is None for a `language_independent` definition, never `declared` for a script-sensitive one, and, for the list form, required for scored and errored results and for `unsupported_language` and `unsupported_script` (free for `language_not_observed` here only because this check has no turns; the record itself follows ADR 0004 Decision 6g, which `check_language` produces and the P1-14 bundle check can verify, N29). Binding parameters are not an input (E6): the parameters are named `result`, `definition` |
| `render_per_turn_share(result) -> str or None` | function | `turn share {share} (turn coverage {counted}/{total} turns)`, the share with two decimals half-even; None unless the result is scored and has a share |

**`tracer_evalschema.verdict` (P1-22)**

| Name | Kind | Pinned contract |
|---|---|---|
