The F1 merge rule (N64) is P1-29 mapping code, not schema code.

### 23.7 Revision 15: needs owner decision. Revision 16: all ten decided by the owner

Each item states the question, the trade-off and a recommendation, as asked. **Revision 16 (2026-10-23):** the owner decided each in chat, one at a time (decision log "Batch 4 follow-ups"); the decision is appended to each item, and the tests are in 23.6 (groups B, D, H, I, J and M to T).

1. **N44. Shape of `UsageRecord.quantities`.** Question: must `quantities` be non-empty with at most one quantity per unit (qa's reading)? Trade-off: an empty list or two quantities of one unit could carry a source's raw shape, but an empty list has no meaning that "no usage record" does not already have, and two quantities of one unit are either a double count or need a summing rule nobody stated. Recommendation: accept qa's reading (non-empty, one per unit, error at `quantities`). Decide before P1-23. **Decided (revision 16): option A**, non-empty, exactly one quantity per unit; importers merge or map to distinct units; the type never sums repeats (follow-up N64).
2. **N45. Seconds and zero quantities.** (a) Are negative seconds rejected (qa's reading)? A negative billable duration is not a usage; recommendation: reject, like negative token counts. (b) What do increment and minimum do to a zero quantity (for example `cache_read_input_tokens: 0` or `audio_input_seconds: "0"`)? Options: (A) a zero quantity costs exactly 0 and the minimum is not applied, because nothing was consumed in that unit and a per-request minimum belongs to the `requests` unit; (B) the minimum applies to every quantity, which charges a minimum for an unused bucket; (C) a zero quantity is not_available. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-23 (a) and P1-25 (b). **Decided (revision 16):** (a) rejected, and no quantity is negative in any unit; (b) option A, a zero quantity costs exactly 0 and no minimum applies (follow-up N63).
3. **N47 remainder. Enforcing P-24.** P-24 says an unchanged entry keeps its identifier across catalog versions and a changed one gets a new identifier, but no function or test enforces it. Options: (A) a pure `pricing.check_catalog_succession(previous, next)` that rejects an identifier reused with different content and identical content under a new identifier, with test `test_entry_identifier_stable_across_catalog_versions` (P1-24); (B) a documented convention only. Trade-off: A adds a function and a check at catalog publication; B lets "same price as before" reports compare identifiers that may not mean the same content. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 16): option A**, `pricing.check_catalog_succession(previous, next)` with test `test_entry_identifier_stable_across_catalog_versions`; enforcement at a publish step later (follow-up N65).
4. **N49 remainder. `applies_when` edge cases.** (a) An empty list `[]` versus absent: recommendation: reject `[]` (absent already means "no condition"; two spellings would give two digests for one entry). (b) A repeated kind (two `region` conditions, or two `context_tokens_gt`): recommendation: reject, since one kind once is enough to express every tier (a band is `context_tokens_gt` plus `context_tokens_le`). (c) Conjunction: recommendation: all conditions must hold, so one known-false condition removes the entry even when another is unknown; this is deduction, not a guess. Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 16): accepted** (a), (b) and (c) as recommended.
5. **N50 remainder. Provenance details.** (a) URL syntax beyond credentials: recommendation: an absolute `http`/`https` URI with a host, ASCII, no white space; a fragment allowed (price pages use anchors), with the credential patterns still searched in it. Alternative: no syntax rule, which accepts any text that passes the credential checks. (b) `provenance.kind`: recommendation: keep it an open `MachineKey` (it names a kind of external document, ADR 0003 Decision 2). (c) `note` on a user-supplied price: recommendation: required when `price_source=user_supplied`, because ADR 0014 Decision 11 relies on it to state the source, and for a converted price the rate and date; the cost is one more required field for user prices. Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 16): accepted** (a), (b) and (c) as recommended (follow-up N66).
6. **N51 remainder. Cost record links.** (a) May an available reported record have empty `usage_record_ids` (a source that reports cost without usage)? Trade-off: requiring usage blocks recording such a cost at all (it cannot become a `UsageRecord` without quantities), while allowing it means P-54 cannot see a double count with a computed record for the same call. Recommendation: allow it for basis `reported` only, treat it as sharing no usage in summaries, and state the residual risk in `SCHEMA_POLICY.md`; importers must link usage whenever the source gives it. (b) Duplicates in `usage_record_ids`: recommendation: reject. (c) A `reported_raw.currency_raw` that the importer would have normalized to USD on an `unsupported_currency` record (or a non-USD label on an `amount_unparseable` record): recommendation: reject, since such a record contradicts Decision 12c. Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 16): accepted** (a), (b) and (c) as recommended (follow-up N67).
7. **N53 remainder. Summary details.** (a) A record without usage records is never "covered" (qa's reading; vacuous coverage would let an unrelated included record cover it): recommendation: accept. (b) `included` and `excluded` keep the input order and `check_cost_summary` does not check order: recommendation: accept (deterministic, and the caller controls it). Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 16): accepted** (a) and (b).
8. **N59 remainder.** (a) A payload label that is USD with a cited contract that states another currency: the owner decided the cost is not_available with the raw values kept, but no reason fits (`unsupported_currency` would claim USD is unsupported). Options: (A) a new reason `currency_conflict` with `currency_source=payload` and an ingest warning `currency_contract_conflict`; (B) `unsupported_currency` with the contract's code. Recommendation: A, and record the `currency_contract_conflict` warning on every disagreement, including the decided non-USD-payload case. (b) The `raw` and `path` of the `unsupported_currency` warning: recommendation: `raw` is the label as sent; `path` is the source field path, passed to `import_reported_cost` as an optional `source_path` argument by the mapping (P1-29, P1-30) and absent otherwise. Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 16):** (a) option A, `currency_conflict` and the warning `currency_contract_conflict` naming both labels (whether it is also recorded for a non-USD payload label: follow-up N68); (b) as recommended (follow-up N69).
9. **N61. Name and type of the price entry identifier.** After N47 the identifier is no longer a typed `pce_` ID, and ADR 0001 I12 requires every `*_id` field to be a typed ID unless it is on the opaque allowlist. Options: (A) `entry_id: MachineKey` (and `PricingSnapshot.entry_ids`), assigned by the catalog author, unique within the catalog; both fields join the opaque allowlist and I12's principle is widened to keys assigned by the author of a versioned record; (B) the same key named `entry_key` (`PricingSnapshot.entry_keys`), which is not a `*_id` field, so I12 and its two walks are unchanged; (C) a tracer-minted UUIDv7 with a prefix in a second registry for identified value objects, which keeps typed IDs but brings `pce` back in another table and adds a second registry concept. Trade-off: A keeps the owner's word "entry_id" but bends a principle the gate checks; B states that the value is a catalog-scoped key, not a global identity; C is the heaviest and contradicts the removal. Recommendation: B. Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 16): option B**, `entry_key: MachineKey`, `PricingSnapshot.entry_keys` scoped by the catalog reference; ADR 0001 I12 and its two walks unchanged.
10. **N62. N50 against N60.** ADR 0007 Decision 8 (a) requires every model whose validation reaches a credential-checked field to hide its input, closed under embedding; N50 makes `PriceProvenance.url` such a field; N60 says `PricingCatalog` and `PriceEntry` do not hide. Options: (A) `PriceProvenance`, `PriceEntry` and `PricingCatalog` hide; a rejected price-page URL is then never echoed, but the currency message inside a catalog names the rule and not the rejected code or its ISO name (`test_price_entry_rejects_non_usd` changes, 23.6 group I item 3); (B) keep N60 for these two models and accept that pydantic's default error text shows the rejected URL, including a presigned signature, which is the leak Decision 8 (a) exists to stop; (C) hide only `PriceProvenance`, which does not work, because the outermost model being validated governs. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 16): option A**, all three hide; `test_price_entry_rejects_non_usd` asserts the rule message, not the code.

### 23.8 Revision 16: new findings for the owner. Revision 17: all seven decided (auto-approved)

Raised by schema-architect while applying the revision 16 decisions (2026-10-23). Each states the question, the trade-off and a recommendation. Until it is decided, no test asserts either answer (23.6 names the cases left open), and the implementation does not choose one beyond what the decided rules already force. Gate item 11 treats them like [Proposed] items.

**Revision 17 (2026-10-23):** under the owner's standing rule of 2026-10-23 (design decisions are auto-approved on the recommended option and logged), the lead recorded a decision for each of N63 to N69 in the decision log ("AUTO-APPROVED ... N63-N69"); those entries are binding and are appended to each item below. Where a logged decision differs from the architect's revision 16 recommendation, the item says so: N66 (b) (loopback hosts rejected on the record, not only in exports), N68 (c) (the normalization warning is also recorded in a conflict) and N69 (a) (two path arguments instead of one `source_path`). The tests are in 23.6 groups U to Z; the details fixed while applying them are D16 to D19 (23.6); the new findings they raise are N70 to N76 in section 23.9.

1. **N63. A zero quantity without a usable price (from N45 (b)).** The owner decided that a zero quantity costs 0 with no minimum. Open: does it still need a resolvable price with `BillingRules`? Example: `input_tokens: 1000` (priced) and `cache_read_input_tokens: 0` for a model with no cache-read entry. Options: (A) resolve it like any quantity: the existing reasons still apply (`unit_unpriced` here, so the whole record is not_available), and its entry is listed in `entry_keys`; (B) a zero quantity needs no price: it contributes 0 whatever the catalog says, is never unpriced, ambiguous or `billing_rules_missing`, and names no entry. Trade-off: A keeps every computed amount tied to named entries and leaves the reason precedence (Decision 13a) untouched, but an unused bucket the source happens to report makes an otherwise computable cost unavailable; B computes that cost (0 times anything is 0, which is exact, not assumed), but needs further rules: a record of zero quantities only would be `computed` with no entry, which the rule "`entry_keys` non-empty" forbids, and the reason precedence must skip zero quantities. Recommendation: A, because it adds no rule and never presents a cost without a named price; importers that emit unused zero buckets can be fixed in the mapping. Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 17, auto-approved): option A.** A zero quantity is resolved like any other quantity: it needs an effective, unambiguous entry whose `billing` is a `BillingRules` value and a price date, and otherwise contributes its reason to Decision 13a exactly as a non-zero quantity would (`no_price_for_model`, `billing_rules_missing`, `unit_unpriced`, `ambiguous_price`). When priced it contributes exactly 0 with neither increment nor minimum (N45 (b)), and its entry's key is listed in `pricing_snapshot.entry_keys` (zero is a known quantity, not a skipped lookup). Rejected: option B, skipping the lookup for zero (it would hide a missing price). ADR 0014 Decisions 13a, 15. Tests: 23.6 group U.
2. **N64. How an importer may merge repeated quantities (from N44).** The type rejects two quantities of one unit; the owner said importers "merge or name distinct units". Merging is a sum, and because increment and minimum apply per usage record and unit (N55), one summed quantity can cost differently from two separate billable events. Options: (A) an importer sums only when the source, or its cited API contract (as for P-53), states that the quantities are parts of one billable event; if they are separate calls it builds one usage record per call; otherwise it records a mapping gap (ADR 0018) with an IngestWarning and builds no quantity for that unit; (B) importers always sum. Trade-off: B is simple but can change the billed amount and is a guess about billing; A needs a citation or yields a gap. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-29 (the first mapping that builds usage records). **Decided (revision 17, auto-approved): option A.** An importer merges two or more quantities of one unit into one quantity (their exact sum) only when the source payload, or its published API contract cited in the mapping's field-provenance table with URL and `retrieved_at` (as for P-53), states that they are parts of one billable event. Quantities of separate billable events (separate calls the source identifies) go into separate usage records, one per call; that is not a merge. Otherwise the importer records a mapping gap (ADR 0018: an IngestWarning and a gap-register row) and never sums, never keeps one count and drops the other, and never relies on the type to add them (increment and minimum apply per usage record and unit, N55, so a merge can change the bill). Rejected: always merge; never merge. What the mapping builds for the affected call, the warning code and the gap's classification are N76 (23.9). ADR 0014 Decision 9, ADR 0018 Decision 3a. Test: 23.6 group V (P1-29).
3. **N65. What "content" means for `check_catalog_succession` (from N47 remainder).** The function rejects a key reused for different content and identical content under a new key. Open: (a) which fields count as content. Taking every field except `entry_key` means that re-checking a price page (a new `provenance.retrieved_at`) or closing an open range when the next price arrives (setting `effective_to`, the usual succession) forces a new key, so "same price as before" rarely holds; (b) does adding an alias change the content; (c) what the function requires of the two catalogs: the same `id`, and `next.version` later than (or exactly one more than) `previous.version`. Recommendation: content is every field except `entry_key`, `provenance` and `effective_to` (aliases included, since they change what the entry prices); provenance stays covered by the catalog digest (D4); require the same `id` and a later `version` (`ValueError` otherwise), not necessarily the next one. Trade-off: a key then survives a change of source document and the closing of its range, which is what P-24 is for, but two versions can hold one key with different `effective_to`. Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 17, auto-approved): as recommended.** The content compared by `check_catalog_succession` is every `PriceEntry` field except `entry_key`, `provenance` and `effective_to`, that is `provider`, `model`, `aliases`, `unit`, `unit_quantity`, `price`, `billing`, `effective_from`, `applies_when` and `price_source` (aliases included, because they change what the entry prices); provenance stays covered by the catalog digest (D4). The two catalogs must have the same `id` and `next.version` must be later than `previous.version`, not necessarily the next integer; otherwise `ValueError` before any entry is compared (D18). Rejected: comparing every field (re-checking a price page or closing a range would force a new key). Whether the order of `applies_when` and `aliases` counts is N72 (23.9). ADR 0014 Decision 10a, ADR 0009 Decision 5. Tests: 23.6 group W.
4. **N66. Price provenance follow-ups (from N50 remainder).** (a) The URL may carry a fragment. The parameter-name check of ADR 0007 Decision 6b runs on the query only, so `#access_token=abc123` passes unless the value matches a Decision 5a pattern (the OAuth implicit flow puts tokens there). Recommendation: also split the fragment at `&` and `;` and check `name=value` pairs against the 25 names; cost: a page anchor named like a credential (`#token`) without `=` stays allowed, one with `=` is rejected. (b) Loopback and private hosts are accepted in `provenance.url` today. Recommendation: accept them in records and reject loopback hosts in shared and benchmark exports, as for artifacts (ADR 0007 Decision 6d, P1-14); private ranges stay accepted as for artifacts. (c) Is an empty or white-space-only `note` a note? Recommendation: reject it at `provenance`/`note` (a required note that says nothing defeats the rule); cost: one more rule. Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 17, auto-approved; the log calls it the fewest-assumption reading):** (a) credential parameter names are checked inside the fragment of `provenance.url`: the fragment is split at `&` and `;` and each name goes through the 6b steps (percent-decode once, case-fold, `-` equals `_`) against the 25 names, as in the query; whether a piece without `=` (`#token`) is a name is N70 (23.9). (b) **Differs from the revision 16 recommendation:** a loopback host, by the D1 definition of ADR 0007 Decision 6d (the same function `artifact.is_loopback_uri`), is rejected in `provenance.url` on the record itself, at `provenance`/`url`, because a price source is a public page; private, link-local and unspecified addresses and other non-loopback names stay accepted, as D1 lists them. (c) A present `note` that is empty or white space only is rejected at `provenance`/`note`, for every entry (D17); which characters count as white space is N71 (23.9). ADR 0007 Decision 6e, ADR 0014 Decision 11. Tests: 23.6 group X.
5. **N67. `usage_record_ids` on not_available records (from N51 remainder (a)).** The owner allowed an empty list "only for reported", which among available records means basis `reported`. Not_available records have no basis: `compute_cost` gives `no_usage` and `usage_not_reported` with an empty list (pinned, N54), and `import_reported_cost` gives `unsupported_currency`, `currency_not_reported`, `amount_unparseable` and `currency_conflict` from the caller's list, which may be empty when the source names no usage. Questions: (a) must a `no_usage` or `usage_not_reported` record have an empty list (a non-empty one would contradict its reason)? (b) must the other computed reasons (`no_price_for_model`, `unit_unpriced`, `billing_rules_missing`, `ambiguous_price`) have a non-empty list? (c) may the imported not_available records have an empty one? Recommendation: (a) yes, (b) yes, (c) yes, the same as an available reported record (they come from the same reported source). Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 17, auto-approved): (a), (b) and (c) yes, as recommended.** By `na_reason`, at `usage_record_ids`: `no_usage` and `usage_not_reported` need an empty list; `no_price_for_model`, `unit_unpriced`, `billing_rules_missing` and `ambiguous_price` need a non-empty one; `unsupported_currency`, `currency_not_reported`, `amount_unparseable` and `currency_conflict` (the imported reasons) may have either. Rejected: the literal "only reported" rule, which would block `compute_cost`'s own outputs. ADR 0014 Decision 12. Tests: 23.6 group Y.
6. **N68. `currency_conflict` follow-ups (from N59 remainder (a)).** (a) The record keeps only the payload label in `reported_raw.currency_raw`; the contract's currency is named only in the `currency_contract_conflict` warning on the Run. Options: no new field, because the contract currency is a citation that the mapping's field-provenance table already holds (P-51), or a new optional `ReportedCostRaw.contract_currency_raw`. Recommendation: no new field. (b) Is `currency_contract_conflict` also recorded when the payload label is not USD and disagrees with a cited contract (for example `EUR` against a USD contract, or `EUR` against `INR`), which the owner decided is `unsupported_currency`? The revision 15 recommendation said "on every disagreement"; the decision text names only the USD-label case. Recommendation: yes, on every disagreement after the P-52 normalization, so a reader always sees a contract conflict. (c) In the conflict case, does a USD label spelled `usd` or ` USD ` also record `currency_label_normalized`? That warning so far means "processed as USD", which a conflict record is not. Recommendation: no; the conflict warning states both values. Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 17, auto-approved):** (a) as recommended, no new field: the contract's currency is not stored on the record; the `currency_contract_conflict` warning names both labels. (b) as recommended, yes: the conflict warning is recorded on every disagreement between a payload label and a cited contract after the P-52 normalization (D15), also when the payload label is not USD; the reason then stays `unsupported_currency` with `currency_source=payload` (the payload wins). (c) **Differs from the revision 16 recommendation:** a USD label spelled other than `USD` (`usd`, ` USD `) in a conflict also records `currency_label_normalized`: two warnings, two facts (the label was normalized to USD; it conflicts with the contract). `currency_label_normalized` therefore means "the label was normalized to USD", not "the cost was processed as USD". How a non-USD label is compared with a contract that states a non-USD code is N73, the order of several warnings N75 (23.9). ADR 0014 Decisions 12a, 12c. Tests: 23.6 group Z.
7. **N69. `source_path` follow-ups (from N59 remainder (b)).** (a) The currency label and the amount are different source fields, and an `unsupported_currency` warning is about the label while `amount_unparseable` and `decimal_normalized` are about the amount, so one `source_path` cannot locate both. Options: `source_path` locates the cost object (the parent of both fields) in the mapping's path form, or two arguments (`currency_path`, `amount_path`). Recommendation: one `source_path` for the cost object, as decided, with each mapping's field-provenance table naming the two child fields. (b) Do the other importer warnings (`currency_label_normalized`, `decimal_normalized`, `amount_unparseable`, `currency_not_reported`, `currency_contract_conflict`) carry the same `path`? Recommendation: yes, all of them, absent when the argument is absent. (c) When the currency comes from a cited contract, no label was sent, so what is the `unsupported_currency` warning's `raw`? Recommendation: absent (`raw` is a source value verbatim, ADR 0012 Decision 7); the message names the contract's code, and `reported_raw.currency_raw` keeps it as written in the contract. Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 17, auto-approved):** (a) **differs from the revision 16 recommendation:** `import_reported_cost` takes two optional keyword arguments, `currency_source_path=None` and `amount_source_path=None`, each the location of that field in the source in the mapping's path form; the revision 16 keyword `source_path` is withdrawn (D19). (b) every importer warning carries the path of the field it is about, as passed, absent when that argument is not passed, never reconstructed: `currency_label_normalized`, `unsupported_currency`, `currency_not_reported` and `currency_contract_conflict` carry `currency_source_path`; `decimal_normalized` and `amount_unparseable` carry `amount_source_path`. (c) as recommended: when the currency comes from a cited contract, the `unsupported_currency` warning's `raw` is absent (nothing was sent); its message names the contract's code. Rejected: one shared `source_path`. The `raw` of `currency_contract_conflict` is D16; the path of a currency warning when the payload sent no label is N74 (23.9). ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 5, ADR 0012 Decision 7. Tests: 23.6 group Z.

Staff-reviewer note (not an owner item): two revision 16 rules cannot be stated in JSON Schema 2020-12, "the kinds in `applies_when` are distinct" (N49) and "a stored currency label agrees with its reason after `normalize_currency_label`" (N51); like N44's "one quantity per unit", they join the code-only rules whose handling N12 and N38 leave to staff-reviewer. Revision 17 adds three more code-only rules of the same kind: the credential names in a `provenance.url` fragment and its loopback host (like every URL credential rule, ADR 0007 Decision 6b) and "a present `note` is not white space only" (N66); the `usage_record_ids` rules of N67 can be stated with `if`/`then` and are emitted as such if staff-reviewer's N12/N38 ruling keeps `if`/`then` emission.

### 23.9 Revision 17: new findings raised while applying N63 to N69. Revision 18: all seven decided (auto-approved on the recommendation)

Raised by schema-architect (2026-10-23). Until each is decided, no test asserts either answer (23.6 groups U to Z name the cases left open), and the implementation does not choose one beyond what the decided rules force. Gate item 11 treats them like [Proposed] items. **Revision 18 (2026-10-23):** the lead logged a binding decision for each, the recommended option in every case (decision log "AUTO-APPROVED api-surface 23.9 findings N70-N76"); the decision is appended to each item. qa has since asserted the decided cases in the batch 4 tests (the "Not asserted (N7x)" sentences of groups U to Z are superseded; 23.10).

1. **N70. A fragment piece without `=` in `provenance.url` (from N66 (a)).** In the query a name is "the text before the first `=`", so `?token` is the name `token` and is rejected. Applied to the fragment, `#token`, `#key` or `#signature` (plain page anchors) would be rejected too; the revision 16 recommendation had allowed them and checked only `name=value` pairs. Options: (A) the query rule unchanged: every piece is a name, with or without `=`; (B) only pieces that contain `=` are checked. Trade-off: A is the same rule in every URL part, which the decision text asks for ("consistent with anywhere in the URL"), and costs a rejected price page whose anchor is exactly one of the 25 names (rare; the anchor can be dropped from the stored URL); B keeps such anchors but makes the fragment rule differ from the query rule. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 18, auto-approved): option A.** Every piece is a name; `#token` and `#a=1&key` rejected, `#tokens=1`, `#token-pricing`, `#access_token_ttl=5` accepted (qa finding 2). ADR 0007 Decision 6e.
2. **N71. What counts as white space in an empty `note` (from N66 (c)).** Options: (A) ASCII white space only (the six characters of D5, as for currency labels); (B) Unicode White_Space (`str.isspace()`, as the importer's language-label trimming of E4); (C) B plus invisible format characters (U+200B, U+FEFF, U+2060). Trade-off: A lets a note of U+00A0 or U+3000 through although it says nothing; B rejects those and stays a standard definition; C also rejects a note made only of invisible characters, but needs a list of characters that is not a Unicode property. Recommendation: B, and record invisible-only notes as a known limitation (`contains_bidi_controls` and the bidi rules already flag some of them). Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 18, auto-approved): option B** (`str.isspace()`); U+200B and U+FEFF are not white space, a known limitation, not asserted (qa finding 3). ADR 0007 Decision 6e, ADR 0014 Decision 11.
3. **N72. Order of `applies_when` and `aliases` in the succession content (from N65).** A key reused with the same conditions or aliases in another order: same content or not? The catalog's overlap key already compares `applies_when` as a set (N49), but the catalog digest sees the list order. Options: (A) compare both as sets (order carries no meaning); (B) compare the lists as written. Trade-off: A keeps a key when an author only reorders; B forces a new key for a change that alters nothing priced. Recommendation: A. Related, not decided anywhere: whether `aliases` may repeat a value or contain the entry's own `model`; recommendation: reject both at `aliases` (each is a second spelling of one match and would make set comparison hide a difference). Decide before P1-24. **Decided (revision 18, auto-approved): option A**, and both alias rules as recommended (rejected at `aliases`). ADR 0014 Decisions 10a, 11; ADR 0009 Decision 5.
4. **N73. Agreement of a non-USD payload label with a contract (from N68 (b), D15).** D15 says a label and a cited contract agree when the label normalizes (P-52) to the contract's currency, but `normalize_currency_label` returns only USD or nothing, so read literally `"EUR"` against a contract stating `EUR` disagrees and records `currency_contract_conflict`. Options: (A) agree iff the label normalizes to USD and the contract states USD, or the label equals the contract's code exactly as written; otherwise disagree (`" eur "` against `EUR` then records a conflict); (B) fold ASCII case and trim the six ASCII white-space characters for every label before comparing (no conflict for `" eur "` against `EUR`); (C) the literal reading. Trade-off: A never claims agreement the importer did not establish and keeps P-52 a USD-only rule; B is friendlier but extends P-52's normalization to codes the record cannot use anyway; C records a conflict where the source and its contract agree. In every option the cost stays `unsupported_currency` with the label kept. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 18, auto-approved): option A**, exact string equality with the contract code (qa finding 5: `"EUR"` against an `EUR` contract gives only `unsupported_currency`; `" eur "` against `EUR` conflicts). ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 2.
5. **N74. The `path` of a currency warning when the payload sent no label (from N69 (b)).** `unsupported_currency` from a cited contract, and `currency_not_reported` with `currency_raw=None`, are about a currency field that is absent from the payload. Options: (A) the warning carries `currency_source_path` whenever it is passed (the importer does not judge; the mapping passes it only where its field-provenance table names a currency field for that source); (B) the importer drops the path when no label was sent. Trade-off: A is the decided rule read literally ("absent when not passed, never reconstructed") and lets a reader find where the label was expected; B second-guesses the caller. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 18, auto-approved): option A** (qa finding 6). ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 3.
6. **N75. The order of several importer warnings (from N68).** Revision 17 makes two warnings possible for one import (`currency_label_normalized` with `currency_contract_conflict`; `unsupported_currency` with `currency_contract_conflict`; `currency_label_normalized` with `decimal_normalized`). Options: (A) the order of the Decision 12c steps: label normalization, then the contract conflict, then the outcome warning (`unsupported_currency` or `currency_not_reported`), then the amount warnings; (B) no defined order (readers and tests compare as a multiset). Trade-off: A gives a deterministic `Run.ingest_warnings` order and byte-identical runs; B leaves a degree of freedom that changes the Run's digest across implementations. Recommendation: A; until decided the tests compare multisets. Decide before P1-25. **Decided (revision 18, auto-approved): option A**; the tests keep comparing multisets, the implementation emits the step order. ADR 0014 Decision 12c.
7. **N76. What a mapping builds when repeated quantities cannot be merged (from N64).** The decision says "record a mapping gap" but not what happens to the call's usage. Options: (A) build no `UsageRecord` for that call at all (not even with its other units), record an IngestWarning (proposed `code` `usage_quantities_not_merged`, `path` the repeated field, `raw` absent) and a gap-register row classified **blocking**, because cost information is lost (ADR 0018 Decision 3); (B) build the record without the repeated unit. Trade-off: B leaves a record that `compute_cost` would price as an available partial cost of the call, which Decision 13 forbids ("a partial sum is never reported"); A loses the call's other units too, and because no cost record says "usage lost", the call can disappear from a summary silently, which is exactly why the gap must be blocking (the gate then fails until the source or a cited contract settles the billing). Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-29. **Decided (revision 18, auto-approved): option A.** ADR 0018 Decision 3a; group V item 1 (iii) is rewritten accordingly.

### 23.10 Revision 18: N70 to N76 and the qa findings decided; batch 4 implemented (schema-architect, 2026-10-23)

**Decisions applied.** N70 to N76 (23.9) were auto-approved on the recommended option under the owner's standing rule of 2026-10-23 and logged (decision log "AUTO-APPROVED api-surface 23.9 findings N70-N76"); each item of 23.9 is marked. The nine qa findings on the batch 4 tests were auto-approved and logged as well (decision log "AUTO-APPROVED qa findings 1-9"). Both are binding; ADRs 0001, 0007, 0009, 0014 and 0018 and the ADR index record them.

**The qa findings 1 to 9: Decided (revision 18, auto-approved).**

| # | Finding | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | D13 claimed `validate_new_prefix("pce")` rejects `pce` as one edit from `per`; the distance is 2 | `pce` is simply unreserved; `validate_new_prefix("pce")` returns `None` | 23.6 D13 (corrected), ADR 0001 Decision 4 |
| 2 | The fragment rule needs examples | `#token` and `#a=1&key` rejected; `#tokens=1`, `#token-pricing`, `#access_token_ttl=5` accepted | ADR 0007 Decision 6e |
| 3 | White space of a note | `str.isspace()` as decided (N71); U+200B and U+FEFF are not white space (not asserted) | ADR 0007 Decision 6e, ADR 0014 Decision 11 |
| 4 | Message fragments | `overlaps_reported` misuse contains "overlap"; URL credential errors contain the part word (`userinfo`, `query`, `path`, `fragment`); loopback errors "loopback host" | ADR 0007 Decision 6e; implemented |
| 5 | N73 | exact string equality with the contract code is agreement: `"EUR"` against an `EUR` contract gives only `unsupported_currency`; `" eur "` against `EUR` conflicts | ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 2 |
| 6 | N74 | `currency_not_reported` with `currency_raw=None` carries `currency_source_path` when passed; so does `unsupported_currency` from a contract with no label | ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 3 |
| 7 | `amount_unparseable` with label `usd` | both `amount_unparseable` and `currency_label_normalized` are recorded | ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 4 |
| 8 | `unsupported_currency` with an empty or white-space `currency_raw` | rejected at `ReportedCostRaw` (a present `currency_raw` is never empty or ASCII white space only, D6) | ADR 0014 Decision 12b |
| 9 | Amounts | `.5`, `-0.0`, any leading `-` and over-scale amounts are `amount_unparseable` (canonical only, D7) | ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 4 |

**How batch 4 is implemented** (all four Python versions, logs in `docs/phase1/evidence/green/batch4-cost/`):
- `tracer_evalschema.usage` (P1-23): `UsageQuantity` (`count` is a left-to-right union of a strict integer in `[0, 2**53 - 1]` and a non-negative canonical `DecimalString`; a model rule ties the type to the unit, error at `count`), `UsageRecord` (`HidingModel` + `EntityBase`, `use`; `occurred_at: UtcDatetime | None`; `quantities` non-empty with one quantity per unit; `raw: IJsonObject | None`; P-39 at `source`).
- `tracer_evalschema.pricing` (P1-24): `PriceProvenance`, `PriceEntry` (value objects, both `HidingModel`), `BillingRules`, `AppliesWhenCondition`, `PricingCatalog` (`HidingModel` + `VersionedEntity`, `pcat`), `check_catalog_succession`. `PriceEntry` is a value object with `entry_key`; the catalog rules (one `entry_key` per catalog, no overlapping half-open ranges under one key with `applies_when` as a set) are reported at `entries`.
- `tracer_evalschema.cost` (P1-25): `PricingSnapshot`, `CostRounding`, `ReportedCostRaw` (`HidingModel`), `CostRecord` (`HidingModel` + `EntityBase`, `cst`), `CostExclusion`, `CostSummary` (`csum`, does not hide), the NamedTuples `PriceFacts`, `PriceResolution`, `ContractCurrency`, `ImportedCost`, and the functions `resolve_price`, `compute_cost`, `check_pricing_snapshot`, `summarize_costs`, `check_cost_summary`, `normalize_currency_label`, `import_reported_cost`, with the signatures of 23.1 as amended by 23.6 (`occurred_at=`, no `usage_times`, `currency_source_path=`/`amount_source_path=`, no `source_path`).
- `url_rules.check_price_source_url` and `PRICE_SOURCE_URL_PATTERN` (ADR 0007 Decision 6e), sharing the character, scheme, host, parameter-name and pattern steps of the endpoint rules and calling `is_loopback_host_uri`, the function behind `artifact.is_loopback_uri` (one definition; `pricing` uses it through `url_rules` because `artifact` imports `url_rules`).
- `primitives.iso4217_currency_name(code)`: names a code in importer warnings (ADR 0014 Decision 3); `cost` may not import `data_loader` directly (D12's module classification).
- `ids.py`: `pce` and `PriceEntryId` removed (26 prefixes).
- The three modules import only the standard library, pydantic (with pydantic-core, as every module does) and package modules that `test_no_float_in_cost_path` classifies; none names `float` or writes a float literal.

**Public names added beyond 23.1** (not pinned by tests; listed for the schema export and staff-reviewer): `usage.USAGE_UNITS`, `INTEGER_UNITS`, `SECONDS_UNITS`, `DOMAINS`, `USAGE_STAGES`, `USAGE_SOURCES`, `UsageUnit`; `pricing.NONE`, `BILLING_UNKNOWN`, `PRICE_SOURCES`, `CONDITION_KINDS`, `POSITIVE_DECIMAL_PATTERN`, `PriceEntry.applies_when_set()`, `.matches_model()`, `.is_effective_at()`, `AppliesWhenCondition.fact_key()`; `cost.RECORD_STATUSES`, `BASES`, `REPORTERS`, `CURRENCY_SOURCES`, `NA_REASONS`, `EXCLUSION_REASONS`, `COMPLETENESS`, `ROUNDING_SCALE`; `url_rules.check_price_source_url`, `PRICE_SOURCE_URL_PATTERN`; `primitives.iso4217_currency_name`.

**Details fixed while implementing** (each follows from the pinned contract or is a guard against a wrong input; not pinned by tests unless stated):
1. **Exact arithmetic.** `compute_cost` works on a private copy of the caller's `decimal.Context` with `Inexact`, `InvalidOperation`, `DivisionByZero` and `Overflow` trapped, so every intermediate step is exact or raises. Each billable event is the exact fraction `(billed quantity x price, or the minimum x unit_quantity if larger) / unit_quantity`; events are summed per `unit_quantity` and the fractions combined; the total is rounded once, half-even at scale 18, by exact integer division (`divmod`, the remainder compared with half the denominator), and `rounding` is set iff the remainder is not zero. A context too small for an exact result is a `ValueError` naming its precision (N79 below); the result never depends on the context's rounding mode or the thread's context (`test_compute_is_pure_and_needs_an_explicit_context`).
2. **Input guards.** `compute_cost`: a non-`Context` is a `TypeError`; an unknown `domain`, a usage record of another domain ("domain") or one given twice ("usage") is a `ValueError`. `resolve_price` and `compute_cost`: `PriceFacts` values of the wrong type (`batch=1`, a negative `context_tokens`) are a `TypeError` (never a silent mismatch); a naive `occurred_at`/`run_started_at` is a `ValueError` (UTC is never assumed). `import_reported_cost`: a non-text `amount_raw` or `currency_raw` is a `TypeError` (an adapter keeps numbers as text); a cited contract with a blank `currency` is a `ValueError` (N80 below). `summarize_costs` and `check_cost_summary`: a record given twice is a `ValueError` ("record").
3. **Orders.** `unpriced_units` lists the unpriced units in the canonical unit order (`USAGE_UNITS`), whatever the order of quantities or usage records; `entry_keys` lists the distinct entries in first-use order (usage records in input order, then quantities); `usage_record_ids` keeps the input order; importer warnings follow the Decision 12c steps (N75).
4. **Integer bounds.** Usage counts and the integer values of `context_tokens_gt`/`_le` are bounded by the I-JSON range (`<= 2**53 - 1`), like the batch 3 integers (22.13 item 3).
5. **JSON Schema `if`/`then`** is emitted for the presence rules, as batch 3 does (22.13 item 11; N12/N38 still open with staff-reviewer): `UsageQuantity` (count type by unit), `UsageRecord` (P-39), `AppliesWhenCondition` (value type by kind), `PriceEntry` (`user_supplied` needs `provenance.note`), `ReportedCostRaw` (`currency_raw` by `currency_source`), `CostRecord` (status, basis, `reported_raw` and its `currency_source` by reason, `unpriced_units`, `usage_record_ids` by reason, N67), `CostExclusion` (`duplicate_of_reported` needs `covered_by`), `CostSummary` (total and `none` by `included`). `unit_quantity` and `increment` emit the positive-decimal pattern `POSITIVE_DECIMAL_PATTERN`; `CostRounding.scale` and `.exact` emit `enum: [18]` and `enum: [false]`. **Code-only** (cannot be written in JSON Schema 2020-12 or is arithmetic): one quantity per unit, a non-negative seconds count (the test pins the count string pattern to `DECIMAL_PATTERN`), non-negative `price`, `minimum`, `amount` and `total`, `effective_to > effective_from`, distinct `applies_when` kinds, an alias equal to the own `model`, unique `entry_key` and range overlaps, every URL rule, a blank note, a blank `currency_raw`, the stored-label rules of N51 (c), `covered_by` within `included`, and `complete` versus `partial`. A combinatorial cross-check (25,344 `CostRecord` documents, plus `CostSummary`, `ReportedCostRaw`, `UsageQuantity`, `AppliesWhenCondition`, `CostExclusion`) found no document that pydantic accepts and the schema rejects; the documents the schema accepts and pydantic rejects are exactly the code-only rules above (script kept outside the repository).
6. **`CostSummary.total >= 0`** at the model (error at `total`): the total is a sum of non-negative amounts (P-38), so a negative stored total is always wrong (N81 below).
7. **`check_cost_summary`** checks, in this order: every named record is supplied [record]; every supplied record is of the summary's domain [domain]; included records are available [available]; included records share no usage [usage]; the total [total]; each exclusion (reason against `na_reason` [reason]; an available record only as `duplicate_of_reported`/`overlaps_reported` [available, duplicate, reason]; those two only on an available computed record [reported, overlap]; `duplicate_of_reported` needs its usage inside the included reported usage and names an included reported record [duplicate, reported]; `overlaps_reported` needs a partial overlap [overlap, duplicate]; `covered_by` equals, as a set, the included records that cover all of the record's usage [covered_by]); finally every supplied record is accounted for [excluded].
8. **`check_catalog_succession`** keeps the pinned parameter name `next` (it shadows the builtin inside the function only). Messages name the catalog ID, versions and entry keys, never a provenance URL.
9. **`check_price_source_url`** rejects a second `#` inside the fragment (not an RFC 3986 URI). Its steps: characters and escapes, scheme, `//` and authority, userinfo, host and port, query names, fragment names (N70), patterns in host, path, query and fragment, loopback host (N66 (b)).
10. **Contract currency.** A cited contract states USD iff `normalize_currency_label(contract.currency)` is `USD`; its code is stored as written in `reported_raw.currency_raw` (`api_contract`), and agreement with a payload label follows N73 (N80 below).

**New findings (revision 18; each with a recommendation the lead may auto-approve; the implementation follows the recommendation where it had to choose, and says so):**
- **N77. `raw` of the `unsupported_currency` warning when the payload sent an empty or blank label and a cited non-USD contract applies.** Options: (A) the label as sent (`""`, `"  "`), as D6 does for `currency_not_reported`; (B) absent, as N69 (c) does when no label was sent. Trade-off: A keeps the rule "raw is the source value verbatim" and shows that the source sent an empty field; B treats a blank label exactly like a missing one. Recommendation: A (implemented). No test asserts it. Decide before P1-29/P1-30 (the mappings that pass labels). **Decided (rev 20, auto-approved; decision log "AUTO-APPROVED api-surface 23.10 findings N77-N82"): option A**, as implemented. ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 3.
- **N78. Repeated IDs in `CostExclusion.covered_by`.** Options: (A) reject at `covered_by` (`uniqueItems`); (B) accept (current: no rule, because none was decided). Trade-off: a repeated cover adds nothing and `summarize_costs` never produces one; rejecting it is one more rule, stated in JSON Schema. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-14 (bundle checks). **Decided (rev 20, auto-approved): option A; implemented in revision 20** (below). ADR 0014 Decision 16a, I55.
- **N79. A `decimal.Context` too small for an exact cost.** Options: (A) `ValueError` naming the precision (implemented); (B) raise the precision internally and ignore the caller's; (C) let the caller's context round. Trade-off: A keeps the caller's context meaningful and never produces a silently rounded amount; B makes the argument decorative; C breaks P-35 (one rounding, half-even, recorded). Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-25 is gated. **Decided (rev 20, auto-approved): option A**, as implemented.
- **N80. The spelling of `ContractCurrency.currency`.** Options: (A) USD iff it normalizes to USD under P-52, stored as written; a blank code is a `ValueError` (implemented); (B) exact `USD` only, so a contract row written `usd` gives `unsupported_currency` with `currency_raw="usd"`, which the stored-label rule then rejects (the import would fail). Trade-off: A tolerates a lower-case table entry; B forces the field-provenance tables to write ISO codes exactly. Recommendation: A, plus a mapping-table lint (P1-35) that the code is written as an ISO code. Decide before P1-29/P1-30. **Decided (rev 20, auto-approved): option A**, as implemented, with the lint at P1-35.
- **N81. `CostSummary.total >= 0` on the model.** Options: (A) keep the rule (implemented; P-38: a total is a sum of non-negative amounts); (B) leave it to `check_cost_summary`. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-25 is gated. **Decided (rev 20, auto-approved): option A**, as implemented.
- **N82. Text that `VerbatimText` rejects (a lone surrogate) in a reported label or amount.** `ReportedCostRaw` and `IngestWarning` store them as `VerbatimText`, so `import_reported_cost` raises a `ValidationError` for such input, against "the import is never rejected" (ADR 0014 Decision 12c). Options: (A) decide with the open lone-surrogate question of P1-31 (escape the source text and record a warning); (B) accept the rejection. Recommendation: A, at P1-31. Until then such input raises. **Decided (rev 20, auto-approved; also closes the P1-31 lone-surrogate item): importers never raise on text content.** A string with a lone surrogate is unparseable (label: `unsupported_currency`; amount: `amount_unparseable`; provider language label: raw kept, treated as non-matching, at P1-31); the stored raw has each lone surrogate replaced by U+FFFD, and the warning `text_not_valid_unicode` records the replacement. This differs from the "escape" wording of option A: the replacement character, not an escape. **Implemented in revision 20** (below). ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 0, I56.

**Tests.** No test was edited. Full suite on Python 3.11.16, 3.12.14, 3.13.15 and 3.14.7 (each in its own environment synced with `uv sync --locked --all-groups`): 1088 collected, **6 failed, 1082 passed**, the same six on every version: the three gate-bound tests `test_every_entity_has_registered_prefix` (RunBundle and ExportBundle not written, P1-14), `test_value_objects_have_no_entity_fields` (SealedPayload and RedactionRecord, P1-26/P1-27) and `test_opaque_id_fields_are_exactly_the_adr_allowlist` (SealedPayload, P1-26), and the three policy-document tests that wait for `SCHEMA_POLICY.md` (P1-20a). `test_versioned_entity_set_matches_adr` turned green (PricingCatalog). Every batch 4 test passes. Notes for qa (no test is wrong): the docstring of `test_price_entry_is_a_value_object_and_prefix_pce_is_withdrawn` still describes D13 as uncorrected and leaves `validate_new_prefix("pce")` unasserted; after the correction it can assert `None`. `test_audio_seconds_decimal` and `test_every_money_amount_schema_is_string_pattern` pin the seconds count pattern to `DECIMAL_PATTERN`, which keeps ">= 0" code-only; accepting a non-negative subset pattern there would let the schema state it.

**Revision 20: N78 and N82 implemented (schema-architect, 2026-10-23).** N77 to N82 are marked Decided above (decision log "AUTO-APPROVED api-surface 23.10 findings N77-N82"); N77, N79, N80 and N81 were already implemented as recommended. qa's red tests `test_cost_summary.py::test_covered_by_repeated_ids_rejected` and `test_cost_import.py::test_import_lone_surrogate_never_raises_and_is_flagged` now pass; no test was edited by the architect.
- **N78.** `CostExclusion.covered_by` is `Annotated[list[CostRecordId], Field(json_schema_extra={"uniqueItems": True}), AfterValidator(_distinct("covered_by"))]`, the pattern `PricingSnapshot.entry_keys` already uses: a repeat is a `ValidationError` at `covered_by` (message "covered_by lists one value twice", no ID quoted), whatever the reason; inside a `CostSummary` the location is `("excluded", i, "covered_by")`. The JSON Schema states `uniqueItems: true`, so the rule is not code-only. Distinct IDs keep their order.
- **N82.** `import_reported_cost` gains a step 0 before the label step (ADR 0014 Decision 12c step 0). The type checks of `amount_raw` and `currency_raw` stay first (a non-text value is still a `TypeError`: a programming error of the mapping, not source text). Then each of the label and the amount that holds a code point in U+D800..U+DFFF is replaced by `text.replace_lone_surrogates` (new public pure function: every such code point becomes U+FFFD, one for one) and one `text_not_valid_unicode` warning is recorded for it: `raw` = the replaced text, `path` = that field's path as passed, message naming each replaced code point and its index (`U+D800 at index 2`), never the surrogate. The rest of the import runs on the replaced text: a replaced label never normalizes to USD (U+FFFD is not ASCII white space; the code also forces it), so it is `unsupported_currency` with `currency_source=payload`, after the step 2 contract comparison (a cited contract therefore also gives `currency_contract_conflict`); a replaced amount is never parsed (`amount_unparseable` when the currency is USD, unparsed otherwise). Warning order (N75 extended): the `text_not_valid_unicode` warnings first, label before amount, then the step 1 to 4 warnings.
- **Public names added:** `text.replace_lone_surrogates(value: str) -> str` (in `text.__all__`). No other public name; the private helper `cost._lone_surrogates` formats the message.
- **IngestWarning code list.** No closed list of warning codes exists: `IngestWarning.code` is an open `MachineKey` (ADR 0003 Decision 2), and no document keeps a register of codes. `text_not_valid_unicode` is documented in ADR 0014 Decision 12c steps 0 and 5, ADR 0012 Decision 7 and ADR 0017 Decision 1.
- **Not done here (P1-31):** the provider-language-label part of N82 (raw kept, treated as non-matching) belongs to the language importer.
- **Tests.** Python 3.11.16, 3.12.14, 3.13.15 and 3.14.7, each in its own environment (`UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=<env> uv sync --locked --all-groups --python X`); logs in `docs/phase1/evidence/green/batch5b-followups/`. Without the mapping tests qa is writing concurrently (untracked `test_mapping_*.py`): 1424 collected, **4 failed, 1420 passed** on every version, the four expected: `test_every_entity_has_registered_prefix` (P1-14) and the three `SCHEMA_POLICY.md` tests (P1-20a). The full directory at the time of the run: 1931 collected, 494 failed, 1437 passed; the 490 extra failures are all in `test_mapping_f1.py`, `test_mapping_f3.py`, `test_mapping_f4_f7.py`, `test_mapping_f6.py` and `test_mapping_no_fabrication.py`, 488 of them `ModuleNotFoundError: tests.support.mappings` (converters not written, P1-28+), one the converter-modules check, and one a `TypeError` inside `test_sarvam_requests_reference_the_hashed_clips` (the test reads `request.get("file", {})` and a request whose `file` is `null` gives `None`; reported to the lead for qa). Tooling: ruff, ruff format, mypy (strict) are clean on the tree except qa's six in-progress mapping files; `check_headers`, `guard_private_fixtures` and `check_licenses` (42 ok) pass.
- **Details fixed while implementing** (not pinned by tests): (1) the `text_not_valid_unicode` warnings come before every other importer warning, label before amount (the amount's flag must be recorded even when step 4 never runs, as the test requires for a non-USD or missing currency); (2) their `raw` is the replaced text, like the other warnings about the field; (3) a replaced label against a cited contract also records `currency_contract_conflict` (it disagrees after P-52, step 2), then `unsupported_currency`.

**New finding (revision 20; with a recommendation the lead may auto-approve):**
- **N83. A lone surrogate in a warning path or in a cited contract's currency.** `currency_source_path` and `amount_source_path` are stored as `IngestWarning.path` (`VerbatimText`), so a path holding a lone surrogate still makes `import_reported_cost` raise a `ValidationError`; the same holds for `ContractCurrency.currency` (stored in `reported_raw.currency_raw` for `api_contract`). Both come from the mapping, not from the reported value, but a path built from source keys (a JSON pointer over an OTLP attribute name) can carry source text. Options: (A) the importer applies `replace_lone_surrogates` to a passed path too and records nothing extra (the path is a locator, not data), while a surrogate in `contract.currency` stays a `ValueError` like a blank code (mapping configuration, linted at P1-35); (B) keep raising for both and require mappings to build paths through one shared helper that replaces and flags; (C) replace in both, with a `text_not_valid_unicode` warning without `raw`. Trade-off: A keeps "importers never raise on text derived from the source" with no new warning shape; B puts the burden on every mapping and leaves a crash path if one forgets; C adds a warning whose field is the path itself, which no reader expects. Recommendation: A. Decide before P1-28 (the first mapping that passes paths).

### 23.11 Revision 21: M3 and A1 to A3 implemented (schema-architect, 2026-10-23)
- **M3 (decision log "AUTO-APPROVED mapping ambiguities").** `UsageRecord.model: MachineKey | None = None`. `None` (key absent or `null`) means the source reported no model; a source placeholder such as Pipecat's `"unknown"` is never stored (the mapping leaves `model` absent and keeps the quantities, character counts included). A given model is still a `MachineKey`; `""`, `"bad key"`, a number, a list or an object is rejected at `model`. ADR 0014 Decision 8.
- **A2.** `resolve_price(catalog, *, provider: str, model: str | None, unit: str, occurred_at: datetime | None = None, run_started_at: datetime | None = None, facts: PriceFacts = PriceFacts()) -> PriceResolution`. `model=None` returns `PriceResolution(None, "no_price_for_model")` after `facts` and the two instants are checked (a malformed argument is still a `TypeError`/`ValueError`), before candidates, date and conditions. `compute_cost` passes `usage.model` unchanged and has no rule of its own. ADR 0014 Decision 15.
- **A1.** A cost whose usage records all lack a model is `not_available`/`no_price_for_model`; one that mixes them with priced usage is `not_available`/`unit_unpriced` with the units listed in `unpriced_units` (the existing partial rule, ADR 0014 Decision 13a reason 4), never an amount. Revisit at the gate.
- **A3.** JSON Schema: `model` is not in `required` and its property is pydantic's `anyOf` of the machine-key string and `null` (the shape is not pinned by tests).
- **Version and migration.** No `schema_version` change: 0.0.1 is unreleased and every 0.0.x file needs an upgrade step anyway (ADR 0008). Widening a required field to optional is additive for writers; no DB table derives from `UsageRecord` yet, so no Alembic migration.
- **Tests.** `test_usage.py::test_usage_record_model_optional_not_reported`, `test_usage.py::test_usage_record_shape_and_required_fields` (changed by qa: `model` left the required set), `test_cost_compute.py::test_usage_without_model_is_not_available_never_zero`, `test_cost_compute.py::test_mixed_model_and_modelless_usage_is_never_a_partial_sum`. No test was edited by the architect.

## 24. Batch 5 (task P1-26): sealed payloads, `KeyRef`, `RedactionRecord`; API assumed by the tests

Owner: qa (sections 24.1 to 24.7, written 2026-10-23 against SCOPE revision 17 as `docs/phase1/api-surface-sealing.md` and merged here in revision 19; that file now only points here) and schema-architect (the revision 19 decisions and implementation notes, marked "(rev 19)", and section 24.8). Tests: `packages/evalschema/tests/test_sealed_payload.py` (257 collected), `packages/evalschema/tests/test_sealing_adversarial.py` (70 collected), helper `packages/evalschema/tests/_support_b5.py` (the test-only reference seal/unseal of ADR 0015 Decision 8, plus document builders). If a name or field below is wrong for a good reason, escalate to staff-reviewer; do not change a test.

**Numbering.** qa numbered the readings of this batch N77 to N90, which collide with the cost findings N77 to N82 of section 23.10. They are **S-N77 to S-N90** everywhere (decision log, ADRs, SCOPE); "N77" alone keeps meaning the cost finding.

Binding inputs: ADR 0015 (all decisions, P-42, P-18), ADR 0006 Decisions 3 to 5 (keyed digests, `key_ref`, the digest domain), ADR 0007 Decisions 3, 4 and 8 (no secret-bearing fields, KeyRef without key material, no-echo), ADR 0016 Decision 7 and I10 (`RedactionRecord`), ADR 0001 Decision 8 and I12 (value objects, opaque `key_id`, typed `subject_id`), the conventions of sections 0, 19, 22 and 23 (`extra="forbid"`, closed sets emit `enum` and never `const`, rules across fields reported at the field they are about, value objects frozen with no entity fields, hiding models, ASCII-only tests).

### 24.1 Modules, classes and pinned fields

Classes are found by name through `registry.model_classes()`; the tests do not import a module path. (rev 19) Homes: `tracer_evalschema.sealed` for `SealedPayload`, its two nested models `DekWrap` and `SealedAadFields`, `PAYLOAD_TYPES` and `aad_bytes`; `tracer_evalschema.privacy` for `RedactionRecord`. `KeyRef` and `ContentDigest` stay in `tracer_evalschema.digest` and are not re-exported by `sealed` (one home per class keeps the registry walk and the schema export unambiguous). Every class below is a value object: `extra="forbid"`, `frozen=True`, none of `id`, `schema_version`, `created_at`, `metadata`, `content_digest`, `version` of its own (the one allowed `schema_version` is inside the aad model, ADR 0001 Decision 8).

| Name | Kind | Pinned contract |
|---|---|---|
| `SealedPayload` | value object, hides its input (S-N88) | Exactly seven fields: `format`, `alg`, `dek_wrap`, `nonce`, `ciphertext`, `aad_fields`, `plaintext_digest`. All required except `plaintext_digest` (default `None`; an absent member and an explicit `null` are the same). `format` is the closed set `{"tracer.sealed.v1"}` and `alg` the closed set `{"A256GCM"}`; each emits an `enum` of one member, never `const`. Any other string, including case, white-space and zero-width variants, is a `ValidationError` located at the field (readers reject unknown `format` or `alg`, ADR 0015 Consequences). `dek_wrap` and `aad_fields` are single nested models (not unions, not optional): `test_ids` walks `SealedPayload.model_fields["aad_fields"].annotation` for exactly one nested model |
| `DekWrap` (rev 19 name; not pinned by tests) | value object, hides its input | Exactly `alg` (closed set `{"A256KW"}`, an `enum`), `kek_ref: KeyRef`, `wrapped_dek`. All required |
| `SealedAadFields` (rev 19 name; not pinned by tests) | value object, hides its input | Exactly `payload_type`, `subject_id`, `subject_version`, `schema_version`; `subject_version` is the only optional field (default `None`, `null` equals absent). `payload_type` is the closed set of 24.1.1 (S-N80); `subject_id` is a typed ID of the kind that the payload type seals (union of `TestProfileId`, `ArtifactId`, `TranscriptId`, `ToolCallEventId`); `subject_version` is a strict integer >= 1; `schema_version` is the form `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` without leading zeros, suffix or white space and is not compared with the current version (S-N79) |
| `RedactionRecord` | value object | Exactly `method`, `tool_ref`, `performed_at`, `covers`, all required. `method` and `tool_ref`: non-empty `VerbatimText` (S-N86). `performed_at: UtcDatetime`. `covers`: non-empty list of distinct members of the closed set `{audio, text, tool_arguments, tool_result}` (S-N87, rev 19: `tool_result` is a member); the JSON Schema states it as an `enum` with `uniqueItems`. The bundle rule that demands a seal or a redaction is P1-14 |
| `KeyRef` | value object (exists, `digest.py`) | Unchanged: fields a subset of `{key_id, key_version, provider_hint}`, `key_id` required; every credential-like or key-material member name is an unknown field. No value-level credential rule (S-N83, a known gap, 24.4) |

#### 24.1.1 Payload types, subject kinds and versions (S-N80, S-N84)

| `payload_type` | `subject_id` kind | `subject_version` |
|---|---|---|
| `test_profile` | `TestProfileId` (`tpf`) | required, integer >= 1 (TestProfile is a versioned entity) |
| `audio` | `ArtifactId` (`art`) | absent (Artifact is not versioned) |
| `transcript_text` | `TranscriptId` (`tsc`) | absent |
| `tool_arguments` | `ToolCallEventId` (`tcl`) | absent |
| `tool_result` | `ToolCallEventId` (`tcl`) | absent |

A `subject_id` of another kind (every other registered prefix is tried), a bare UUID, a v4 UUID, an upper-case or foreign string, `null` or a number is a `ValidationError` at `subject_id`; a missing, `null`-for-required, zero, negative, float, string or boolean `subject_version` on a profile, and any `subject_version` on the other types, is a `ValidationError` at `subject_version`. The error text names the field and the rule, never the id. (rev 19) The table is `sealed.PAYLOAD_SUBJECT_TYPES` (read-only), `sealed.VERSIONED_PAYLOAD_TYPES == ("test_profile",)`.

#### 24.1.2 Encoding rules (ADR 0015 Decision 5, I3, I4)

- All binary fields are base64url without padding, canonical: alphabet `A-Z a-z 0-9 - _` only (no `+`, `/`, `=`, white space, line breaks, NUL, non-ASCII), length not 1 modulo 4, and the unused trailing bits of the last character are zero (the second spelling of one byte string is rejected). Each variant is a `ValidationError` at its field.
- `nonce`: exactly 12 bytes (16 characters). The JSON Schema states the length.
- `dek_wrap.wrapped_dek`: exactly 40 bytes (54 characters, last character in `AQgw`), the RFC 3394 size of a wrapped 256-bit key (S-N81). The JSON Schema states the length.
- `ciphertext`: at least 16 bytes, because the 16-byte GCM tag is appended (S-N82); exactly 16 bytes is an empty plaintext and is valid. The JSON Schema states the alphabet (no padding, no white space); qa's contract leaves the trailing-bit rule, the impossible length and a trailing newline code-only. (rev 19) The implementation's JSON Schema also states the trailing-bit rule and the possible lengths, as the pattern `^(?:[A-Za-z0-9_-]{4})*(?:[A-Za-z0-9_-][AQgw]|[A-Za-z0-9_-]{2}[AEIMQUYcgkosw048])?$` with `minLength: 22` (16 bytes), so a verifier in another language gets the canonical form from the schema alone; only the trailing newline stays code-only for Python's `re.search` (S-N90).

#### 24.1.3 The digest rule (ADR 0015 Decision 6, I11, I11a, P-42, A-32)

`plaintext_digest` is absent, `null`, or a `ContentDigest` of scheme `hmac-sha256-jcs/v1` with its `key_ref`, for every one of the five payload types. A plain `sha256-jcs/v1` digest is a `ValidationError` located at `plaintext_digest`, whether given as a document, as a validated `ContentDigest` instance or as the result of `compute_digest()`; the message names the rule and neither the digest value nor the scheme. An unregistered scheme raises `UnknownDigestScheme` unwrapped (ADR 0006 Decision 3), never "unverifiable". The key named by the digest's `key_ref` must not be the key-encryption key: a `key_ref` equal to `dek_wrap.kek_ref` (same `key_id` and `key_version`) is rejected at `plaintext_digest` (S-N85). (rev 19) The error of the key-separation rule is located at `("plaintext_digest", "key_ref")`. The JSON Schema states "absent, `null` or a keyed scheme with `key_ref`"; key separation compares two objects and is code-only.

#### 24.1.4 AAD and authentication (ADR 0015 Decision 4, I7 to I10)

`AAD = JCS(aad_fields)` of the JSON-mode document with every `null` member removed (S-N78); RFC 8785 key order, no white space, non-ASCII not escaped. One fresh 256-bit DEK and one fresh 12-byte nonce per payload; the DEK is wrapped with AES Key Wrap (RFC 3394) under the KEK; the ciphertext is AES-256-GCM output with the 16-byte tag appended. A changed ciphertext, nonce, wrapped DEK, `subject_id`, `subject_version`, `payload_type` or `schema_version`, or another KEK, fails authentication. The schema package ships none of this: the reference implementation is test-only (`_support_b5.seal`, `unseal`, `dek_of`), and the golden vectors (G1 profile with a keyed digest, G2 audio) were computed outside the package. (rev 19, S-N78) The package ships the one definition of the AAD bytes: `sealed.aad_bytes(aad_fields: SealedAadFields | Mapping[str, Any]) -> bytes` (pure; a mapping is validated as `SealedAadFields` first, then dumped in JSON mode without null members and canonicalised with `digest.jcs_bytes`).

#### 24.1.5 The plaintext and its digest (S-N77)

The plaintext is `bytes`. When `plaintext_digest` is present, the plaintext is the UTF-8 text of a JSON object and the digest is over that object through `compute_digest`/`verify_digest` (the digest domain of ADR 0006 Decision 5: top-level `schema_version` and `content_digest` removed, nulls removed at every depth), checked after authentication. For a sealed `test_profile` it is therefore the keyed digest of the profile document, the link to the referenced profile. Audio and other non-JSON plaintext can only be sealed with the digest absent.
